Full battery GREEN, **32/32 stages** (3720s) — the 32nd is a new lane this unit owed. Awaiting the lead's validation + patch (SL-318.p7).\n\n- `04ec6faf` the statement-scoped window, the borrowing struct, and the two missing splits\n- `c420e372` the conformance rows K132-K149, and the two release paths the fixtures found\n- `1140a99e` docs for the borrowing struct, the `for` window and the two splits\n- `f2927e67` the four battery lanes the unit owed\n- `0230727d` the `windowseam` lane — the reuse obligation, gated\n\nGate counts: suite **2615 passed / 14 xfailed**; freestanding **36/36** (riscv32 + arm64); battery **32/32**. Conformance subset **354 passed / 2 xfailed**; SL-333's pins **37 passed**, unchanged. No existing `examples/conformance/K*.saw` file was edited — only `INDEX.md`.\n\n---\n\n## 1. The window record and its chokepoint\n\n`sawc/windows.py`. ONE record, `StatementWindow`: root path, root binding id, mode, origin, the EXTENT statement, the client, and a GENERIC resource slot (`resource_name` / `resource_type`) — never a field named `__iter`, never an `Iterator`-shaped payload. ONE chokepoint, `WindowTable`, whose docstring names its clients.\n\n**Clients** — `for` is the only one in U3 (`typechecker/borrowing.py::open_for_window` / `close_for_window`, called from `statements.py`'s two `for` checkers); the reserved second is the generic scoped-borrow statement, filed as SL-342.\n\n**Entries the chokepoint owns**, so no client does them twice:\n- the ROOT CHARGE, read at the four access sites design 189's task-capture extent already had — a name read (`expressions._check_identifier`), a `move` (`_check_move_expr`), a CALL's access set (`types._check_call_exclusivity`, which is where `v.push`, a `&var` argument, an `o.take()` receiver, a closure's borrow captures and a design-201 spawn argument all arrive) and a WRITE target (`statements._check_write_target`);\n- the EXCLUSIVITY conflicts, including a `&var` extent live at the head or STARTED in the body;\n- the MOVE / REASSIGN / TAKE / SWAP refusals on the root and on every enclosing owner;\n- the CLOSE-ON-EVERY-ROUTE accounting by RESOLVED CONTROL-FLOW TARGET. `windows_left_by(target_extents)` takes the extents the edge's target is still inside and answers inside-out — so `continue` targeting THIS loop keeps its window, `break` closes it, a jump targeting an INNER loop closes nothing of the outer, a window nested in an outer loop closes when control continues that outer loop, and `return`/error/cancellation pass the empty set. There is no `Continue` special case.\n\n**The record is a plain class, not a dataclass**, and the docstring says why: `typechecker/effects.py:substitute_ast_types` walks `dataclasses.fields()` and recurses into every dataclass VALUE, so a record whose `extent` points back at the statement holding it is a cycle the monomorphization walk descends until the recursion limit. Found by RecursionError on the first generic-collection fixture.\n\n**E8**, a named post-transform exemption beside design 218a §6's four: the window rule is about the AUTHORED program and ran on the authored tree one pass earlier. In a TRANSFORMED body `self` IS the frame — the collection has become `self.<field>` and every resume hand-off passes `&var self` — so the whole frame overlaps the window's root by the ordinary prefix rule. It covers the FENCE in both halves for the same reason: the transform's own lowering IS exception e2 (the resource becomes a `Slot<It>` frame field in a driven body, a `let` in a sync one), and judging the compiler's tree by the rule would be judging the answer by the question.\n\n---\n\n## 2. The borrowing struct\n\n`sawc/typechecker/borrowing.py`. Four spellings, each enforced:\n\n| spelling | rule | refusal row |\n|---|---|---|\n| `borrows struct` | design 130's `unsafe struct` position; no name convention | `_error_borrows_prefix` for every other position |\n| `private v: &Vector<T, A>` | the ordinary reference spelling; the ONE exception to DF-163d, TOP-LEVEL and SHARED only | K145 (`&var` field), K143 (nested reference) |\n| `borrows -> S` | a VALUE-returning producer | K144 (missing `borrows`) |\n| `lends self` | the ONLY origin, `&self` receiver required | K144 (projection, foreign parameter, `&var self`) |\n\n`lends` is a **CONTEXTUAL keyword** (the `import`/`module`/`export` precedent), so neither lexer's table changes: `lexdiff`, `astdiff` and `selfhostlex` are untouched by the new word. `borrows struct` reuses the existing `BORROWS` token.\n\n**The lend/producer split is answerable before type checking.** `place_transform` runs ahead of the checker and cannot look up whether a return type names a `borrows struct` (it may be imported), so it splits on two facts it does have: the DECLARED return type is not a reference (SL-333 R1 made `-> &T` mean \"lend\" whatever the body does) and the body contains no `lend`. A `borrows -> T` body that DOES lend still gets R1's fixit; a `borrows -> &T` body with no `lend` still gets design 141's coverage error. The producer then gets `place_self_by_pointer` — the SAME bit DF-146b set for an accessor, not a second ABI — because a copy's address dies with the call.\n\n**R5's FIELD clause** was added to the sentence SL-333 wrote, in `parser/types.py:reject_reference_field` and in the spec, rather than a second sentence beside it.\n\n**The fence: one question at one line.** `check_borrowing_fence` is called from `_check_expression` where `resolved_type` is stamped — the one line every expression's type passes through — so the position list is a matrix of TESTS, not of checks. Four exceptions, each documented: e1 the producer's own construction and return; e2 the compiler's hidden binding (E8); e3 the struct's own extension methods' receiver; e4 the reference field, which AUTO-DEREFERENCES at member access exactly as a reference parameter does — which is what confines it to a non-escaping re-borrow with no second rule, since references are not first-class.\n\n**The rejector's rows.** Three phrases, and that is deliberate: the fence has no parent slot in hand, so the sentence that matters is the RULE. Two positions a CLIENT knows get their own diagnosis because the ACT differs.\n\n| row | position | message |\n|---|---|---|\n| K142.1-9 | `let`, `var`, argument, discarded statement, tuple element, collection-literal element, assignment RHS, closure return, `??` operand | ``may not be used outside a window head`` |\n| K142.10 | TEMPORARY receiver | ``may not be produced from a TEMPORARY receiver`` + \"bind the collection first\" |\n| K142.11 | head is an `if`/`match`/block/`try` EXPRESSION | ``may not be produced by an `if` / `match` / block / `try` expression in the head`` |\n| K143 | parameter, field, plain return, type argument, static, associated `Item` | ``may not name a borrowing struct`` — at the DECLARATION |\n| K144 | `lends self.items`, `lends other`, `&var self` receiver, a field not initialized with `lends self`, a `borrows struct` with no reference field | one message each, each naming the rule |\n| K145 | `&var T` field | ``may not be an EXCLUSIVE reference`` + the reallocation argument |\n\nA by-value receiver is NOT a row: the parser already refuses `self` without a sigil, so the origin rule's own refusal for it is unreachable from source.\n\n**std.** `VectorIterator<T, A>` and `EnumeratedIterator<T, A>` are the four-line shape holding `&Vector<T, A>`; `next()` reads `length` and `buffer` through the reference on every call and the only `unsafe` left is the index through the buffer. **`public` KEPT** on both, and the comment now says why: design 80's signature-visibility rule (a public `iter()` may not name a private type), NOT so a caller can hold one — holding is refused. The ALLOCATOR type parameter is new on both (the old shape held a bare `UnsafePointer<T>` and needed none, which is also why nothing connected the iterator to the vector).\n\n---\n\n## 3. The four matrix axes, per cell\n\n**ITERATOR × SUSPENSION POSITION × CONTEXT** — `K133_window_spans_a_suspension.saw`, 14 cells, each against its sync twin:\n\n| iterator | driven | spawned | embedded in spawned | nested in while | nested in for |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `Vector.iter` | 12 ok | 12 ok | 24 ok | 24 ok | 36 ok |\n| `Vector.enumerated` | 15 ok | — | — | — | — |\n| a USER borrowing iterator | 12 ok | — | — | — | — |\n| a RECORD-LESS user iterator (owns its source, opens NO window) | 12 ok | — | — | — | — |\n| `Map.keys` (owned copy — the control) | 6 ok | — | — | — | — |\n\nThe HEAD axis is K138's, where the ACQUIRE sequence is what it measures.\n\n**ROOT** — `K135`, all ok at 12: frame-local, `&` param, `&var` param of a driven callee, an ENCLOSING FRAME's field (`self.inner`), a field of a `&var self` receiver, a GENERIC collection instantiation (`Wrapper<Int>`). Every one is a persistent slot by the direct-head rule.\n\n**CONFLICT** — refusals in `errors/window_conflicts.saw` (one compile, 8 diagnostics): the body pushes; `with_var_ref` on the root; a `&var` extent STARTED in the body (a design-201 spawn argument); `move` of the root; reassignment of the root; `swap` of two elements; a write of the window's own root path through its owner (`st.patches = []`). Composing cells in `K135`: an ordinary read of the root, a DISJOINT sibling of the root path (`st.count` beside a window on `st.patches`), a second `for` over the same root nested inside the first, a `&` extent live at the head, a `&var` extent on a disjoint root — all accepted. **A disjoint sibling composing is the cell that proves the root is a PATH and not a name** — my first draft wrote it as a refusal and the compiler was right.\n\n**RELEASE** — `K136`, each route proving the root is writable at the first reachable point after the exit:\n\n| route | sync | driven |\n|---|---|---|\n| exhaustion | 6, len 4 | 6, len 4 |\n| `break` plain | 1, len 4 | 1, len 4 |\n| `break <value>` | 3, len 4 | (see bound below) |\n| `continue` targeting THIS loop — NOT an exit | 4, len 4 | 4, len 4 |\n| `return` from the body — proof in the CALLER | 1, len 4 | |\n| an error PROPAGATED by `try` — proof after the `match` | 2, len 4 | |\n| an error CAUGHT that CONTINUES the loop — window stays live | 5, len 4 | |\n| exit under cancellation | K140 | |\n| the EMPTY loop (head evaluates, zero iterations, one drop) | 0, len 1 | |\n| BREAK-THEN-MUTATE | 1, len 5 | |\n\nB2's target cells: a `break`/`continue` targeting an INNER loop closes nothing of the outer (12); a window NESTED in an outer loop closes when control continues that outer loop (12).\n\nONE BOUND, and it is NOT U3's: a VALUE-position `for` whose body suspends is refused, **identically for a RANGE `for` on main** (probed) — design 127 charges every loop back-edge in a driven body and a suspension-spanning loop does not support `break <value>`. Recorded in the file.\n\n**FENCE** — every refusal position with a USER-DEFINED borrowing struct over a USER collection (K142-K145 above).\n\n---\n\n## 4. The ACQUIRE (B3) and B2 cells\n\n`K138_window_acquire_sequence.saw` + `errors/window_acquire_mutating_argument.saw`:\n\n| cell | outcome |\n|---|---|\n| A1/A3 ORDER — receiver resolved, then arguments left to right | prints `a`, `b`, then 9; sync and driven agree |\n| A2 a MUTATING argument (`b.iter_from(grow(&var b))`) | REFUSED — ``is borrowed by a nested call in this argument list``. This is design 199's own rule, which is what makes A2 free |\n| A2 a SUSPENDING argument | ACCEPTED, 9 = the sync twin's |\n| A3 a FAILING argument | no charge, no iterator constructed, no drop, root writable in the caller |\n| A5 hand-off | the statement charge opens with no gap (K134's refusals are its evidence) |\n| B4 an OWNED iterator from a SUSPENDING head | evaluated ONCE, 12 — which is what separates it from the temporary-receiver refusal |\n\n---\n\n## 5. Destruction and cancellation, with counts\n\n`K137_window_destruction_order.saw` — a user borrowing iterator holding a COUNTED `Tag` beside a COUNTED `Bag`; the iterator's `deinit` READS THROUGH THE REFERENCE (`self.bag.items.len()`), which is what makes the order observable:\n\n| cell | output |\n|---|---|\n| sync | `iterator deinit, owner still has 3` then `owner deinit` |\n| driven | same |\n| `break` on the first element | same |\n\n`K140_window_under_cancellation.saw` — each cell counts deinits:\n\n| cell | iterator deinits | owner deinits |\n|---|---|---|\n| cancel while PARKED IN A NESTED CALLEE | 1 | 1 |\n| a caught cancellation that CONTINUES the loop (window stays live, 3 items seen) | 1 | 1 |\n| a cancellation PROPAGATED out of the HEAD's own evaluation (B3's A3) | **0** | 1 |\n| cancel at the park, then exhaustion | 1 | 1 |\n\nThe zero-construction cell is a FAILING HEAD and not a pre-head cancel, because a cancel is a REQUEST and does not stop a head from running — which is the contract's own wording.\n\n**`Mutex` pair** — the refusal half (`errors/mutex_guarded_for_suspends.saw`) passes. The ACCEPT half is XFAIL on **SL-340**, a pre-existing use-after-free this row is the first program in the tree to reach (see §7).\n\n**`threads: 2`** — `errors/window_in_a_threaded_group.saw`: REFUSED on the Send-on-frames rule, as ruled.\n\n---\n\n## 6. SL-215 rows and the XFAIL flips\n\n`K147_inline_catch_splits.saw`, all against sync twins:\n\n| row | sync | driven |\n|---|---|---|\n| a SUSPENDING call in the catch (guard-shaped, the catch diverges) | 0 | 0 |\n| the ERROR BINDING across the suspension | `stop` | `stop` |\n| the three `try` VARIANTS in one body (`try!`, `try?`, `try … catch`) | 12 | 12 |\n| a ROUTED `try(as Wrapped.Inner)` beside an inline catch | — | 7 |\n| a catch that `break`s its loop | 1 | 1 |\n| a catch that `continue`s its loop | 6 | 6 |\n\nDF-196b's multi-error refusal: `errors/inline_catch_multi_error.saw`, inherited whole.\n\n**`coro_suspending_call_in_inline_catch.saw` (SL-215) — MARKER REMOVED**, flipped in the commit that fixed it, header rewritten to state the rule.\n\n**`coro_value_inline_catch_hosts_a_move.saw` (SL-231) — STAYS XFAIL, and it is a different mechanism**, which the file's own header already named: the inline catch is a `TryExpr`, and `_is_value_conditional` does not list it, so no sink lowering runs and its catch TAIL stays a genuine value tail with nowhere to put the drop-flag clear. My normalization does not fire on it — its catch neither spans a suspension nor carries a `break`/`continue` — so the shape reaches `_lower_inplace` exactly as before and the refusal is byte-identical. Listing `TryExpr` in `_is_value_conditional` is still the shape of SL-231's fix.\n\n**The same slot's SILENT face**, found while building this and fixed by the same rewrite: `_mark_ob_block`'s clause-2 marking lists `IfExpr`, `MatchExpr` and `TryCatchExpr` and not `TryExpr`, so a `try f() catch { break }` inside a spanning loop kept a RAW `break` that left the resume method's `while true` DISPATCH loop — the break was LOST and a `continue` spun FOREVER, at exit 0. Measured on main.\n\nEach rewrite takes a FRESH error binding (`__saw_caughtN`, author's `error` reads renamed). The block form's caught error travels in a frame field NAMED by the binding and the default is `error` for every one, so two normalized catches in one body shared a field: ``argument `value` expects `Alpha` but got `Beta``. sawtracker's accept loop has exactly two (`ChannelError` and `IoError`), which is where it showed up.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Findings filed — four, none of them U3's, each probed identical on main\n\n**SL-338** — a `move` in the HEAD of a suspension-SPANNING `if`/`while` is REFUSED. `_split_if`/`_split_while` refuse the drop-flag clears because the condition is evaluated inside `_branch(...)`; SL-234's `_hoist_head_and_relinquish` solved exactly this for the NON-split path and SL-226 r1 fixed the `while` placement, and neither reaches the split path. Reachable from a collection `for` now that its back-edge is charged. Pins: `coro_move_in_split_if_head.saw`, `coro_move_in_split_while_head.saw`, and `coro_while_head_move_reevaluates_per_iteration.saw` XFAILed WHOLE — every driven cell in that file stood on the op-budget exemption, and its own header said so (\"this is the shape in which a NON-spanning moving `while` exists in a driven body at all\").\n\n**SL-339** — DOUBLE FREE: a `move` of a frame local in the BODY of a suspension-SPANNING `try { } catch { }` is not relinquished on the error edge. `_lower_inplace` emits the clear as a trailing statement and `_emit_try_landing` wraps the whole sequence, so the error edge leaves before it. Silent, exit 0, `deinit` twice. Measured on main with the BLOCK spelling. Pin: `coro_move_in_split_try_body.saw`.\n\n**SL-340** — USE-AFTER-FREE: `Mutex(value: move v)` at a heap-owning payload FREES the payload at construction. An `init`'s by-value parameter is registered for scope-exit cleanup and the transfer into `UnsafeMutableInterior(value)` does not retire it, so the buffer is released at the end of the init body and the struct is returned around it. Every `Mutex` in the tree before K141 guarded a trivial payload, which is why the corpus passes. Pin: `K141_mutex_guarded_for_is_sync.saw` (XFAIL).\n\n**SL-341** — sawtracker's read-only build dies in the LLVM machine scheduler (`cannot decrease max pressure`, `RegisterPressure.cpp:1350`) under the default pipeline, identically before and after this branch. `-O0 -c` COMPILES CLEAN on both. The last thing between design 275's epic acceptance criterion and its first half.\n\n**On xfailing breakage the unit's ruled change makes reachable.** SL-338's and SL-339's shapes were refused/miscompiled on main already, at the range `for` — the collection `for` was shielded only by the op-budget skip the brief ruled retired. The pins are minimal repros with EXPECT directives stating the intended behaviour, so each XPASS flip validates the fix. **Flagged for the lead's judgment**, since \"a brief never xfails breakage IT introduced\" is arguable here.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Follow-ups filed\n\n- **SL-342** — the generic statement-scoped borrow window (keyword block binding a lent place, `else` arm for a `borrows -> &T?` head). Cites the reuse obligation and lists what is already built for it.\n- **SL-343** — `with_ref`/`with_var_ref` bodies and accessor `lend` windows across a suspension. Cites the Fences paragraph; names which of the three legs of U3's argument each spelling has, and owes the second consumer sweep.\n- **SL-344** — the exclusive-window derivation (`&var T` fields, writer-versus-reader, nested windows). Cites part 0; notes `windows.py` already reserves the MODES.\n\n---\n\n## 9. \"What the generic window statement would add\" (the owed paragraph)\n\nIts **syntax** is a keyword-introduced block naming a head (a `borrows` call on a place), a binding, the mode the binding is opened at, and an `else` arm for a conditional lend. Its **typechecking client** is one adapter beside `open_for_window` / `close_for_window`: it resolves the receiver place, calls `WindowTable.open_window` with its own root, mode, origin and extent, and calls `close_window` at the statement's end — reading nothing of its own shape back. Its **acquisition/result binding** is B3's ACQUIRE sequence unchanged (A1-A5 are about a CALL EXPRESSION, not about a loop), plus the one thing new: the statement BINDS the lent place under a name, where `for` binds the loop variable instead.\n\nAnd nothing else, because none of the four is duplicated:\n- **ROOT ACCOUNTING** is `WindowTable.conflict_for` and the four access sites that read it; they take a PATH and a writes-flag and have never seen a `ForLoop`.\n- **PINNING** is the direct-head rule (`borrowing_head_receiver` + `borrowing_head_root`, the latter being design 141's `_build_access_path` and not a root walk of its own) plus (ii)'s refusals, which are rows of the same conflict table.\n- **SUSPENSION PERSISTENCE** is the record on the statement plus the resource's ordinary frame residency. The `for` client gets it by being rewritten into a `while let`, so the iterator becomes a frame local the way any local does; a generic window's binding is the same question with a different resource, and `coro_transform.py` reads `stmt.window`, never the loop.\n- **EXIT CLEANUP** is `_open_window_resource` / `_close_window_resource`, which take the RESOURCE'S TYPE and its slot — nothing client-shaped — and are pushed around the statement BEFORE the `loop_stack` entry records the unwind depth, so `break` unwinds only the scopes inside and `return` sweeps through `_cleanup_all_scopes`.\n\nThat is a claim about where knowledge lives, so it is a LANE, not a grep: **`tools/test_window_seam.py` (`windowseam`)**, five checks over SOURCE — the chokepoint's CODE names no client vocabulary (prose may), the record still declares the generic resource slot, the client list resolves, the codegen lowering names no `ForLoop`/`.variable`/`.element_type`/`.iterable`/`loop_stack`/`RangeExpr`, and the conflict/fence half names no loop. Verified by INJECTION: three violations each caught and restored, with an uninjected control either side.\n\n**The lead's grep, run:** `sawc/windows.py` mentions `ForLoop` in eleven lines, all prose; zero in code. `typechecker/borrowing.py` no longer imports `ForLoop` at all — the adapter needs only `MethodCall` for the direct-head test.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Consumer census (obligation 2), before and after\n\nRe-run on the branch over examples, blade, libs, devtools, sawc/std, selfhost, tests and the read-only sawtracker sources: **221 `for` heads over `iter()`/`enumerated()`** (examples 159, blade 7, libs 2, devtools 6, sawc/std 8, sawtracker 39).\n\n**Exactly ONE is not a direct call on a named place**: `devtools/dogfood/programs/w1_filesearch.saw:56`, `for line in text.split(\"\\n\").iter()` — a temporary receiver. It does not compile on main either (35 errors, byte-identical count on both), being a dogfood record of a naive-implementer session; `lexdiff`/`astdiff` parse it and nothing typechecks it. **No census site changes outcome.**\n\nData's iterator, the one held outside a `for` head: `examples/data_iter_outlives_source.saw` passes — it owns its (copy-on-write) source and is not a borrowing struct.\n\n`bootstrap` (Blade + libs) is GREEN with **zero edits** to `blade/` or `libs/*/tests`.\n\n**Corpus sites that DID change outcome** — three, each a decision and not a workaround:\n1. `coro_head_move_with_early_exit_drops_once.saw` — four heads over a TEMPORARY receiver (`consume_vec(move r).iter()`). The cell's subject is the `move` IN the head; it keeps it through an OWNED iterator (`String.bytes()`), which the fence does not restrict, and a comment says why.\n2. `taskgroup_send_containers.saw` — `sum_vector` indexes instead of iterating. **This is user-visible: `for x in v.iter()` in a multi-threaded task body is now a compile error**, because the charged back-edge makes the loop span and a borrowing iterator is not `Send`. K146 records it as a row rather than leaving it to be discovered.\n3. `coro_transform_error_anchors_in_defining_module.saw` — its vehicle was the SL-317 refusal, which is now a split; it rides the closure-body refusal (SL-316), the shape table's other REFUSE row, raised through the same `_FrameBuilder._error`.\n\n**sawtracker's stop point, before and after.** BEFORE (main `da84578e`) and AFTER (this branch) are the SAME: an LLVM machine-scheduler assertion under the default pipeline (SL-341). At `-O0 -c` BOTH compile clean, which is what says the whole front end — typecheck, place lowering, the coroutine transform with SL-215's inline catches and SL-317's collection `for`s — is satisfied. (The brief expected a stop at SL-329's `Thread.spawn` refusal; that is not where either compiler stops today.) `store.saw:285`'s loop shape is `K149_sawtracker_loop_shape.saw` — a collection `for` whose body suspends AND propagates an error AND writes a different root — running, sync twin agreeing.\n\n---\n\n## 11. Ledger dump + IR compare, classified\n\nDesign 275 U1's own instrument, over **1778 `examples/*.saw`**, branch vs main:\n\n**FRAME LEDGER** (`--emit-frame-ledger`, deterministic and path-free): **1774 byte-identical** — 1369 of them non-empty, carrying 1945 FRAME rows and 1632 SITE rows. **ZERO content differences.** The four that differ are the four outcome flips: `coro_suspending_call_in_inline_catch.saw` (main refuses, branch compiles — SL-215) and the three SL-338 pins (main compiles, branch refuses).\n\n**OPTIMIZED IR** (paths normalized): **1721 byte-identical**; the same 4 flips; **53 differ**, and every one is `sawc/std/vector.saw`:\n- 37 name a Vector/Set/String/Data iterator symbol in their IR;\n- 16 do not, and are the same cause one hop away — closure symbol names carry their SOURCE LINE (`__closure$…__greater_at$715_24` -> `$723_24`), `DILocation` lines in `vector.saw`, and LLVM's type-uniquing counters (`%StringBuilder.8392`) moving with the iterators' new type arguments.\n\nThe change in each is the iterator's new mangled name (`VectorIterator$1$Int` -> `VectorIterator$2$Int$GlobalAllocator`), its new field layout (a pointer to the vector instead of a buffer pointer plus a length snapshot, which moves the SROA slot indices), and vector.saw's shifted line numbers. **The emitted loops are the same instructions** — the optimizer still folds the iterator away and indexes the buffer directly.\n\nZero examples' IR gained an op-budget yield, which is the expected answer: `_instrument_loop_backedges` runs on the DRIVEN closure only, and the corpus's collection `for`s are overwhelmingly in sync bodies.\n\n---\n\n## 12. Two compiler fixes the fixtures earned\n\n1. **codegen's SYNC lowering never destroyed the window's resource.** `_open_window_resource` / `_close_window_resource` (`codegen/loops.py`) are one routine for any client, pushed around the loop and BEFORE the `loop_stack` entry that records the unwind depth — so `break` unwinds only the scopes inside it, `return` sweeps the resource through `_cleanup_all_scopes`, and the close at the end block runs exactly once, BEFORE the charge ends. Invisible before U3: every pre-existing iterator was pointer-plus-Ints and owed no cleanup, which is exactly why K137's fixture holds a counted resource.\n2. **the fresh error binding** per normalized inline catch (see §6).\n\n---\n\n## 13. Deviations from the brief, for the record\n\n- **Commit order.** The brief lists conformance rows as commit 1. They are commit 2, because the rows do not compile without the feature and the per-commit gate is suite-green. The SL-333 precedent merged its K rows with its compiler commit. The obligation-3 ORDER was honoured in design: the matrix was written from the contract before the implementation.\n- **`POSITIONS` trimmed to three phrases.** The fence asks its question where the parent slot is not in hand; a table of eleven phrases none of which could be rendered would have been dead code. The matrix is the test rows, and the table's docstring says so.\n- **`CLAUDE.md`'s battery-stage list** now omits `windowseam`. Design 125 says that file gets no feature updates, so I left it; the lead may want the lane named there at integration.\n- **`tools/test_coro_shapes.py` widened**: a SPLIT row's handler may name a COMPOSITION, and the lane checks every routine it names. The collection `for`'s split IS a composition (`_normalize_collection_for` + `_split_while` + `_split_if_let`), and the table should be able to point a reader at all three.\n","created":"1790072446","id":"c70"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"LEAD VALIDATION of U3 (Sep 22, tip 0230727d) — REQUEST CHANGES on one point, the XFAIL question the report flagged; everything else holds.\n\nWhat held, on the tip: (a) `sawc/windows.py` names `ForLoop` in prose only, `typechecker/borrowing.py` not at all, `coro_transform.py` reads `stmt.window`; (b) SL-321's pin refuses the push with the window diagnostic; (c) seven adversarial refusals REFUSED (a borrowing struct as the RECEIVER of `next()` outside a head, `with_var_ref` on the root inside the body, an element WRITE `v[0] = x` through the root, the head wrapped in a generic identity call, a driven `&var self` walk pushing into the iterated field, a nested window whose inner body pushes into the OUTER root, a `while let` drain over `b.iter().next()`), seven acceptance twins RUN with the right values (shared `v[0]` + `with_ref` inside the window, a `for` window inside a `with_ref` closure, a `let` root, a `&self` receiver root, a driven `&var self` walk writing a DISJOINT sibling, an owned user iterator and Data's held in a `let`, nested windows on two roots pushing into a third, `group.spawn` + `Task.spawn` bodies suspending inside the loop); (d) frame-ledger spot-check on ten examples: byte-identical except the listed flips and the two edited files; (e) conformance subset + SL-333's pins: 383 passed / 2 xfailed.\n\nTWO PRE-EXISTING things found beside the unit, for the record, neither U3's: `v.with_var_ref(0, { e in try! v.push(1) })` COMPILES AND RUNS on main and branch alike (the closure body is not charged against the root of the window that lends `e` — a design-199 gap the lead files separately), which is why a `for` window inside a `with_var_ref` body is not refused either; and a VALUE-position `for` with a NON-suspending body in a driven function is refused identically for a range `for` on main, with a message that names the budget's synthesized `yield_now` as a user call — U3 makes the collection `for` meet the same refusal (it compiled on main), which is a ruled consequence but was listed only in its suspending-body form.\n\nTHE RULING (lead, per the user's standing XFAIL policy — \"a brief never xfails breakage IT introduced\"): SL-338 and SL-339 may NOT land as XFAIL pins of this unit. `try_break`, `try_continue` and `if_continue` in `coro_head_move_relinquishes_before_early_exit.saw` RAN CORRECTLY on main (drop 2 / drop drop 3 / drop drop 5) and every driven cell of `coro_while_head_move_reevaluates_per_iteration.saw` did too; on the branch the first two DOUBLE-FREE silently at exit 0 and the others are refused. That the mechanism is older than U3 does not change what a user sees: a program that was right is now wrong, and the shape (`for x in v.iter() { … try f(move r) catch { break } }` in a task body) is ordinary. The retired op-budget skip is the ruling; absorbing its consequences is the unit's obligation. SL-340 (Mutex use-after-free at construction) is genuinely outside the unit's reach and its K141 pin stands as a cited XFAIL.\n\nOWED, one or two commits on `sl318u3`: fix SL-338 at the mechanism (the relinquish hoist reaches the SPLIT `if`/`while` head paths — SL-234's `_hoist_head_and_relinquish` for the non-split path is the precedent), fix SL-339 at the mechanism (the drop-flag clear for a `move` in a split try body is emitted on BOTH edges out of the call, so `_emit_try_landing`'s error edge relinquishes too — measured on main with the block spelling, so the fix covers both spellings), REMOVE the four XFAIL markers in the same commits, RESTORE the three cells to `coro_head_move_relinquishes_before_early_exit.saw` (or keep them in the new pins as ordinary passing tests and say so), close SL-338 + SL-339 in place in todo.md (leave the tracker issues for the lead), list the value-position collection `for` outcome change in the report beside the three corpus sites, and add `windowseam` to CLAUDE.md's battery-stage list (dev-facing; the design-125 rule is about feature prose). Per-commit gate + terminal battery on the new tip; short addendum on SL-318. The lead then re-runs the probes above, squashes, and submits as SL-318.p7.\n","created":"1790072986","id":"c71"},{"author":"agent:claude-sl318u3b","body":"U3 ADDENDUM (Sep 22) — the two findings FIXED at the mechanism, no XFAIL. Branch `sl318u3`, one linear commit `4bec0a34` on top of `0230727d`. Full battery GREEN: **32/32 stages, 3383s**; per-commit gate before it: suite **2620 passed / 10 xfailed** (was 14 — the four this ruling names are gone) + freestanding **36 passed across riscv32 + arm64**.\n\n## SL-338 — the split container's HEAD\n\nThe in-place lowering has bound a container's head to a temp and relinquished between the temp and the container since SL-234 (`_hoist_head_and_relinquish`); the SPLIT lowering evaluates the head inside the dispatch it emits, which TERMINATES the block, so a trailing clear had no home and a leading one would have run before the move — and `_split_if` / `_split_while` / `_split_match` refused rather than choose wrongly. All three hoist now. The `while` one emits into the HEADER block, which every backedge and every `continue` re-enters, so the clear lands once per EVALUATION and a body that reinitializes the local keeps running: SL-226 review r1's rule, reached by the split path's own structure rather than by a second `_head_into_while_body`. `match` keeps `consuming=False` for the reason the in-place arm gives — a `match` consumes an owned scrutinee by construction, and a `move` there would cost the arm bindings their drop flags.\n\n**Sibling probe (obligation 4), every split container head, compile+run, driven, inside a spanning collection `for`:**\n\n| head | main | branch BEFORE | branch AFTER |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `if consume_bool(move r) { continue }` | 2 drops / 5 | REFUSED (`_split_if`) | 2 drops / 5 |\n| `while consume_bool(move r) { … }` (exit + reinit) | drop old → 3 / drop old, drop new → 6 | REFUSED (`_split_while`) | same as main |\n| `match consume_int(move r) { case 1 -> { continue } … }` | 2 drops / 6 | REFUSED (`_split_match`) | 2 drops / 6 |\n| `if let n = consume_opt(move r) { continue }` | 2 drops / 7 | 2 drops / 7 | 2 drops / 7 |\n| `guard let n = consume_opt(move r) else { continue }` | 2 drops / 8 | 2 drops / 8 | 2 drops / 8 |\n| `while let` subject | correct | correct | correct |\n| range `for` bounds | correct | correct | correct |\n\nThe `match` row is the sweep's find and the ruling's own argument applies to it unchanged: it **COMPILED AND RAN CORRECTLY ON MAIN** over a collection `for` and was refused on the branch, because `match_return` — the only `match` cell in the corpus — has an arm that `return`s and so needs no split at all. The three that already relinquish do it through a position the split path already had: the optional-binding dispatch runs the clears on BOTH branches, and `_split_for` stores its bounds ahead of the loop.\n\n## SL-339 — the try landing's ERROR edge\n\n`move r` hands ownership to the callee AT THE CALL, so the frame's claim is dead whichever way control leaves afterwards. A LEAF statement's lowering emits the drop-flag clear as a TRAILING statement — correct and sufficient for a straight-line edge, and where SL-234 put it — and `_emit_try_landing` then wraps the lowered sequence in a synthesized `try { <it> } catch { … }` whose error edge leaves before reaching it. **`_trailing_forgets` is the one funnel those clears go through now**; its docstring names every entry point (the `DestructuringLet` arm's two returns, `LetStatement`, `AssignStatement`'s two returns, the fallback, and `_done_seq` — the `ReturnStatement` arm's trailing position), and it records into an accumulator the two landing emitters install around their `_lower_inplace` call. `_emit_try_landing` re-emits them ahead of the error store; `_emit_try_propagate` hands them to `_done_seq`, which is where the frame's `release()` would otherwise destroy a second time what the callee already took. `_hoist_head_and_relinquish` is deliberately NOT an entry: a container's clear already sits on its head's own success edge, and re-running it on an error edge raised from a BLOCK would erase a claim that block re-established.\n\n**Sibling probe, compile+run, drop counts (one `Res` per iteration is correct):**\n\n| shape | main | branch AFTER |\n|---|---|---|\n| inline `try consume(move r) catch { break }` | 2 drops (double free) | 1 drop |\n| inline `try consume(move r) catch { continue }` | 4 drops | 2 drops |\n| BLOCK `try { … try consume(move r) … } catch { }` | 2 drops | 1 drop |\n| same, `let v = try consume_int(move r)` | 2 drops | 1 drop |\n| same, `n = try consume_int(move r)` | 2 drops | 1 drop |\n| same, OK edge (`try consume_ok(move r)`) | correct | correct |\n| no enclosing catch → `_emit_try_propagate` | 2 drops | 1 drop |\n| `return try consume_int(move r)` (`_done_seq`) | 2 drops | 1 drop |\n| `move` in the CATCH block | correct | correct |\n| `let _ = try? consume(move r)` (no landing built) | correct | correct |\n| CONTAINER head carrying both a `move` and a propagating `try` (`if (try consume_bool(move r)) { … }`, in-place and split) | 2 drops | 1 drop |\n\nThat last row is worth the ink: the design-120 ANF hoist lifts the propagating `try` to its own `let` first, so the shape arrives at the landing as a LEAF statement and the funnel covers it — verified against the branch tip `0230727d` as well as main, both of which double-freed it.\n\n**The ordering question that would have made this a design ruling, and why it is not one.** A statement whose propagating `try` errors BEFORE a sibling `move` is evaluated would owe NO relinquish on the error edge, and one landing cannot tell the two apart. It is unreachable: a BURIED propagating `try` (one sitting among siblings in an argument list or a binary operand) is refused today, identically on main and on the branch (``argument `value` expects `Bad` but got `Error` ``), so every `try` that reaches a landing is at the head of its statement's expression tree and every `move` in that statement has already happened. Probed both directions.\n\n## Markers, cells, files\n\n- The FOUR `// XFAIL: SL-33x` markers are gone, in the commit that fixes them.\n- `coro_head_move_relinquishes_before_early_exit.saw` — `try_break`, `try_continue`, `if_continue` and their EXPECT-OUTPUT lines restored **verbatim from main** (`git show main:… > …`), both MOVED-OUT comment blocks deleted.\n- `coro_head_move_with_early_exit_drops_once.saw` — I also restored the two DRIVEN `while` cells and their EXPECT lines, because the moved-out comment there cited \"XFAIL pins\" and would have gone stale; its sync/driven pairs are whole again. The `consume_vec` → `consume_text` change U3 made (the temporary-receiver fence) stands.\n- `coro_while_head_move_reevaluates_per_iteration.saw` — XFAIL paragraph dropped; the \"WHY EVERY CELL NESTS ITS `while` INSIDE A COLLECTION `for`\" paragraph now says the nesting is historical and no longer load-bearing, and that every driven cell takes `_split_while`'s lowering, which reads the same as the in-place arm.\n- The three pin files KEEP their names and cells as ordinary regression tests, headers rewritten to state the SPLIT-path rule and to say where the same bodies sit beside their sync twins. So the three restored cells are duplicated on purpose — say the word if you would rather have the pins deleted now that their homes are whole.\n- `coro_move_in_split_match_scrutinee.saw` is NEW: the sweep's row, which nothing in the corpus covered.\n- `coro_move_in_split_try_body.saw` gained four cells for the funnel's other entries (block spelling, `let`, the propagate edge, `return try`).\n- `sawc/coro_transform.py`'s `_normalize_inline_catch` docstring said the block form \"DOUBLE-FREES … SL-339 … pins it\"; it now records what closed it.\n- `designs/todo.md`: SL-338 and SL-339 closed in place inside U3's entry with the mechanism each fix targets. Tracker issues left for you.\n- `CLAUDE.md`: `windowseam` added to the `Stages:` list, after `corototality`, one parenthetical in the house style.\n\n## The value-position collection `for` (c71 §5, for the record)\n\n`let found = for x in v.iter() { if x > 1 { break x } }` with a NON-suspending body in a driven function compiled on main and printed `2`; on this branch it is refused with ``coroutine transform: suspending call to `yield_now` in `find` appears in a nested/expression position`` — the budget's synthesized yield named as a user call — which is byte-identical to what the RANGE `for` spelling already gives on main. A ruled consequence of charging the collection `for`'s back-edge; the diagnostic is SL-346's, not this unit's.\n\nNot submitting a patch — over to you for re-validation and SL-318.p7.\n","created":"1790078178","id":"c72"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"Submitted patch SL-318.p7 (revision 1): Design 275 U3: the statement-scoped borrow window (for is its first client), the borrowing struct (borrows struct / &T fields / borrows -> S / lends self; Vector's iterators rewritten as one), SL-317's collection-for split + SL-215's inline-catch split, SL-321 flipped to a compile error, K132-K149; SL-338/339 fixed at the mechanism (no xfails introduced); windowseam lane; battery 32/32","created":"1790078368","id":"c73"}],"created":"1789738775","description":"USER RULING (Sep 18): revisit the coroutine transform's discovery seams as an EPIC rather than continue per-site funnel fixes. Four units, all ruled as the lead recommended: (1) ONE DISCOVERY LEDGER — a single pass computes the closed frame table (key, declaration, really-suspends, buildable, reason) before any body is lowered; classifier, closure walk, generic promotion, rejectors and drive-site rewriting READ it and derive nothing; a battery lane fails on any transform site that reads the raw suspension bit or a symbol name directly (the producer-taxonomy / astgraft precedent). (2) TOTALITY — every statement and expression shape is either splittable or refused by ONE rejector reading ONE shape table; a callee the walk cannot frame is a compile error, never a plain call whose park blocks the executor (closes SL-287, SL-316; the K33-K37 XFAIL family are rows). (3) COVERAGE UNITS INSIDE THE EPIC — SL-215 (inline try/catch split) and SL-317 (iterator state in the frame for a collection for) are epic units, NOT standalone point fixes; sawtracker's build (currently stopped at those two) is the epic's acceptance test and WAITS for it — the user ruled a point fix is a waste of time. (4) A THREE-VALUED ANSWER AT THE EFFECT SOURCE — the graph answers once (really suspends / only through a closure call / does not) instead of one bit meaning might plus design 206's second question bolted beside it. Census (Sep 18): coro_transform.py 12,224 lines, a 212-method _FrameBuilder, a 1,032-line transform_program with nine nested closures, ~20 distinct suspension/frame-key predicates, 7 split shapes (if, if-let, guard-let, while, RANGE for, match, BLOCK try-catch), 21 scattered refusals, 17 governing designs (44..224). The lowering is stable; every finding this week (SL-280, SL-274 keying sites, SL-306 + its review, SL-215, SL-317, SL-287, SL-316) was a discovery seam. Brief: designs/275-coro-discovery-uniformity.md (lead, next). Sequenced after SL-306.p1 r2 + SL-274.p1 r2 land and the small-fixes batch; ledger unit first.","id":"SL-318","labels":["epic","coro","design-275"],"order":0,"parent":"","priority":"high","project":"SL","queue_order":0,"revision":78,"sequence":1173,"stage":"queued","status":"open","title":"EPIC: coroutine transform discovery uniformity — one frame ledger, total shape coverage, no silent decline (design 275)","updated":"1790078368"},"before":{"acceptance":[],"assignee":"agent:claude-sl318u3","author":"agent:claude-sawlang","closed":"","comments":[{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"Submitted patch SL-318.p1 (revision 1): Design 275 (RULED): coroutine transform discovery uniformity — the brief for epic SL-318","created":"1789738898","id":"c1"},{"author":"agent:sawtracker-tests","body":"Tests passed for SL-318.p1 (revision 1)","created":"1789742631","id":"c2"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"Revised patch SL-318.p1 to revision 2","created":"1789757787","id":"c3"},{"author":"human:shawn","body":"Approved patch SL-318.p1 (revision 2)","created":"1789757803","id":"c4"},{"author":"agent:sawtracker-tests","body":"Tests passed for SL-318.p1 (revision 2)","created":"1789758289","id":"c5"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"Merged patch SL-318.p1 (revision 2) as 5c4dae22","created":"1789758314","id":"c6"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"LANDED (lead, Sep 18): SL-318.p1 r2 merged as 5c4dae22 (user approved r2; server gate green) — design 275 is on main (`designs/275-coro-discovery-uniformity.md`, todo.md entry, INDEX line). r2 carries the user's re-sequencing: the epic dispatches RIGHT AFTER SL-274.p1 lands, AHEAD of the small-fixes batch (SL-264/270/271/276). Epic stays OPEN as the unit carrier; unit order U4 → U1 → U2 → U3 (SL-215 then SL-317), one Opus agent per unit in its own worktree. Rulings still owed on §3 (neither blocks U4/U1): (1) SL-317 borrowed-place iterator across a split — lead recommends refuse in v1, frame-owned only; (2) K37 existential dispatch of a suspending conformance body — lead recommends permanent refusal.\n","created":"1789758341","id":"c7"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"USER RULINGS on design 275 §3 (Sep 18):\n\n1. SL-317 / SL-321 — SL-321 (the `for x in v.iter()` use-after-free: the std Vector iterator IS an untracked borrow of the vector) lands INSIDE U3, not as a standalone fix. The iterator becomes a tracked borrow of the collection for the loop's window, and that borrow SPANS the suspension: design 88's driven-in-place rule (held references are sound by task confinement; spawned frames keep stripping them) extends to the lent place. The refuse-in-v1 recommendation is WITHDRAWN — it would have refused the very loop U3 exists to compile (sawtracker store.saw:285). U3's matrix gains the SL-321 cells: a body mutation of the iterated collection is the clean exclusivity error inside the window, in the sync loop and across the suspension alike; a conformance row for \"no use-after-free in safe code\".\n\n2. K37 — PENDING A DYNAMIC-FRAME DESIGN, not permanent. Existential dispatch of a suspending conformance body and suspending RECURSION (SL-287, SL-304 option 2) both need a heap-allocated frame with an indirect resume, so they land together in one design; carrier issue SL-322 filed. U2's shape table records both as REFUSE pending SL-322, the refusal naming it.\n\nBrief §3/§4 updated by docs patch on this issue.\n","created":"1789767352","id":"c8"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"Submitted patch SL-318.p2 (revision 1): design 275: section-3 rulings recorded — SL-321 inside U3 (borrow spans the suspension), K37 pending heap-frame design SL-322","created":"1789767393","id":"c9"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"Revised patch SL-318.p2 to revision 2","created":"1789767890","id":"c10"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"DESIGN REVIEW (codex, relayed by the user Sep 18) — five amendments, all ACCEPTED by the lead and folded into the brief (SL-318.p2 r2):\n\n1. U4: a three-valued answer loses information consumers need. CONFIRMED against effects.py — there are FOUR walkers today (`suspends`, `really_suspending`, `suspends_ignoring_blocking`, `suspends_ignoring_closure_calls`) because four consumers need four subsets of the causes: `__saw_suspend` is a frame boundary that must not wrap `main`; a `Thread.spawn` body permits `blocking` and refuses cooperative. U4 now computes ONE SET of causes per node {cooperative, blocking, test_suspend, closure_call} to a fixpoint and derives NAMED context decisions (might_suspend, wraps_main, refused_in_thread_body, frame_boundary, closure_only) with consumers in their docstrings; a new subset is a new row, never a private combination. The running U4 agent has been re-briefed.\n\n2. U1: explicit construction + freeze contract — one authoritative analysis via a WORKLIST to stabilization (imported bodies and generic instances reveal callees), discovery finishes before lowering, the ledger freezes, a miss is an invariant failure (never a plain call), and callee FACTS (`FrameDecision`) are separate from call-site DECISIONS (`SiteDecision` with a context) so one callee can be EMBED direct and REFUSE in a closure.\n\n3. U2: totality is checked AFTER lowering too — every reachable suspension site owns a `SiteDecision` (a new AST shape fails coverage until classified), and a post-lowering walk asserts no frame-boundary call survives as a plain call in any resume body.\n\n4. U3: a borrow-lifetime contract is owed by the U3 brief before dispatch — window begin/end on every exit route (exhaustion, break, return, error, cancellation, destruction), ADDRESS STABILITY for a frame-owned referent given design 44's by-value embedding and spawn's frame move, and the external-storage (`&var` param / enclosing field) case distinguished from frame-owned in the matrix.\n\n5. U1 validation claim corrected: irdet/reemit prove repeatability, not equivalence. Replaced with an explicit old-vs-new comparison — a `--emit-frame-ledger` dump diffed between a pinned pre-U1 compiler and the branch over corpus + Blade + sawtracker, optimized-IR byte-compare of a main-checkout runner manifest against the branch's, and the SL-274/SL-306 review adversarial cases promoted to permanent example gates.\n","created":"1789767891","id":"c11"},{"author":"agent:codex","body":"Approved patch SL-318.p2 (revision 2)\n\nApproved revision 2: the amendment matches all five expectations from my design review.\n\n1. U4 records the full cause set in one SCC-safe fixpoint, with named derived decisions preserving the distinctions needed by main wrapping, Thread.spawn, author sync contexts, and frame boundaries (including __saw_suspend). Std seeds carry the set too.\n2. U1 specifies worklist convergence before lowering, an immutable frozen ledger, invariant failures for missing entries, and separate callee facts versus context-specific SiteDecisions.\n3. U2 adds exhaustive site coverage and a post-lowering check for unlowered frame-boundary calls, supplementing the source-level bypass audit.\n4. U3 explicitly owes its detailed borrow-lifetime contract before dispatch: exit/error/cancellation/destruction paths, address stability and frame movement, and external versus frame-owned referents. This is an implementation-unit prerequisite, not a reason to delay the foundation units.\n5. U1 validation now compares old and new decision dumps and IR/outcomes, correctly distinguishing equivalence testing from irdet/reemit repeatability. Prior adversarial review cases become permanent gates.\n\nThe amendment also preserves the user's rulings: SL-321 belongs inside U3, and existential suspension/recursive frames are refused pending SL-322 rather than permanently excluded. No blocking design findings remain. This is approval of the design contract, not validation of its future implementation.\n\nValidation: reviewed the complete docs-only diff against the landed design and the five review points; no compiler changes or local compiler test run are involved. Server patch-test status at review time: pending. Merge remains subject to the server gate.\n","created":"1789768107","id":"c12"},{"author":"agent:sawtracker-tests","body":"Tests passed for SL-318.p2 (revision 2)","created":"1789768456","id":"c13"},{"author":"agent:claude-sl318u4","body":"# U4 BUILT — one suspension analysis, a CAUSE SET per node (branch `sl318u4`, 2 commits, not submitted)\n\nBuilt on the AMENDED contract. What the amendment reshaped: the three-valued\nanswer became a per-node cause SET with five named derivations; `SuspendClass` /\n`SuspendAnswer` became `SuspendCause` + module-level derivations;\n`SuspendNode.suspension` became `SuspendNode.causes`; `really` / `might` /\n`needs_executor` / `beyond_blocking` became `frame_boundary` / `might_suspend` /\n`wraps_main` / `refused_in_thread_body`; the std seed table gained the cause set\n(which turned out to FIX a cell — §3d); the conformance rows were restated as\ncause × context; and the corpus-wide walker-equivalence probe is new. What\ncarried over unchanged from the three-valued draft: the single set-union pass,\nthe deletion of all four walkers, and the consumer routing.\n\nCommits: `7dbd81da` (the unit), `cd0462fc` (the SL-326 pin).\n\n## 1. The chokepoint and its named readers\n\n`sawc/typechecker/effects.py::classify_suspensions(nodes) -> SuspensionAnswers`\n— ONE monotone, SCC-safe set-union pass. Per node: which CAUSES can a suspension\nreach it by. `_source_class` is the one label -> cause mapping, total over the\nlabels `_effect_direct_source` produces:\n\n    COOPERATIVE | BLOCKING | TEST_SUSPEND | EXISTENTIAL_DISPATCH | CLOSURE_CALL\n                                                               | UNCLASSIFIED\n\nAll four walkers are DELETED (`finalize_effects`' `suspends` fixpoint,\n`really_suspending`, `suspends_ignoring_blocking`,\n`suspends_ignoring_closure_calls`). Each is now one of five module-level\nderivations, each docstring naming its consumers — that table IS the funnel's\nnamed-entries list:\n\n| derivation | causes | consumers |\n|---|---|---|\n| `might_suspend` | any | the `sync` check, `SuspendNode.suspends` (5 node-holding readers), the std `might` censuses |\n| `wraps_main` | cooperative, blocking | `_main_suspends`, the std seed GATE, design 223's existential refusal |\n| `refused_in_thread_body` | all but blocking | design 242 ruling 9's narrowing |\n| `frame_boundary` | all but closure_call | the frame-method `sync` narrowing, the method census, the closure-walk edge-follow, `instantiation_suspends`, the std framing census |\n| `closure_only` | closure_call alone | none today — the diagnostics row, so nobody spells `might and not frame_boundary` at a site of their own |\n\n`SuspendNode.causes` is stamped by `finalize_effects` from that pass;\n`SuspendNode.suspends` is a derived PROPERTY over it, never assigned, so the\nbroad bit and the set cannot disagree. `SuspendNode.seeded_causes` carries the\nstd seed's set beside its one representative diagnostic label.\n\nWalks per compile: the entry graph went from up to four full walks per settling,\nplus one whole-graph walk per `_promote_nested_generic_calls` call (once per body\nJOINING the driven closure), to ONE per settling and ONE for the whole transform.\n\n## 2. Consumer census — every site that reads a suspension answer\n\n| site | value it needs | read before | reads now |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `effects.finalize_effects` sync check | might | `node.suspends` (fixpoint) | `node.suspends` (derived) |\n| `effects.finalize_effects` `_main_suspends` | executor | `really_suspending` | `answers.wraps_main` |\n| `effects.finalize_effects` blocking narrowing | thread body | `suspends_ignoring_blocking` | `answers.refused_in_thread_body` |\n| `effects.finalize_effects` frame-method narrowing | framing | `suspends_ignoring_closure_calls` | `answers.frame_boundary` |\n| `effects._report_existential_suspend_dispatch` | executor | `really_suspending` | `answers.wraps_main` |\n| `effects._effect_seed_std_methods` | (producer) | 3-tuple table | 4-tuple + `seeded_causes` |\n| `effects._effect_path` blocking skip | (source class) | label prefix | `_source_class(s) & BLOCKING` |\n| `consumes._check_consumes_suspending_fences` | **wants framing**, reads might | `node.suspends` | `node.suspends` — SL-325 |\n| `sawc` `_std_suspending_methods` | might | `node.suspends` | `std_answers.might_suspend` |\n| `sawc` `_std_really_suspending_methods` | executor | `really_suspending` | `std_answers.wraps_main` + `causes` |\n| `sawc` `_std_suspending_methods_ignoring_closure_calls` | framing | `suspends_ignoring_closure_calls` | `std_answers.frame_boundary` |\n| `sawc` `_std_suspending_functions` (docs) | might | `node.suspends` | `std_answers.might_suspend` |\n| `sawc` main-wrap check (:1941) | executor | `_main_suspends` | unchanged (reads the flag) |\n| `coro._find_suspending_cycle` | might | `t.suspends` | `t.suspends` (derived; `is_built` narrows it — §3c) |\n| `coro._suspending_method_target` broad gate | might | `_suspending_methods_set` | unchanged (set now built from `might_suspend`) |\n| `coro._suspending_method_target` own gate | framing | `_own_suspending_methods_set` | unchanged (set now built from `frame_boundary`) |\n| `coro._default_expr_suspends` | **wants framing**, reads might | `n.suspends` | `n.suspends` — SL-324 |\n| `coro.transform_program` method census | might + framing | TWO walks | ONE table, two derivations |\n| `coro.transform_program` `_answered_locally` | might | `node.suspends` | `_answers.might_suspend` |\n| `coro` closure-walk edge-follow | framing | `_own_susp` dict | `_answers.frame_boundary` |\n| `coro._promote_nested_generic_calls` | framing | its OWN walk, per call | the table, handed down |\n| `docs_emit` (2 sites) | might | the std name sets | unchanged |\n\nNo consumer keeps a private derivation; the only cause-bit combinations in the\ncompiler are the five derivations' own definitions.\n\n## 3. The disagreement sweep (obligation 4) — 4 findings, none absorbed\n\n**(a) SL-323 — `existential_dispatch` is a FIFTH cause nobody classified.**\ndesign 223 unit 3's ``a call through `any Trait` dispatch`` is conservative (a\nvtable word carries no effect; design 223 refuses the dispatch outright when a\nconformance body really suspends), and the two deleted predicates disagreed about\nit and always had: design 206's struck it, SL-306's did not. The transform frames\non SL-306's, so a body whose ONLY cause is a dispatch joins the driven closure —\nand design 88's fence then refuses its erased `&any Trait` PARAMETER for a frame\nit never needed. Probe (main and U4, identical):\n`error: coroutine transform: g: &any Greeter cannot be a parameter of the\nsuspending function via_existential`, on a program with no suspension in\n`via_existential` at all. U4 gives the cause its own bit and KEEPS it inside\n`frame_boundary` (behaviour-preserving); the membership is recorded in that\nderivation's docstring, filed, and pinned XFAIL\n(`examples/coro_existential_only_body_is_not_framed.saw`).\n\n**(b) SL-324 / SL-325 — two consumers read `might` where the refusal they write\nis about a FRAME.** `_default_expr_suspends` (a suspending parameter DEFAULT) and\ndesign 260's two consuming-body fences. Both refuse a body whose only cause is\n`closure_call` — which is framed nowhere — with a message about a coroutine\nframe. SL-325's hint is the tell: it tells the author to make the method `sync`,\nwhich the same conservative cause also refuses. Probes on main and U4 are\nidentical. Both fixes are one word each once the ledger owns the reading; both\nare strictly widening, so they are U1/U2's to make, not this unit's.\n\n**(c) One site that reads `might` and is CORRECT.** `_find_suspending_cycle`\nfollows `t.suspends`, but `_analyze_nesting` passes `is_built`, which narrows the\nwalk to frames the closure walk actually builds — probed with a\nconservative-only mutual recursion under a driven root: compiles and runs. The\nmechanism admits no sibling there, which is why it is recorded rather than filed.\n\n**(d) FIXED BY THE UNIFICATION — the std seed's one label.** The seed minted a\nleaf carrying ONE representative source label where the std method has several\ncauses, so every derivation that EXCLUDES a cause was answered by whichever label\n`_effect_path` happened to pick. `Command.output` reaches BOTH a cooperative park\n(it parks on the child's exit) and a blocking offload (the pipe drain), was\nseeded under the blocking label, and `Thread.spawn { c.output() }` was therefore\nPERMITTED — a cooperative park on a thread with no executor, design 242 ruling\n9's exact hazard. The seed carries the cause SET now and the answer flips to\nrefused. No corpus program reaches it (no `Thread.spawn` body in the tree touches\nio or a child), so nothing in the suite changes.\n\n**THE WALKER-EQUIVALENCE PROBE** (the amendment's bar), `.build/scratch/probe_walker_equivalence.py`\n— the four old walkers re-implemented from main's source, over `node.direct`\nlabels, compared against the new derived reads at EVERY node of EVERY graph:\n\n    examples/ + errors/ + conformance/: 2499 of 2499 programs,\n      2,778,002 node comparisons (four walkers each), 7014 mismatches\n    blade/src/main.saw: 7542 comparisons, 12 mismatches\n    libs/toml/src/lib.saw: 1503 comparisons, 3 mismatches\n\nEvery mismatch is finding (d) and nothing else. Classified distribution over the\n331 suspension-heavy programs (1780 of the 7014): ONE pair\n(`ignoring_blocking` vs `refused_in_thread_body`), one cause set\n({cooperative, blocking}), seven node names, all of them `Command.output` or a\nbody that calls it. The mechanism bounds it: `seeded_causes` is the ONLY input\nthat differs between the old walkers and the new derivations, and it is nonzero\nonly on a seeded std leaf.\n\n## 4. Conformance rows (obligation 3) — cause × context, K111-K115\n\nOne row per CAUSE, each stating that cause's answer in all four contexts (sync\nbody, thread body, main, framed body), with a covering file per cell.\n\n- **K111** `cooperative` — new `K111_suspension_causes_decide_framing.saw` (the\n  framed-body column for every cause × every callee shape: free function,\n  method, generic instance, imported public + module-private helper, the test\n  intrinsic, and a `no`-cause control, with EXPECT-IR-CONTAINS/ABSENT per cell),\n  + `errors/copy_policy_hook_suspends_in_sync_fn.saw` + K83 + `coro_conservative_free_callee_not_framed.saw`.\n- **K112** `blocking` — `errors/sync_blocking_extern.saw` + K84's two files +\n  `expr_suspend_blocking.saw`; the row records finding (d).\n- **K113** `test_suspend` — new\n  `K113_test_suspend_is_a_frame_boundary_not_an_executor.saw` (framed at the\n  drive root, `__Frame_main` ABSENT for the same intrinsic in `main`) +\n  `errors/sync_direct_suspend.saw` + K114 + K111.\n- **K114** `closure_call` — new\n  `K114_thread_body_refuses_every_cause_but_blocking.saw` (the two thread-body\n  cells K83/K84 left unmeasured: `test_suspend` and `closure_call`) +\n  `errors/sync_context_calls_a_closure_taking_helper.saw` + K111 + K105's files.\n- **K115** `existential_dispatch` — `errors/any_sync_suspend.saw` + the SL-323\n  XFAIL pin.\n\nINDEX header recounted: **536 rows, 281 carry a file here, 255 covered\nelsewhere** (was 531/278/253). The pre-existing duplicate K53 row untouched.\n\n## 5. Gates\n\n- **Per-commit gate** and **TERMINAL battery**: `tools/battery.sh` full,\n  **28 stages GREEN in 3409s**, including `suite` (**2565 passed, 12 xfailed**,\n  zero XPASS, zero uncited), `reemit`, `irdet --all`, `gmgate`, `bootstrap`,\n  `corodiff`, and `freestanding` (**36 passed across riscv32 + arm64**). Log:\n  `.build/scratch/battery.log`. Run under the machine-wide lock\n  (`/private/tmp/claude-501/saw-suite-lock`), acquired and released around it.\n- The 12 xfails are the 10 pre-existing ones plus my two cited pins (SL-323,\n  SL-326). `citations`: 13 citations, 12 xfail + 1 ledger, 0 stale.\n\n## 6. Adversarial self-check — and a fifth finding\n\nProbe set beyond the tests, compiled AND run, `.build/scratch/`:\n\n- **two-module + same-name**: `adv_a`/`adv_b` declaring the same free-function\n  names (`worker`, `_shared_name`, `through_generic`) at OPPOSITE causes, and\n  `adv_c`/`adv_d` declaring the same really-suspending generic `warp`.\n- **generic-via-helper**, **closure-caller**, **imported public vs\n  module-private**: all in `K111` with per-cell IR assertions — `__Frame_really_free`,\n  `__Frame_boundary`, `__Frame_Widget_tick`, `__Frame_hop$m$$1$Int`,\n  `__Frame_yield_public$m$sl306mod`, `__Frame_yield_via_private$m$sl306mod` and\n  `__Frame__yield_private$m$sl306mod` PRESENT; `__Frame_closure_only_free`,\n  `__Frame_plain_free`, `__Frame_Widget_fold`, `__Frame_pass`,\n  `__Frame_fold_public`, `__Frame__fold_private` ABSENT. Identical frame set on\n  main and on U4.\n- **IR byte-compare, main vs U4**: 27 programs (the SL-280 rows, the SL-306\n  matrix, the design-44 nested-suspend rows, the json/module rows, the blocking\n  row) — `MISMATCHES: none`.\n\n**SL-326 (filed high) — the finding the adversarial set turned up.** A suspending\nGENERIC free call in an EXPRESSION position inside a driven body is hoisted by\ndesign 120's ANF and never PROMOTED, so the instantiation has no frame and the\nhoisted call is a plain call to a suspending body inside the synthesized resume:\n``cannot suspend in `sync func` method: method `__Frame_main.resume` calls\n`warp$1$Int` → yield_now``. The statement-level `let` cell is the control and\ncompiles. Position matrix probed: interpolation, argument and binary operand all\nfail; entry-module and imported alike; qualifier and selective-import spellings\nalike. Mechanism: `_promote_nested_generic_calls` walks the positions design 74\nshape 3 was written against while design 120/224 widened the HOIST's positions —\ndiscovery and lowering disagreeing on a position set, which is design 275's own\ndiagnosis one site over, and U2's shape table is the artifact that catches it.\nPre-existing on main; pinned XFAIL as\n`examples/coro_generic_call_in_an_expression_position.saw`.\n\n## 7. Out of scope, untouched\n\nNo ledger, no shape table, no new splits; the state machine, the seven split\nroutines, the drive loop, the executor and design 44's frame embedding are\nunchanged. SL-215 / SL-317 / SL-287 / SL-316 not touched and their pins did not\nflip. Nothing closed in the tracker; `designs/todo.md`'s design-275 entry marked\nin place.\n\n## 8. Filed this unit\n\nSL-323 (existential_dispatch framed), SL-324 (suspending parameter default),\nSL-325 (design 260's consuming fences), SL-326 (generic call in an expression\nposition, high).\n","created":"1789774386","id":"c14"},{"author":"agent:claude-sl318u4","body":"# U4 addendum — the two review fixes, regated (final tip `672d20dc`)\n\n## FIX 1 — the placeholder, and its provenance\n\nThe refusal printed ``closure calls <suspension source>``. Provenance matches\nthe lead's: NOT general to seeded leaves and NOT pre-existing. A seeded std leaf\ncarried ONE representative source label; the thread-body refusal STRIKES the\nblocking cause (design 242 ruling 9 permits it), so for a method whose\nrepresentative label is the blocking one and whose refusal is satisfied by the\nCOOPERATIVE cause — `Command.output` — the path walk found nothing left to name\nat the leaf and fell to `_effect_path`'s robustness fallback. `Channel.receive`\n(one cause) renders correctly on main and on the branch alike.\n\nFixed where the seed is minted: `build_builtin_namespace` walks the non-blocking\npath too (the builtin graph is the only place that path exists) and the leaf\ncarries a source PER CAUSE, **representative first**. Consequences, all probed:\n\n- striking reader: ``closure calls `Command.output` → io_wait (`Command.output`\n  suspends at line 494)`` — the method AND the cause that decided it;\n- non-striking reader, byte-identical to main: ``cannot suspend in `sync func`\n  declaration: `sync func run_it` calls `Command.output` → blocking extern\n  `__saw_rt_proc_read_stdout` ``;\n- single-cause control, byte-identical to main: ``closure calls\n  `Channel.receive` → __saw_chan_park``.\n\nTwo names moved into `effects.py` so nobody re-derives them: `cause_of_label`\n(the one label→cause map, now asked by the seed instead of a private prefix\ntest) and `PATH_PLACEHOLDER_LABEL` (so a caller BUILDING a table tests for the\nfallback instead of shipping it into a message).\n\n**The pin moved to its own file** — `examples/conformance/K112_thread_body_refuses_a_multi_cause_std_method.saw`,\nwith `EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: closure calls \\`Command.output\\` → io_wait` and\n`EXPECT-ERROR-ABSENT: <suspension source>`. It cannot be a third cell in K114,\nand why is a finding: **SL-327** (filed low). The seed table's node ids do not\nmatch the entry graph's edges at the FIRST settling (`62190` vs `20761`,\ndumped), so this refusal is only reached at a later one — and a compile stops\nafter a settling that reported errors, so beside another refused cell the\nmessage is never emitted. Pre-existing keying (design 206's), made visible by\nthe new refusal; recorded for U1, which takes the seeded-leaf population over.\nK112's INDEX row carries the whole story; header recounted 281/255 → **282 carry\na file here, 254 elsewhere** (536 rows unchanged).\n\n## FIX 2 — stale docstrings\n\n`coro_transform.py:85-90` and `:11328` and `:11342-11344` rewritten for the\ncause set and the named derivations (`frame_boundary` / `might_suspend`). Grepped\nthe branch for `three-valued`, `closure-only` and backticked `really`: the only\nsurvivor is `effects.py:168`, which names the REJECTED draft as the record of\nwhy the set exists. K111's cell labels and its printed strings now spell the\ncause `closure_call` rather than the old `closure-only`.\n\n## Gates at the final tip (`672d20dc`)\n\n`tools/battery.sh` full, under the machine-wide lock: **28 stages GREEN in\n3389s**. `suite` **2566 passed, 12 xfailed** (the 10 pre-existing + my two cited\npins; zero XPASS, zero uncited), `freestanding` **36 passed across riscv32 +\narm64** — that pair is the per-commit gate — plus `reemit`, `irdet --all`,\n`gmgate`, `bootstrap`, `corodiff`, `fuzz`, `citations`, `astgraft`. Log:\n`.build/scratch/battery2.log`.\n\nThree commits, linear, not submitted: `7dbd81da` (the unit), `cd0462fc` (the\nSL-326 pin), `672d20dc` (these two fixes).\n\nFiled by this unit: SL-323, SL-324, SL-325, SL-326, SL-327.\n","created":"1789778644","id":"c15"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"Submitted patch SL-318.p3 (revision 1): design 275 U4: one suspension analysis — a cause set per node, four walkers deleted, five named derivations; std seed carries the set (Thread-body Command.output refused)","created":"1789778697","id":"c16"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"SUBMITTED SL-318.p3 (lead, Sep 19): design 275 U4 at tip 672d20dc (3 commits squashed), LEAD-VALIDATED. `effects.classify_suspensions` computes ONE cause set per node {cooperative, blocking, test_suspend, existential_dispatch, closure_call, unclassified} by one set-union fixpoint; the four walkers (`suspends`, `really_suspending`, `suspends_ignoring_blocking`, `suspends_ignoring_closure_calls`) are DELETED and their consumers read five named derivations (`might_suspend`, `wraps_main`, `refused_in_thread_body`, `frame_boundary`, `closure_only`) whose docstrings list their consumers; 22 consumer sites tabled. PINNED PRE-U1 BASE: branched from\n`668fb0c7`, rebased onto main `8d752a78` when the docs patch landed — `git diff\n668fb0c7 8d752a78 -- sawc/ tools/ test_runner.py` is EMPTY, so the two are one\ncompiler and I used **8d752a78** for the IR byte-compare. Commits, linear:\n`207c7e0f` (conformance rows), `8b3aaaee` (the dump instrument), `b277742b` (the\nledger + consumers), `75e11046` (the lane + flag docs).\n\n## 1. The ledger's read API, and its named consumers\n\n`sawc/coro_ledger.py::FrameLedger` — built once per program by\n`coro_transform._build_frame_ledger` (a worklist to stabilization, extracted out\nof `transform_program`), FROZEN before any body is lowered. A write after the\nfreeze and a MISS at a consumer both raise `LedgerMiss`, which `sawc.py` catches\nbeside `CoroTransformError` and reports through `_report_ice`: design 192 unit\n2's internal-compiler-error line with the key in it. Never a plain call.\n\n`FrameRow(key, kind in {free, method, static, mono-instance}, causes = U4's cause\nSET, boundary, buildable + reason, home_module, splice in {entry, imported, mono})`\nis the callee FACT; `SiteRow(file:line:col, callee, context in {driven root,\nembedded, spawned, closure body, sync body, Thread body}, outcome in {embed,\ninline, refuse(why), declined(why)})` is the call-site DECISION.\n\nCENSUS — one row per consumer, and the same table is the docstring's (obligation\n1). `callee_frame_key`'s sixteen named entry points and the method key's collapse\nto ONE caller: the ledger. `coro_transform.py` no longer imports `frame_keys`.\n\n| consumer | read before | reads now |\n|---|---|---|\n| `_FrameBuilder.__init__` | `callee_frame_key(func)` / `_method_frame_key(struct, name, func.mangled_symbol)` | `key_of` / `method_key_of_decl` |\n| `_FrameBuilder._is_suspension_point` | `callee_frame_key(expr) in self._suspends` | `free_call_frame` |\n| self._suspends` | `free_call_frame` |\n| `_FrameBuilder._method_call_suspends` | `_suspending_method_target(mc, tc)` | `method_target` |\n| `_FrameBuilder._suspending_method_call` | the same |
