The lead then re-runs the probes above, squashes, and submits as SL-318.p7.\n","created":"1790072986","id":"c71"},{"author":"agent:claude-sl318u3b","body":"U3 ADDENDUM (Sep 22) — the two findings FIXED at the mechanism, no XFAIL. Branch `sl318u3`, one linear commit `4bec0a34` on top of `0230727d`. Full battery GREEN: **32/32 stages, 3383s**; per-commit gate before it: suite **2620 passed / 10 xfailed** (was 14 — the four this ruling names are gone) + freestanding **36 passed across riscv32 + arm64**.\n\n## SL-338 — the split container's HEAD\n\nThe in-place lowering has bound a container's head to a temp and relinquished between the temp and the container since SL-234 (`_hoist_head_and_relinquish`); the SPLIT lowering evaluates the head inside the dispatch it emits, which TERMINATES the block, so a trailing clear had no home and a leading one would have run before the move — and `_split_if` / `_split_while` / `_split_match` refused rather than choose wrongly. All three hoist now. The `while` one emits into the HEADER block, which every backedge and every `continue` re-enters, so the clear lands once per EVALUATION and a body that reinitializes the local keeps running: SL-226 review r1's rule, reached by the split path's own structure rather than by a second `_head_into_while_body`. `match` keeps `consuming=False` for the reason the in-place arm gives — a `match` consumes an owned scrutinee by construction, and a `move` there would cost the arm bindings their drop flags.\n\n**Sibling probe (obligation 4), every split container head, compile+run, driven, inside a spanning collection `for`:**\n\n| head | main | branch BEFORE | branch AFTER |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `if consume_bool(move r) { continue }` | 2 drops / 5 | REFUSED (`_split_if`) | 2 drops / 5 |\n| `while consume_bool(move r) { … }` (exit + reinit) | drop old → 3 / drop old, drop new → 6 | REFUSED (`_split_while`) | same as main |\n| `match consume_int(move r) { case 1 -> { continue } … }` | 2 drops / 6 | REFUSED (`_split_match`) | 2 drops / 6 |\n| `if let n = consume_opt(move r) { continue }` | 2 drops / 7 | 2 drops / 7 | 2 drops / 7 |\n| `guard let n = consume_opt(move r) else { continue }` | 2 drops / 8 | 2 drops / 8 | 2 drops / 8 |\n| `while let` subject | correct | correct | correct |\n| range `for` bounds | correct | correct | correct |\n\nThe `match` row is the sweep's find and the ruling's own argument applies to it unchanged: it **COMPILED AND RAN CORRECTLY ON MAIN** over a collection `for` and was refused on the branch, because `match_return` — the only `match` cell in the corpus — has an arm that `return`s and so needs no split at all. The three that already relinquish do it through a position the split path already had: the optional-binding dispatch runs the clears on BOTH branches, and `_split_for` stores its bounds ahead of the loop.\n\n## SL-339 — the try landing's ERROR edge\n\n`move r` hands ownership to the callee AT THE CALL, so the frame's claim is dead whichever way control leaves afterwards. A LEAF statement's lowering emits the drop-flag clear as a TRAILING statement — correct and sufficient for a straight-line edge, and where SL-234 put it — and `_emit_try_landing` then wraps the lowered sequence in a synthesized `try { <it> } catch { … }` whose error edge leaves before reaching it. **`_trailing_forgets` is the one funnel those clears go through now**; its docstring names every entry point (the `DestructuringLet` arm's two returns, `LetStatement`, `AssignStatement`'s two returns, the fallback, and `_done_seq` — the `ReturnStatement` arm's trailing position), and it records into an accumulator the two landing emitters install around their `_lower_inplace` call. `_emit_try_landing` re-emits them ahead of the error store; `_emit_try_propagate` hands them to `_done_seq`, which is where the frame's `release()` would otherwise destroy a second time what the callee already took. `_hoist_head_and_relinquish` is deliberately NOT an entry: a container's clear already sits on its head's own success edge, and re-running it on an error edge raised from a BLOCK would erase a claim that block re-established.\n\n**Sibling probe, compile+run, drop counts (one `Res` per iteration is correct):**\n\n| shape | main | branch AFTER |\n|---|---|---|\n| inline `try consume(move r) catch { break }` | 2 drops (double free) | 1 drop |\n| inline `try consume(move r) catch { continue }` | 4 drops | 2 drops |\n| BLOCK `try { … try consume(move r) … } catch { }` | 2 drops | 1 drop |\n| same, `let v = try consume_int(move r)` | 2 drops | 1 drop |\n| same, `n = try consume_int(move r)` | 2 drops | 1 drop |\n| same, OK edge (`try consume_ok(move r)`) | correct | correct |\n| no enclosing catch → `_emit_try_propagate` | 2 drops | 1 drop |\n| `return try consume_int(move r)` (`_done_seq`) | 2 drops | 1 drop |\n| `move` in the CATCH block | correct | correct |\n| `let _ = try? consume(move r)` (no landing built) | correct | correct |\n| CONTAINER head carrying both a `move` and a propagating `try` (`if (try consume_bool(move r)) { … }`, in-place and split) | 2 drops | 1 drop |\n\nThat last row is worth the ink: the design-120 ANF hoist lifts the propagating `try` to its own `let` first, so the shape arrives at the landing as a LEAF statement and the funnel covers it — verified against the branch tip `0230727d` as well as main, both of which double-freed it.\n\n**The ordering question that would have made this a design ruling, and why it is not one.** A statement whose propagating `try` errors BEFORE a sibling `move` is evaluated would owe NO relinquish on the error edge, and one landing cannot tell the two apart. It is unreachable: a BURIED propagating `try` (one sitting among siblings in an argument list or a binary operand) is refused today, identically on main and on the branch (``argument `value` expects `Bad` but got `Error` ``), so every `try` that reaches a landing is at the head of its statement's expression tree and every `move` in that statement has already happened. Probed both directions.\n\n## Markers, cells, files\n\n- The FOUR `// XFAIL: SL-33x` markers are gone, in the commit that fixes them.\n- `coro_head_move_relinquishes_before_early_exit.saw` — `try_break`, `try_continue`, `if_continue` and their EXPECT-OUTPUT lines restored **verbatim from main** (`git show main:… > …`), both MOVED-OUT comment blocks deleted.\n- `coro_head_move_with_early_exit_drops_once.saw` — I also restored the two DRIVEN `while` cells and their EXPECT lines, because the moved-out comment there cited \"XFAIL pins\" and would have gone stale; its sync/driven pairs are whole again. The `consume_vec` → `consume_text` change U3 made (the temporary-receiver fence) stands.\n- `coro_while_head_move_reevaluates_per_iteration.saw` — XFAIL paragraph dropped; the \"WHY EVERY CELL NESTS ITS `while` INSIDE A COLLECTION `for`\" paragraph now says the nesting is historical and no longer load-bearing, and that every driven cell takes `_split_while`'s lowering, which reads the same as the in-place arm.\n- The three pin files KEEP their names and cells as ordinary regression tests, headers rewritten to state the SPLIT-path rule and to say where the same bodies sit beside their sync twins. So the three restored cells are duplicated on purpose — say the word if you would rather have the pins deleted now that their homes are whole.\n- `coro_move_in_split_match_scrutinee.saw` is NEW: the sweep's row, which nothing in the corpus covered.\n- `coro_move_in_split_try_body.saw` gained four cells for the funnel's other entries (block spelling, `let`, the propagate edge, `return try`).\n- `sawc/coro_transform.py`'s `_normalize_inline_catch` docstring said the block form \"DOUBLE-FREES … SL-339 … pins it\"; it now records what closed it.\n- `designs/todo.md`: SL-338 and SL-339 closed in place inside U3's entry with the mechanism each fix targets. Tracker issues left for you.\n- `CLAUDE.md`: `windowseam` added to the `Stages:` list, after `corototality`, one parenthetical in the house style.\n\n## The value-position collection `for` (c71 §5, for the record)\n\n`let found = for x in v.iter() { if x > 1 { break x } }` with a NON-suspending body in a driven function compiled on main and printed `2`; on this branch it is refused with ``coroutine transform: suspending call to `yield_now` in `find` appears in a nested/expression position`` — the budget's synthesized yield named as a user call — which is byte-identical to what the RANGE `for` spelling already gives on main. A ruled consequence of charging the collection `for`'s back-edge; the diagnostic is SL-346's, not this unit's.\n\nNot submitting a patch — over to you for re-validation and SL-318.p7.\n","created":"1790078178","id":"c72"}],"created":"1789738775","description":"USER RULING (Sep 18): revisit the coroutine transform's discovery seams as an EPIC rather than continue per-site funnel fixes. Four units, all ruled as the lead recommended: (1) ONE DISCOVERY LEDGER — a single pass computes the closed frame table (key, declaration, really-suspends, buildable, reason) before any body is lowered; classifier, closure walk, generic promotion, rejectors and drive-site rewriting READ it and derive nothing; a battery lane fails on any transform site that reads the raw suspension bit or a symbol name directly (the producer-taxonomy / astgraft precedent). (2) TOTALITY — every statement and expression shape is either splittable or refused by ONE rejector reading ONE shape table; a callee the walk cannot frame is a compile error, never a plain call whose park blocks the executor (closes SL-287, SL-316; the K33-K37 XFAIL family are rows). (3) COVERAGE UNITS INSIDE THE EPIC — SL-215 (inline try/catch split) and SL-317 (iterator state in the frame for a collection for) are epic units, NOT standalone point fixes; sawtracker's build (currently stopped at those two) is the epic's acceptance test and WAITS for it — the user ruled a point fix is a waste of time. (4) A THREE-VALUED ANSWER AT THE EFFECT SOURCE — the graph answers once (really suspends / only through a closure call / does not) instead of one bit meaning might plus design 206's second question bolted beside it. Census (Sep 18): coro_transform.py 12,224 lines, a 212-method _FrameBuilder, a 1,032-line transform_program with nine nested closures, ~20 distinct suspension/frame-key predicates, 7 split shapes (if, if-let, guard-let, while, RANGE for, match, BLOCK try-catch), 21 scattered refusals, 17 governing designs (44..224). The lowering is stable; every finding this week (SL-280, SL-274 keying sites, SL-306 + its review, SL-215, SL-317, SL-287, SL-316) was a discovery seam. Brief: designs/275-coro-discovery-uniformity.md (lead, next). Two refusals follow —\\n   an outer `-> &var T` backed by an inner `-> &T` is refused at the `lend`, and\\n   an EXCLUSIVE-ONLY inner accessor makes the outer one exclusive-only too.\\n-  v1 fences: a borrows body is `sync` (a window never spans a suspend —\\n-  `with_ref`/`with_var_ref` stay the long-window spelling), no borrows function\\n+  **A BORROWING STRUCT holds a lent place, and the `for` head is its window**\\n+  (design 275 U3). THE VALUE LIVES ONLY IN ITS\\n+  WINDOW: legal in exactly one position, the DIRECT head of a `for` whose\\n+  receiver is a place rooted in a named binding. `let it = v.iter()` is\\n+  refused, and so is `for x in make_vector().iter()` (\\\"bind the collection\\n+  first\\\" — a temporary has no persistent storage for the window to point\\n+  into), an argument, a field, a `move` operand, a `??` operand, a closure\\n+  return. NOTHING ERASES IT either: not `Optional<It>`, `Vector<It>`,\\n+  `Box<It>`, a bare `T` or an `any Trait` — each refused at the DECLARATION\\n+  that names the type, which is what makes a laundering helper unwritable\\n+  rather than caught. `lends self` is the ONLY origin (a projection, another\\n+  reference parameter and a `&var self` receiver are each refused by name),\\n+  fields are SHARED only (`&var` is refused: an iterator holding `&var\\n+  Collection` could reallocate what another reader is walking), and `Item` is\\n+  owned. Owned fields and a `deinit` work; at the close the struct is\\n+  destroyed and THEN the root charge ends, so a `deinit` may read through the\\n+  reference.\\n+  v1 fences: a borrows ACCESSOR's body is `sync` and its `lend` window never\\n+  spans a suspend (`with_ref`/`with_var_ref` stay the long-window spelling and\\n+  keep the same rule; the `for` window over a BORROWING STRUCT is the one that\\n+  does span one), no borrows function\\n   VALUES or existentials, no trait requirements, and a borrows body cannot\\n   FORWARD another CONDITIONAL place (`lend other.get(k)!` is not expressible —\\n   split the search out and lend your own storage instead). `Set` still gets no\\n@@ -2642,12 +2676,15 @@ dump_tasks()                // every live task's logical backtrace (std.task)\\n   inserted at the TOP of each loop body (a `continue` hits it too) in the task's own\\n   body, in the suspending callees the compiler embeds, and in a suspending `main` —\\n   which means the body becomes SUSPENDING even if you wrote nothing that suspends.\\n-  Four bounds worth knowing: a SYNC callee is NOT instrumented (a compute loop\\n+  Three bounds worth knowing: a SYNC callee is NOT instrumented (a compute loop\\n   inside a never-suspending helper called from a task still starves — move the loop\\n-  into the task or put a `yield_now` in the helper); a `for` over a COLLECTION\\n-  (`for x in v.iter()`) is not instrumented, nor is any loop nested inside one\\n-  (only a range `for` can be state-split — use a `while` over an index); a CLOSURE\\n-  body is not instrumented; std's io loops use the 89-c charge instead. Loops outside task bodies are untouched.\\n - A spawned task may CALL `TcpListener.accept()`, and a **multi-connection\\n@@ -2855,6 +2892,21 @@ dump_tasks()                // every live task's logical backtrace (std.task)\\n   suspends. It is design 44's test-only entry and it used to crash the compiler\\n   there; a suspending body already runs inside an executor, so the diagnostic\\n   names the direct call — which is what embeds.\\n+  **A SUSPENSION INSIDE A `for` OVER A COLLECTION EMBEDS (design 275 U3 /\\n+  SL-317).** The iterator becomes frame state and the loop head re-enters\\n+  through `next()`; any `Iterator` conformer works, the head is evaluated\\n+  once, and `continue` re-enters at `next()`. It was a clean refusal (\\\"use a\\n+  `while` loop over an index\\\") before, so a build that gives that message\\n+  predates U3 — and, since the op budget now charges such a loop's back-edge,\\n+  a `for x in v.iter()` in a MULTI-THREADED task body is refused on `Send`\\n+  (the iterator holds a reference): index there instead.\\n+  **AND THE INLINE `try EXPR catch { … }` SPLITS (SL-215).** A suspending call\\n+  in its catch embeds, and a `break`/`continue` written there reaches the loop\\n+  it names — the transform rewrites the inline form into the block form it\\n+  means. An empty\\ndiff --git a/CLAUDE.md b/CLAUDE.md\\nindex 2b773c95..9c049f92 100644\\n--- a/CLAUDE.md\\n+++ b/CLAUDE.md\\n@@ -207,6 +207,13 @@ objects are built + cached under `.build/rt/` and auto-linked (delete\\n   six, because the plain-call one runs once per family a call comes in: a free\\n   call plus an INSTANCE, a STATIC and a GENERIC-struct method, each grafted from\\n   a node the compiler really produced),\\n+  `windowseam` (design 275 U3: the statement-scoped borrow window is a STRUCTURE,\\n+  not a `for`-loop feature — `tools/test_window_seam.py` fails if `sawc/windows.py`\\n+  names an AST class or any iteration vocabulary in its code, if the record's\\n+  resource slot stops being generic, if the chokepoint's docstring lists a client\\n+  that no longer resolves, or if either the typechecker or codegen half reaches\\n+  past its ONE adapter, so the generic window statement a follow-up specifies adds\\n+  syntax and a binding rather than a second implementation of root accounting),\\n   `stdseed` (SL-327: the design-206 std seed table is keyed by `Method.node_id`,\\n   and a wrong key costs a DIAGNOSTIC rather than a failure, so nothing in the\\n   corpus can see it — `tools/test_std_seed_keying.py` checks the rule directly:\\ndiff --git a/LANGUAGE_SPEC.md b/LANGUAGE_SPEC.md\\nindex 3a0369d5..a86068be 100644\\n--- a/LANGUAGE_SPEC.md\\n+++ b/LANGUAGE_SPEC.md\\n@@ -4789,15 +4789,102 @@ indexing (`a[0]` is not an accessor, so constant distinct indices stay disjoint)\\n \\n Three fences hold in this version:\\n \\n-- A `borrows` body is `sync`. A place window may not span a suspension: the root\\n-  stays borrowed for the whole window, so yielding with one open would let\\n-  another task invalidate it. `with_ref` / `with_var_ref` remain the explicit\\n-  long-window and multi-statement spellings.\\n+- A `borrows` body is `sync`. An accessor's `lend` window may not span a\\n+  suspension, and neither may a `with_ref` / `with_var_ref` body: the root\\n+  stays borrowed for the whole window, and those two spellings record no extent\\n+  a reader could check it against. `with_ref` / `with_var_ref` remain the\\n+  explicit long-window and multi-statement spellings. The `for` window over a\\n+  borrowing struct is the one window that DOES span a suspension — see\\n+  *Borrowing structs* below, and *Suspension and the coroutine transform* for\\n+  what is known about it that is not known about these two.\\n - There are no `borrows` function *values* or existentials. A `borrows` method\\n   cannot be bound to a name or erased behind `any Trait`.\\n - Traits cannot require a `borrows` method. A generic `T: IndexPlace` bound is\\n   not part of this version.\\n \\n+#### Borrowing structs\\n+\\n+**Status: implemented.** A **borrowing struct** is a type that holds a lent\\n+place. It is declared `borrows struct`, one of its fields is a plain shared\\n+reference, and a `borrows` function returns one by value:\\n+\\n+```saw-fragment\\n+public borrows struct VectorIterator<T, A: Allocator = GlobalAllocator> {\\n+    private vector: &Vector<T, A>\\n+    private index: Int\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension Vector<T: Copy, A: Allocator = GlobalAllocator> {\\n+    public func iter(&self) borrows -> VectorIterator<T, A> {\\n+        VectorIterator<T, A>(vector: lends self, index: 0)\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+```\\n+\\n+Four spellings, one fact. The `borrows struct` keyword declares the type's\\n+nature where a reader meets the type; the `&Vector<T, A>` field is the\\n+ordinary reference spelling, and no second modifier restates it; `borrows` on\\n+a value-returning signature says at the declaration that the result borrows\\n+the receiver; and `lends self` at the initializer is the body's proof.\\n+\\n+**The window is the `for` statement.** A `borrows` call returning a borrowing\\n+struct is legal in exactly one position: the direct head of a `for` whose\\n+receiver is a place rooted in a named binding — a local, a parameter, `self`,\\n+or a field path of one. The window opens at the head's evaluation and closes\\n+when the statement ends, on every route out.\\n+\\n+```saw-fragment\\n+for x in v.iter() { total = total + x }   // the window's extent is this statement\\n+let it = v.iter()                          // error: a borrowing struct may not\\n+                                           // be bound by a `let`\\n+for x in make_vector().iter() { }          // error: bind the collection first —\\n+                                           // a temporary has no persistent\\n+                                           // storage for the window to point into\\n+```\\n+\\n+While the window is open the collection is borrowed shared, so the body may\\n+read it and may not grow it, replace it, move it or swap its elements. That is\\n+the same exclusivity error `v.push` already gets inside a `with_ref` window,\\n+and it is what makes iteration safe: before this rule an iterator held a raw\\n+buffer pointer and a length snapshot, so a body that pushed until the buffer\\n+reallocated read freed memory in safe code.\\n+\\n+**Nothing erases it.** A borrowing struct may not instantiate a type parameter\\n+— not `Optional<It>`, `Result<It, E>`, `Vector<It>`, `Box<It>` or a bare `T` —\\n+may not be erased into an `any Trait` existential, and may not be a parameter,\\n+a struct field, a plain return type or an associated type. Each is refused at\\n+the declaration that names the type, so a helper that would forward one cannot\\n+be written rather than being caught at its call. It may conform to `Iterator`,\\n+which is how `for` reaches `next`, and only static dispatch reaches the\\n+conformance.\\n+\\n+**The origin is the receiver.** `lends self` is the only spelling, it requires\\n+a `&self` receiver, and every returning path must initialize every reference\\n+field with it. A projection (`lends self.buffer`), another reference parameter\\n+or a local names a root the call site cannot charge, and each is refused by\\n+name. The rule is why the iterator lends the vector rather than its buffer:\\n+`next()` reads `length` and `buffer` through the reference on every call, so\\n+nothing is snapshotted and there is no stale pointer to go stale.\\n+\\n+**Shared only.** A borrowing struct's reference fields are `&T`. A `&var`\\n+field is refused: an iterator holding `&var Vector<T>` could reallocate the\\n+collection another reader is walking through its own field, and recording the\\n+root would not catch it. Mutating the borrowing struct's own state is\\n+unaffected — `next(&var self)` advances its cursor, which is not mutable\\n+access to the borrowed collection.\\n+\\n+**Item is owned.** A borrowing struct's `Iterator.Item` may be neither a\\n+reference nor another borrowing struct, so the loop variable owns what it is\\n+handed. `Vector`'s `T: Copy` elements satisfy this.\\n+\\n+**Owned fields and `deinit` are supported**, and two things happen at the\\n+window's close in this order: the borrowing struct is destroyed, and then the\\n+root charge ends. The order is what lets a hand-written `deinit` read through\\n+the reference.\\n+\\n+A place is never a value outside a borrowing struct, and a borrowing struct is\\n+never a value outside its window.\\n+\\n #### Standard library accessors\\n \\n `Vector` and `Data` publish their element access as places:\\n@@ -7322,10 +7409,19 @@ Observable rules:\\n     spawned body needs none of that — it points into the task's own frame, which\\n     the box keeps alive. A `threads: N` group refuses a reference parameter\\n     outright, on `Send`.\\n-  - Container-internal borrows (`Vector.with_ref`/`with_var_ref`) keep their\\n-    `sync`-body restriction: unlike a confined stack/frame referent, a container\\n-    borrow projects into shared, reachable storage that a concurrent task could\\n-    reallocate across a suspension, so it may not span one.\\n+  - The `for` WINDOW spans a suspension; the closure and accessor spellings do\\n+    not. A `for x in v.iter()` head opens a tracked window over the collection\\n+    (see *Borrowing structs* under Places), and the window may hold across a\\n+    park because three things are known about it: its ORIGIN is recorded, its\\n+    EXTENT is the statement, and the Law of Exclusivity sees every competing\\n+    safe writer. A `&var` extent live at the head or started inside the body is\\n+    the writer-beside-reader error, a `&` extent composes, a `threads: N` group\\n+    refuses the frame on `Send` before the question arises, and nothing else in\\n+    this task runs while the frame is parked. `Vector.with_ref` /\\n+    `with_var_ref` bodies and `borrows` accessor `lend` windows RETAIN the\\n+    `sync` restriction: a suspension inside one is a compile error, and the\\n+    same argument would lift it, but it wants a consumer sweep and rows of its\\n+    own.\\n - **`deinit` may not suspend** — a `deinit` is always a `sync` context, so a\\n   suspension inside one is a compile error (deterministic destruction).\\n - **Effect polymorphism — generic suspending functions/methods** (design 70,\\n@@ -7571,10 +7667,31 @@ Observable rules:\\n       served\\n   }\\n   ```\\n+- **A suspension inside a `for` over a COLLECTION embeds.** The iterator becomes\\n+  frame state, the loop head re-enters through `next()`, and the split is the\\n+  one a `while let` already had: a spanning collection `for` is rewritten into\\n+  the loop it denotes, so `_split_while` and `_split_if_let` do the work and\\n+  there is no third routine. Any `Iterator` conformer works — an owned iterator\\n+  becomes an ordinary frame field, and a borrowing struct (see *Places*) adds\\n+  the tracked window. The head is evaluated exactly once, `continue` re-enters\\n+  at `next()`, and `break` leaves as it always did.\\n+\\n+  ```saw-fragment\\n+  func open_store(names: &Vector<Path>, st: &var Store) -> Result<Int, IoError> {\\n+      for name in names.iter() {\\n+          let raw = try read_file(name)      // suspends, inside the loop\\n+          put(&var st, name, raw)\\n+      }\\n+      st.len()\\n+  }\\n+  ```\\n+- **The INLINE `try EXPR catch { … }` splits too.** A suspending call in its\\n+  catch block embeds, and a `break` or `continue` written there reaches the\\n+  loop it names. The transform rewrites the inline form into the block form it\\n+  means, so both spellings take the same CFG split.\\n - **Not yet supported** (rejected with a diagnostic anchored at the user's source\\n   line, not miscompiled): a\\n-  suspension-spanning `if let`/`guard let` with a *tuple pattern*; a suspension inside a `for`\\n-  over a non-range iterable; a value-producing `break` out of a suspension-spanning\\n+  suspension-spanning `if let`/`guard let` with a *tuple pattern*; a value-producing `break` out of a suspension-spanning\\n   loop; a chained assignment through MORE THAN ONE optional hop whose RHS\\n   suspends (`a?.b?.c = stream.read()` — the single-hop form works; bind the inner\\n   optional with `if let` first); and a suspending `try { … } catch { … }` block\\n@@ -7744,17 +7861,20 @@ anti-suspension boundary, so it is `sync`) plus `wake_reason(&self) sync -> Int`\\n \\n   The compute check goes at the TOP of each loop body (so a `continue` reaches it\\n   too) in the task's own body, in the suspending callees the compiler embeds into\\n-  it, and in a suspending `main`. Three bounds, each deliberate:\\n   - a SYNC callee is not instrumented, so a compute loop inside a never-suspending\\n     helper called from a task stays unpreempted. Put the loop in the task, or give\\n     the helper a `yield_now` (which makes it suspending);\\n-  - a `for` over a COLLECTION (`for x in v.iter()`) is not instrumented, and\\n-    neither is any loop nested inside one — only a range `for` can be state-split.\\n-    Write it as a `while` over an index if the loop is long enough to matter;\\n   - a CLOSURE body is not instrumented (a closure is not driven, so a yield there\\n     would do nothing);\\n   - std's own io loops keep the 89-c charge rather than carrying both.\\n \\n+  A `for` over a COLLECTION used to be a fourth bound, and it went with its\\n+  reason: it was exempt because only a range `for` could be state-split, so\\n+  instrumenting one would have turned working programs into compile errors.\\n+  Both `for` shapes split now, so both are charged, and so is every loop nested\\n+  inside either.\\n+\\n   Cost: a wrapping decrement and a branch per iteration, plus the larger effect\\n   that the loop it guards joins the frame's state machine (its variables become\\n   frame fields). Measured at 1.53x on a maximally tight arithmetic loop (200M\\ndiff --git a/README.md b/README.md\\nindex 62e62f3f..54fb8cac 100644\\n--- a/README.md\\n+++ b/README.md\\n@@ -553,6 +553,39 @@ extension Document {\\n }\\n ```\\n \\n+A type can hold a lent place too. `borrows struct` declares one, and its value\\n+lives for exactly one `for` statement — which is how iteration is safe:\\n+\\n+```saw-error\\n+// error-contains: `v` cannot be accessed by reference here\\n+func main() {\\n+    var v: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    var seen = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() {\\n+        seen = seen + x\\n+        let _ = v.push(9)\\n+    }\\n+    print(seen)\\n+}\\n+// error: exclusive access violation: `v` cannot be accessed by reference here\\n+//        — the window opened at line 5 borrows `v` for the whole of the `for`\\n+//        statement that iterates it\\n+```\\n+\\n+`Vector.iter()` hands back a `VectorIterator` holding `&Vector<T, A>`, so the\\n+loop borrows the collection for its whole run and the body may read it but not\\n+grow it, replace it, move it or swap its elements. The iterator reads the\\n+vector's length and buffer through the reference on every step, so a growth\\n+that reallocated the buffer cannot leave it pointing at freed storage. The\\n+borrow is tracked by the ordinary Law of Exclusivity, not by a lifetime\\n+annotation: the value cannot be bound to a name, stored in a field, passed as\\n+an argument or wrapped in a generic, so there is one place it can be and the\\n+compiler knows where that is.\\n+\\n+Such a loop may suspend. The iterator becomes part of the task's frame and the\\n+loop head re-enters through `next()`, so a `for` over a collection can await\\n+I/O in its body like any other statement.\\n+\\n A method can also END its receiver. `consumes` says so at the declaration, and\\n `move` says so at the call:\\n \\ndiff --git a/designs/todo.md b/designs/todo.md\\nindex 7d35e1a1..5ed90802 100644\\n--- a/designs/todo.md\\n+++ b/designs/todo.md\\n@@ -38,11 +38,12 @@ is scheduled and in what order is the whole of what they say.\\n - Design 259 — the self-hosted parser (designs/259-selfhost-parser.md; QUEUED Sep 1 by the user, §3 ruling batch fully RULED same day incl. Four units in order U4 → U1 → U2 → U3: the effect answer at the source, the frame LEDGER + `corodiscovery` gate lane, TOTALITY (no silent decline; SL-287, SL-316, K33–K37), then coverage SL-215 + SL-317 as epic units — NOT point fixes; sawtracker's build waits for the finished foundation (user ruling). Dispatches right after SL-274.p1 lands, AHEAD of the small-fixes batch (user re-sequenced Sep 18); one Opus agent per unit, serially\\n+  - **U3 BUILT Sep 22 (branch `sl318u3`, awaiting the lead's validation + patch).** COVERAGE: the two shapes the table had no SPLIT for, with SL-321 landing inside as the BORROWING-STRUCT model. THE STATEMENT-SCOPED WINDOW is the structure and `for` is its first client, not a for-loop feature: `sawc/windows.py` holds ONE record (root path, mode, origin, extent statement, and a GENERIC resource slot — never an `__iter` field nor an `Iterator`-shaped payload) opened and closed through ONE chokepoint whose docstring names its clients, and the chokepoint performs the root charge, the exclusivity conflicts, the move/reassign/take/swap refusals and the close-on-every-route accounting BY RESOLVED CONTROL-FLOW TARGET (`windows_left_by` takes the extents the edge's target is still inside, so `continue` keeps this loop's window, `break` closes it, an inner loop's jump closes nothing of the outer, and `return`/error/cancellation close every crossed scope — no `Continue` special case). The four access sites that read the charge are the four design 189's task-capture extent already had. `ForLoop` appears in the chokepoint only through the adapter (`typechecker/borrowing.py::open_for_window` / `close_for_window`), and the record is a PLAIN CLASS rather than a dataclass because `substitute_ast_types` recurses into every dataclass value and a record whose `extent` points back at its own statement is a cycle. THE BORROWING STRUCT (`typechecker/borrowing.py`): `borrows struct` in design 130's `unsafe struct` position, a plain SHARED `&T` field (a `&var` one is refused with the shared-window rule named), `borrows -> S` on a VALUE-returning producer — separated from a design-141 lend by the DECLARED return type and the absence of a `lend`, which is a question `place_transform` can answer before type checking — and `lends self` as the ONLY origin, requiring a `&self` receiver and recorded on the declaration as `Method.borrow_origin`, a DECLARED annotation field so it rides monomorphization and travels with an import. `lends` is a CONTEXTUAL keyword (the `import`/`module` precedent), so neither lexer's table changes and `lexdiff` is untouched. THE FENCE is one question asked at the one line every expression's type is stamped, which is what makes the position list a matrix of TESTS rather than of checks; the IMPOSSIBLE SPELLINGS (a parameter, a field, a plain return, a type argument, an existential, an `Item`) are refused at the DECLARATION that names the type, so a laundering helper is unwritable rather than caught. `Vector`'s two iterators are the four-line shape holding `&Vector<T, A>` and reading length and buffer through it on every `next()` — SL-321's repro flips from `seen = 1025` to a compile error at the push. SL-317: a spanning collection `for` is REWRITTEN into the `while let` it denotes, so `_split_while` + `_split_if_let` are its split and `coro_shapes.SPLIT_LIMITS` is empty; the op budget charges its back-edge with every other loop's. SL-215: the INLINE `try EXPR catch { … }` becomes `try { try EXPR } catch { … }` wherever the state machine has to express its catch, so design 196 unit 3's split does the work — its XFAIL pin flips, and the SAME slot's silent face goes with it (`_mark_ob_block` never listed `TryExpr`, so a `catch { break }` in a spanning loop kept a raw `break` that left the resume DISPATCH loop: the break was lost and a `continue` spun forever, at exit 0). Each rewrite takes a FRESH error binding, because the block form's caught error travels in a frame field NAMED by the binding and two normalized catches in one body shared one. Conformance rows K132-K149, over all four axes. TWO COMPILER FIXES the fixtures earned: codegen's SYNC lowering never destroyed the window's resource (one routine for any client, pushed around the loop and before the `loop_stack` entry that records the unwind depth — invisible before U3 because every previous iterator was pointer-plus-Ints and owed no cleanup), and the fresh error binding above. CORPUS COST, listed not absorbed: a head over a TEMPORARY receiver is the fence's refusal, so `coro_head_move_with_early_exit_drops_once.saw`'s cell keeps its subject through an OWNED iterator (`String.bytes()`); a borrowed iterator held across a suspension meets the Send-on-frames rule and the U3 ruling is that frame address stability is no argument to relax it, so `taskgroup_send_containers.saw`'s multi-threaded cell indexes instead — and `for x in v.iter()` in a `threads: N` task body is a compile error now, which K146 records as user-visible; `coro_transform_error_anchors_in_defining_module.saw`'s vehicle was the SL-317 refusal and rides the closure-body one. FILED. SL-338 and SL-339 were filed as pre-existing and CLOSED IN U3 by the lead's ruling (a brief never xfails breakage it makes reachable — the four XFAIL markers are gone and every moved-out cell is back in its home file): **SL-338 CLOSED** — `_split_if`, `_split_while` and `_split_match` route their heads through `_hoist_head_and_relinquish` instead of refusing, the `while` one into the HEADER block so the clear lands once per EVALUATION (SL-226 r1); the sibling sweep found `match` (refused, and it COMPILED on main) and cleared `if let`/`guard let`/`while let`/range-`for`, which already relinquished. **SL-339 CLOSED** — `_trailing_forgets` is the one funnel a leaf statement's trailing drop-flag clears go through, and `_emit_try_landing` + `_emit_try_propagate` re-emit what it recorded on the edge they own, so the claim is given up on BOTH edges out of the call; six shapes double-freed on main (inline and block spellings, bare/`let`/assignment statements, the propagate edge and `return try f(move r)`) and all six are regression cells now. SL-340 (`Mutex(value: move v)` FREES a heap-owning payload at construction; K141's accept half is XFAIL on it and its refusal half passes), SL-341 (sawtracker's read-only build dies in the LLVM machine scheduler under the default pipeline, `-O0 -c` clean on both — the last thing between the epic and its acceptance criterion). A container that owns a block and has no row is an INVARIANT failure naming the AST class. TWO TOTALITY CHECKS beside it: (a) after the site census, a re-walk with an INDEPENDENT suspension predicate requires a recorded decision for every suspension position, and asks the table about every container it passes — so a NEW AST shape fails coverage until the table classifies it; (b) after every resume body is generated, a walk asserts that no `frame_boundary` callee survives as a plain call, naming the frame and the callee — `closure_calls_permitted`'s question made total and run for every frame, which is the runtime-facing half. `declined` is GONE from both ledger tables: a generic template is `template` (64 of U1's 67 rows — it owed no frame in the first place), a callee that owes one and has none is `refused`, and a site the transform will not lower is `refuse(<why>)`. Conformance rows K118-K121; the `corototality` battery lane (`tools/test_coro_shapes.py`, six checks — three compare the table against `ast_walk`'s two enumerations and the transform's consumers, three INJECT the failure and require the named ICE, each beside an uninjected control). TWO USER RULINGS folded in Sep 20 before submission: **SL-323** — `existential_dispatch` MOVES OUT of `frame_boundary` into the conservative half beside `closure_call`, so the boundary is `cooperative | blocking | test_suspend` and a body whose only cause is a dispatch is not framed (design 223's refusal AT the dispatch is what keeps it sound — it fires before any framing question, so every dispatch that reaches one has all-sync implementations); `CONSERVATIVE_CAUSES` states the membership once, `conservative_only` joins the derivations beside `closure_only`, the K115 pin flips XPASS and its INDEX row is rewritten to the ruled answer, spec + skill updated. **SL-327** — REPRODUCED and fixed at the KEYING, no change to settling. The symptom is cache-state dependent, which is why it looked environmental: a FRESH std build agrees with itself, and five of eight cached blobs on this machine held a seed table keyed to an AST that was not the one stored beside them (`Command.output` keyed 20761 in the table and 62190 in both the AST and the namespace symbol), every one written by a process whose id bound was an order of magnitude past a fresh std build's — the state `_prepared_builtins`' own docstring says the pair must not be stored from. TWO keying fixes: `stdcache.store` refuses to publish an inconsistent pair, and `sawc._rekeyed_std_seed` re-keys a restored table onto the AST the compile actually holds (by `(owner, method)`, the granularity every other std census already uses), so blobs already on disk are harmless. Proven against the real bad blob: 18 dangling keys -> 0, and the namespace symbol's id seeded after and not before. `examples/errors/thread_body_reports_both_violations.saw` is the standing test, and it FLAPPED before the fix — passing on a freshly built cache and failing on a restored one, minutes apart in one tree. **Design 275 §3 RULING 2 built at the lead's validation:** the two call shapes design 44's by-value embedding cannot serve are rows in the shape table (`coro_shapes.PENDING_SL322`) and each refusal NAMES SL-322 — the K37 dispatch hint keeps DF-223b and adds the pending note, and the suspending-recursion message adds it too AND moved its anchor to the RECURSIVE FUNCTION's declaration (the note SL-280's landing asked for; it used to point at whichever root seeded the cycle walk, e.g. `func main`). Both messages render the note from the one row, both pins assert `SL-322`, the recursion pin pins the anchor line, and the `corototality` lane fails if the row, the raiser and the pin stop agreeing. R5 states the reference-in-a-return-position rule once, in the parser's return-clause funnel, leaving U3 only its field clause. R6 deletes the `&var self` fence on `#lend_var` and re-keys the two-specialization split from the receiver spelling onto the DECLARED MODE, with eligibility computed on the shared copy AFTER the fold — so `Data.[]`'s copy-on-write gate costs an ordinary read nothing. R4 separates the LENT VALUE's mode from the RECEIVER's required access: an accessor is SHARED-ELIGIBLE iff its shared specialization passes the ordinary receiver-permission rules, recorded by `_self_borrow_is_exclusive`'s own five entry points (which now RECORD as well as refuse) onto a declared annotation field and read at every call site by the one window funnel — so `let v` plus `v[0]` still compiles under R2, while an accessor that mutates `self` outside the place it lends is refused on a `let` root by name and two of its read-only windows on one `var` root conflict. R5 states the reference-in-a-return-position rule once, in the parser's return-clause funnel, leaving U3 only its field clause. R6 deletes the `&var self` fence on `#lend_var` and re-keys the two-specialization split from the receiver spelling onto the DECLARED MODE, with eligibility computed on the shared copy AFTER the fold — so `Data.[]`'s copy-on-write gate costs an ordinary read nothing. R7 gives a FORWARDED lend the enclosing specialization's mode, spending design 179's one stated v1 limit (a nested CoW read no longer copies) with two refusals falling out of the same fact. `place_uses._window_call` now names its seven author-visible entries and reads both declaration facts at every one; `--emit-docs` carries the mode and the eligibility. Corpus: 105 declarations (sawc/std 12, libs 3, examples 90), blade and libs/*/tests unchanged. Conformance rows K123-K130. Design 102 item 2 made a cancel wake an io-parked task; a deadline gives that park a second way to end, and the row asserts that adding one did not cost the other AND that the two stay distinguishable — a task cancelled mid-park must not report `TimedOut`, or a shutdown would read exactly like an idle connection. K101-K105 are five faces of ONE mechanism, and this row is the mechanism itself: a DECISION re-derived at every consumer, policed by reading. Twenty-odd predicates in `coro_transform.py` each asked the raw inputs — `node.suspends`, `mangled_symbol`, `resolved_symbol`, `module_free_call`, a callee node's written `name`, `_suspending_methods_set` / `_own_suspending_methods_set`, the entry and imported body tables, the instantiation registry, the method tables — and every finding of the week of Sep 14 was two of those readings disagreeing: SL-280's six keying sites, SL-274's review finding two more the funnel had not reached, SL-306's classifier-versus-closure-walk split, and its own review finding a fourth reader of the broad bit. `sawc/coro_ledger.py::FrameLedger` is the one authority now: a worklist runs to stabilization BEFORE any body is lowered, the ledger FREEZES, and `callee_frame_key` / `method_frame_key` survive only as its private composers — so a new consumer cannot ask a raw input even by accident, which is what the `corodiscovery` battery lane (`tools/test_coro_discovery.py`) enforces by parsing the module. THE FILE IS THE ADVERSARIAL SET from the SL-274 and SL-306 reviews, promoted from lead probes to a standing gate: three modules declaring one suspending `worker` (entry, an inline submodule, a dependency) answering +1 / +100 / +1000; a module `s_t` beside the module `s.t` (K110's `module_tag` collision, at the frame key); a suspending generic instantiation reached through a NON-generic helper (the depth the seed walk alone does not reach); a conservative-only closure caller, whose frames must be ABSENT; an imported module-PRIVATE helper reached by REFERENCE (the SL-280 wedge's own shape); and an imported generic whose mono clone is spliced into the entry module and stays keyed to its HOME module. Every cell asserts the RUN OUTPUT and the framed-versus-unframed IR fact, because each program prints the same number either way — `__saw_drive_steps` separates a surviving suspension (1) from a dropped one (0) and the `__Frame_` grep names the frames, which is the check the lead did by hand in three consecutive reviews |\\n | K117 | a ledger MISS at a consumer is an INVARIANT FAILURE naming the key — never permission to emit a plain call | `coro_imported_private_helper_suspends.saw` + `coro_imported_private_helper_parks_on_io.saw` + `K116_one_frame_ledger_answers_every_discovery_question.saw` + `errors/coro_suspending_recursion.saw` | design 275 U1, and the half that decides what a gap COSTS rather than whether one exists. Before the ledger, a consumer whose key matched nothing simply answered \\\"does not suspend\\\" — the same answer a genuinely sync callee gets — so a discovery gap and a correct negative were indistinguishable, and K102's face of it was a park lowered through codegen's out-of-frame fallback, which registers the fd and then parks the cooperative executor's OWN thread: a whole process stopped on one idle socket, exit code 0, nothing said. The ledger's read API raises an ICE breadcrumb naming the key instead, so the failure is a located compiler bug rather than a wedged program — at `free_call_frame`, which is the read the five call-site classifiers ask, not at a strict lookup nothing calls (codex's SL-318.p4 r1 review found the first draft's invariant decorative for exactly that reason: the decision tables were populated only under `--emit-frame-ledger`, and absence still carried the negative on the lowering path). The DECISION is three-valued now — `framed` / `no-frame-owed` / `declined(reason)` — so a decided negative and a gap are different facts, and `tools/test_coro_discovery.py` proves the gap reaches the consumer by DROPPING one recorded decision and requiring the compiler to name the dropped key. NOTE THE BOUNDARY U1 DELIBERATELY DOES NOT CROSS: a site the transform DECLINES today — a callee or closure body the walk skipped and lowered as a plain call (SL-287, SL-316) — is RECORDED in the ledger's site table with outcome `declined(reason)` and lowered exactly as before, because U1 is behaviour-preserving by construction. Turning those declines into refusals is U2's flip and owes U2's consumer sweep; `--emit-frame-ledger` dumps the list, which is what makes the worklist auditable instead of remembered. **U2 SPENT IT:** `declined` is gone from both tables — a callee that owes a frame and has none is `refused` and a call to it is a compile error (K119), a generic template is `template` (it owed no frame in the first place, which is 64 of the 67 rows), and a site the transform will not lower is `refuse(<why>)` carrying the shape table's message |\\n | K122 | the two call shapes design 44's by-value embedding cannot serve are REFUSED, and each refusal NAMES the design it waits on | `K37_existential_dispatch_suspending_impl_refused.saw` + `errors/coro_suspending_recursion.saw` + `tools/test_coro_shapes.py` (the `corototality` lane) | design 275 §3 ruling 2 (user, Sep 18), built in U2. An `any Trait` dispatch of a suspending conformance body and a suspending-call CYCLE are refused for one structural reason — a frame is a compile-time identity embedded BY VALUE in its caller's, and neither has one (a vtable word where the identity would be; no finite embedded size at all) — and they need one fix between them: a heap-allocated frame with an indirect resume, carried by SL-322, so the two land together and neither alone. That makes both refusals PENDING rather than permanent, and the ruling's words are \\\"the refusal naming it\\\": `coro_shapes.PENDING_SL322` is the row, `pending_note` the one spelling both messages render, and the lane checks that each raiser renders it from the row (not from a literal that can drift) while each pin asserts the citation literally. THE RECURSION REFUSAL ALSO MOVED ITS ANCHOR — the note SL-280's landing asked for. The guarantee designs 96/101/104 state is POSITIONAL, and every violation of it reached the corpus the same way: a walk enumerated the positions it knew about and a new shape joined the language without joining the enumeration — DF-193a's `try { } catch { }` (invisible to every hoist), DF-224a's `match` scrutinee (neither embedded nor refused, spinning at 100% CPU), SL-326's ANF-hoisted generic call and SL-330's module-qualified one (hoisted, never promoted, then blamed on a `sync` region the author never wrote). `sawc/coro_shapes.py` is the ONE table now: `CONTAINERS` says SPLIT (and under which stamp) or REFUSE per container, `HEADS` says HOIST per head slot, `STATEMENTS` and `EXPRESSIONS` carry the rest, and `refusal_of` renders one message per REFUSE row — which is what collapses the 21 scattered refusal texts design 275's census counted. The file is the BEHAVIOURAL half: seven SPLIT containers, six HOIST heads and four design-224 expression positions, each asserting `__saw_drive_steps` rather than the value, because a body prints the same number whether or not its suspension survived. THE FAILURE MODE, stated as a rule rather than as a keying fix — SL-280's was the third instance of it, after design 96's and SL-208's. When the closure walk declines an edge the callee subtree stays sync and its park lowers through codegen's out-of-frame fallback, which is CORRECT for a genuinely sync caller with no executor to hand back to and, reached from inside a task, registers the fd with a null token and then BLOCKS THE COOPERATIVE EXECUTOR'S OWN OS THREAD: every other task stops, including the timer-bounded ones (measured on a server whose accept loop had its own one-second deadline — not one tick fired for the whole block), and with an untimed park the process is stopped until that single fd happens to become ready. The non-io face is the same hole with a quieter symptom: a declined `yield_now()` is dropped and `__saw_drive_steps` reports 0 where 1 was owed. U1 recorded the decision and the reason; `FrameLedger.unbuildable_callee` is the read that spends it, and its ONE consumer is the ONE rejector, so the refusal is anchored at the call the author wrote and carries the ledger's own words for why. Same program shape, one file boundary apart, two outcomes — and the silent one is the documented-impossible third. The entry-module twin was refused because the closure's suspension reaches its enclosing function's effect node, so a frame IS built and the driven caller's own body scan (`_reject_buried_suspend_call`) refuses it; the IMPORTED twin was not, because the enclosing helper carries only the conservative closure-call cause, no frame is built for it, and NO FRAME BUILDER EVER SCANS ITS BODY. It compiled and printed 0 drive steps where two were owed, and the io face of it is the dangerous one (a `stream.read()` in that closure body lowers through the out-of-frame park and stops the executor's thread rather than losing a cede). The question is asked of the CLOSURE now, not of its caller, so the answer no longer depends on who happens to reach it — `_refuse_undriven_suspending_closures` runs after every frame is built, so a closure in a DRIVEN body keeps the message that names its driven function and this one speaks only for the bodies nothing else will ever look at. The corpus cost is listed rather than absorbed: `vector_closure_suspending_transform.saw` CHANGED OUTCOME (design 216's `(&T)`-element claim is covered by `vector_closures_over_nocopy_elements.saw`, which never suspended), and `examples/modules/sl306mod`'s three unused `each_yield_*` helpers moved to a fixture module of their own, `sl316closure`, which exists to be refused. **THE NO-ROOT CELL** (codex, SL-318.p6 r1 P1) is the last one and it closed BOTH sides of the file boundary at once: the refusal lived behind frame construction, so a program with no driven root, no spawn and a non-suspending `main` never entered the transform at all and neither twin was refused — the imported fixture compiled and printed `2`, and so did the byte-equivalent entry-module spelling, while adding one unrelated `__saw_drive_steps` call to the same `main` refused both. Whether an unsupported closure is rejected cannot depend on an unrelated drive site, so the transform's ENTRY CONDITION gained its second half (`_has_suspending_closure_body`, `frame_boundary` over the closure nodes — the same derivation `closure_owns_suspension` reads, so the gate and the walk cannot disagree) and `_build_frame_ledger` answers a ROOTLESS ledger instead of `None`: the body tables and the cause set, no frames, no sites, which is all a question about a BODY needs. ONE walk, two mutually exclusive entry points — after every frame is built when there is a root, immediately when there is not |\\n | K121 | no `frame_boundary` call survives as a PLAIN CALL in any generated resume body — the runtime-facing half of totality | `tools/test_coro_shapes.py` (the `corototality` lane, check 6) + `K118_every_shape_splits_hoists_or_refuses.saw` + `coro_qualified_generic_call_embeds.saw` + `coro_entry_template_chain_names_a_suspending_callee.saw` | design 275 U2, codex's review point 3 on the brief. The discovery gate (`corodiscovery`) and the site-coverage check are both STATIC: they police the decisions discovery records and the raw inputs a consumer may read, and neither can prove the LOWERING then carried every decision out. This can, and it asks at the only place where a miss still costs something. It is `closure_calls_permitted`'s question made TOTAL: that flag lets a synthesized frame method be judged by `frame_boundary` rather than `might_suspend`, which is the right question and is asked only of the bodies the sync checker happens to visit, only after the post-transform re-typecheck, and with a diagnostic that blames the author's `sync` region for a frame the transform declined to build — which is exactly the message SL-326 and SL-330 produced. THE ORACLE IS THE SYNC TWIN: every cell runs the same body\\n+// without a suspension, and the two answers must agree.\\n+//\\n+// THE AXES this file walks, one cell per combination it names:\\n+//\\n+//   ITERATOR   Vector.iter, Vector.enumerated, a USER borrowing iterator, a\\n+//              RECORD-LESS user iterator (one that owns its source and opens\\n+//              NO window — the split admits any `Iterator` conformer, and the\\n+//              borrowing struct is what the std iterators ADD), and Map.keys\\n+//              (an owned copy — the control that shows the split is not about\\n+//              borrowing at all).\\n+//   SUSPENSION in the BODY. parent, and NESTED inside a `while` and inside another\\n+//              `for`.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// iter sync 12\\n+// iter driven 12\\n+// enumerated sync 15\\n+// enumerated driven 15\\n+// user sync 12\\n+// user driven 12\\n+// owned sync 12\\n+// owned driven 12\\n+// map sync 6\\n+// map driven 6\\n+// spawned 12\\n+// embedded in spawned 24\\n+// nested in while 24\\n+// nested in for 36\\n+\\n+import std.task.{yield_now}\\n+\\n+func step(x: Int) -> Int {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    x * 2\\n+}\\n+\\n+func step_sync(x: Int) -> Int { x * 2 }\\n+\\n+// ---- a USER borrowing iterator over a user collection ----------------------\\n+\\n+struct Bag {\\n+    private items: Vector<Int>\\n+}\\n+extension Bag: NoCopy {}\\n+\\n+borrows struct BagIterator {\\n+    private bag: &Bag\\n+    private index: Int\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension Bag {\\n+    public func iter(&self) borrows -> BagIterator {\\n+        BagIterator(bag: lends self, index: 0)\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension BagIterator: Iterator {\\n+    type Item = Int\\n+    public func next(&var self) -> Int? {\\n+        if self.index < self.bag.items.len() {\\n+            let i = self.index\\n+            self.index = self.index + 1\\n+            self.bag.items.get(i)\\n+        } else {\\n+            None\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- a RECORD-LESS user iterator: it OWNS its source, so no window opens ---\\n+\\n+struct Counter {\\n+    private next_value: Int,\\n+    private last: Int\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension Counter: Iterator {\\n+    type Item = Int\\n+    public func next(&var self) -> Int? {\\n+        if self.next_value > self.last {\\n+            None\\n+        } else {\\n+            let v = self.next_value\\n+            self.next_value = self.next_value + 1\\n+            v\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- the cells -------------------------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+func iter_sync(v: &Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() { total = total + step_sync(x) }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func iter_driven(v: &Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() { total = total + step(x) }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func enumerated_sync(v: &Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for pair in v.enumerated() { total = total + pair.0 + step_sync(pair.1) }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func enumerated_driven(v: &Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for pair in v.enumerated() { total = total + pair.0 + step(pair.1) }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func user_sync(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in b.iter() { total = total + step_sync(x) }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func user_driven(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in b.iter() { total = total + step(x) }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func owned_sync() -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in Counter(next_value: 1, last: 3) { total = total + step_sync(x) }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func owned_driven() -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in Counter(next_value: 1, last: 3) { total = total + step(x) }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func b/examples/conformance/K135_window_conflicts_that_compose.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,185 @@\\n+// K135 (design 275 U3): THE OTHER HALF OF THE CONFLICT AXIS — what COMPOSES.\\n+//\\n+// A `for` window is SHARED, so it excludes writers and nothing else. The rows\\n+// here are the ones a rule that merely \\\"locked the collection\\\" would have\\n+// broken, and each is measured rather than asserted:\\n+//\\n+//   * an ordinary READ of the root inside the body (`v.len()`)\\n+//   * a read of a DISJOINT sibling of the window's root path (`st.count`,\\n+//     where the window holds `st.patches`)\\n+//   * a SECOND `for` over the same root, nested inside the first — two shared\\n+//     windows on one root\\n+//   * a `&` extent live at the head (a design-189 SHARED task capture)\\n+//   * a `&var` extent on a DISJOINT root beside the window\\n+//\\n+// …and the ROOT AXIS, which is about where the referent LIVES rather than who\\n+// competes for it: a frame-local, a `&` parameter, a `&var` parameter of a\\n+// driven callee, an 21\\n+// disjoint sibling 27\\n+// nested over one root 36\\n+// shared extent composes 6\\n+// disjoint exclusive extent 6\\n+// root frame-local 12\\n+// root shared param 12\\n+// root var param 12\\n+// root enclosing frame field 12\\n+// root receiver field 12\\n+// root generic instantiation 12\\n+\\n+import std.task.{yield_now}\\n+\\n+func step(x: Int) -> Int {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    x * 2\\n+}\\n+\\n+struct Store {\\n+    patches: Vector<Int>,\\n+    count: Int\\n+}\\n+extension Store: NoCopy {}\\n+\\n+struct Holder {\\n+    inner: Vector<Int>\\n+}\\n+extension Holder: NoCopy {}\\n+\\n+struct Wrapper<T> {\\n+    held: Vector<T>\\n+}\\n+extension Wrapper<T>: NoCopy {}\\n+\\n+// ---- conflicts that compose -----------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+func read_the_root(v: &Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() {\\n+        total = total + step(x) + v.len()   // a READ of the root, inside\\n+    }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func disjoint_sibling(st: &var Store) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in st.patches.iter() {\\n+        total = total + x + st.count        // a DISJOINT sibling of the root\\n+    }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func nested_over_one_root(v: &Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for a in v.iter() {\\n+        for b in v.iter() {                 // two SHARED windows on one root\\n+            total = total + a * b\\n+        }\\n+        yield_now()\\n+    }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func observe(v: &Vector<Int>) -> Int { v.len() }\\n+func bump(n: &var Int) -> Int { n = n + 1  n }\\n+\\n+func shared_extent_composes(v: Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var owned = move v\\n+    var group = TaskGroup()\\n+    let h = group.spawn(observe(&owned))    // a SHARED extent live at the head\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in owned.iter() { total = total + x }\\n+    let _ = h.join()\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func disjoint_exclusive_extent(v: Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var owned = move v\\n+    var other = 0\\n+    var group = TaskGroup()\\n+    let h = group.spawn(bump(&var other))   // a `&var` extent on a DISJOINT root\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in owned.iter() { total = total + x }\\n+    let _ = h.join()\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- the ROOT axis ---------------------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+func root_frame_local() -> Int {\\n+    var v: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() { total = total + step(x) }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func root_shared_param(v: &Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() { total = total + step(x) }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func root_var_param(v: &var Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() { total = total + step(x) }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+// The ENCLOSING FRAME's field: `self.inner` is `&self.<field>` into the parent\\n+// frame, which does not move (design 134's
