WINDOW.\\n+//\\n+// The extent is LEXICAL, so every route out of the `for` ends the charge —\\n+// and each row PROVES it by writing the root at the first reachable point\\n+// after the exit, which the window would have refused. THE ORACLE IS THE SYNC\\n+// TWIN throughout: each cell runs driven and sync and the two answers agree.\\n+//\\n+// THE SEVEN ROUTES of part (i):\\n+//   1 exhaustion              5 an error propagated by `try` (proof in the\\n+//   2 `break`, with and         catch, block and INLINE forms alike)\\n+//     without a value         6 an error CAUGHT inside the body that\\n+//   3 `continue` TARGETING      CONTINUES the loop — the window STAYS live\\n+//     THIS loop: NOT an       7 exit under cancellation (K138)\\n+//     exit\\n+//   4 `return` from the body — the writable-again proof sits in the CALLER\\n+//\\n+// …plus the EMPTY loop (the head still evaluates, zero iterations, the\\n+// iterator still drops once) and BREAK-THEN-MUTATE, the release-timing cell.\\n+//\\n+// B2 — A WINDOW CLOSES BY CONTROL-FLOW TARGET, not by the spelling of the\\n+// exit:\\n+//   * `continue` targeting THIS loop keeps its window (row 3);\\n+//   * `break`/`continue` targeting an INNER loop closes nothing of the outer;\\n+//   * a window NESTED inside an outer loop closes when control continues that\\n+//     outer loop — so the next outer pass opens a fresh one.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// exhaustion sync 6 4\\n+// exhaustion driven 6 4\\n+// break plain sync 1 4\\n+// break plain driven 1 4\\n+// break with value sync 3 4\\n+// continue keeps the window sync 4 4\\n+// continue keeps the window driven 4 4\\n+// return from the body 1 4\\n+// error propagated 2 4\\n+// error caught and continued 5 4\\n+// empty loop 0 1\\n+// break then mutate 1 5\\n+// inner loop jump closes nothing 12\\n+// outer continue closes the inner window 12\\n+\\n+import std.task.{yield_now}\\n+\\n+struct Bad { why: String }\\n+extension Bad: Error {\\n+    func format(&self, into: &var StringBuilder) { try! into.append(self.why) }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func slow(x: Int) -> Int {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    x\\n+}\\n+\\n+func fails(x: Int) -> Result<Int, Bad> {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    if x > 1 { return Bad(why: \\\"stop\\\") }\\n+    x\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- 1. exhaustion ---------------------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+func exhaustion_sync(v: &var Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() { total = total + x }\\n+    let _ = v.push(9)                 // writable again, right after the statement\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func exhaustion_driven(v: &var Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() { total = total + slow(x) }\\n+    let _ = v.push(9)\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- 2. break, with and without a value ------------------------------------\\n+\\n+func break_plain_sync(v: &var Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() { total = total + x  break }\\n+    let _ = v.push(9)\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func break_plain_driven(v: &var Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() { total = total + slow(x)  break }\\n+    let _ = v.push(9)\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+// SL-222's discarded-body rule is unchanged: the loop's value arrives through\\n+// `break <value>`, and the `for` types `T?`.\\n+func break_value_sync(v: &var Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    let got = for x in v.iter() { break x + 2 }\\n+    let _ = v.push(9)\\n+    got ?? 0\\n+}\\n+\\n+// THERE IS NO DRIVEN TWIN of this cell, and the bound is not U3's. Design 127\\n+// charges every loop back-edge in a driven body, so a `for` in a DRIVEN body\\n+// has a suspending body whatever the author wrote — and a VALUE-position loop\\n+// whose body suspends is a standing refusal (\\\"a suspension-spanning loop does\\n+// not support `break <value>`\\\"). The RANGE `for` answers identically on main,\\n+// which is the point: SL-317 charged the collection `for`'s back-edge with\\n+// every other loop's, so the two `for` shapes now share this bound as they\\n+// share the split.\\n+\\n+// ---- 3. `continue` TARGETING THIS LOOP is NOT an exit -----------------------\\n+\\n+func continue_sync(v: &var Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() {\\n+        if x == 2 { continue }        // the window and the charge stay live\\n+        total = total + x\\n+    }\\n+    let _ = v.push(9)\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func continue_driven(v: &var Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() {\\n+        if x == 2 { continue }\\n+        total = total + slow(x)\\n+    }\\n+    let _ = v.push(9)\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- 4. `return` from the body: the proof is in the CALLER ------------------\\n+\\n+func return_from_body(v: &Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    for x in v.iter() { return x }\\n+    0\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- 5. an error PROPAGATED out of the body --------------------------------\\n+\\n+func propagates(v: &Vector<Int>) -> Result<Int, Bad> {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() { total = total + (try fails(x)) }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- 6. an error CAUGHT inside the body that CONTINUES the loop -------------\\n+//\\n+// The window stays live and the iterator is NOT destroyed — this is a route\\n+// that does not leave the statement at all.\\n+func caught_and_continued(v: &var Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() {\\n+        let got = try fails(x) catch { 2 }\\n+        total = total + got\\n+    }\\n+    let _ = v.push(9)\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- the EMPTY loop and BREAK-THEN-MUTATE ----------------------------------\\n+\\n+func empty_loop(v: &var Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() { total = total + slow(x) }   // v is empty here\\n+    let _ = v.push(9)\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func break_then_mutate(v: &var Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() {\\n+        total = total + slow(x)\\n+        break\\n+    }\\n+    let _ = v.push(9)                 // the FIRST reachable point after the exit\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- B2: control-flow TARGETS ----------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+func inner_loop_jump_closes_nothing(v: &Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() {\\n+        var n = 0\\n+        while n < 3 {\\n+            n = n + 1\\n+            if n == 2 { continue }    // targets the INNER loop: closes nothing\\n+            if n == 3 { break }       // …and neither does this\\n+        }\\n+        total = total + slow(x) * 2\\n+    }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func outer_continue_closes_the_inner_window(v: &Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    var n = 0\\n+    while n < 2 {\\n+        n = n + 1\\n+        for x in v.iter() {           // a window NESTED inside an outer loop\\n+            total = total + slow(x)\\n+            if x == 2 { continue }    // this one targets the `for`\\n+        }\\n+        if n == 1 { continue }        // continuing the OUTER loop closed it\\n+    }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    var a: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    print(\\\"exhaustion sync {exhaustion_sync(&var a)} {a.len()}\\\")\\n+    var b: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    print(\\\"exhaustion driven {__saw_drive(exhaustion_driven(&var b))} {b.len()}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var c: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    print(\\\"break plain sync {break_plain_sync(&var c)} {c.len()}\\\")\\n+    var d: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    print(\\\"break plain driven {__saw_drive(break_plain_driven(&var d))} {d.len()}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var e: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    print(\\\"break with value sync {break_value_sync(&var e)} {e.len()}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var g: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    print(\\\"continue keeps the window sync {continue_sync(&var g)} {g.len()}\\\")\\n+    var h: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    print(\\\"continue keeps the window driven {__saw_drive(continue_driven(&var h))} {h.len()}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var i: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    let r = __saw_drive(return_from_body(&i))\\n+    let _ = i.push(9)                 // the caller is where the root is writable\\n+    print(\\\"return from the body {r} {i.len()}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var j: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    let p = match __saw_drive(propagates(&j)) {\\n+        case Ok(n) -> n,\\n+        case Err(_) -> 2\\n+    }\\n+    let _ = j.push(9)                 // the proof for the propagating route\\n+    print(\\\"error propagated {p} {j.len()}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var k: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    print(\\\"error caught and continued {__saw_drive(caught_and_continued(&var k))} {k.len()}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var m: Vector<Int> = []\\n+    print(\\\"empty loop {__saw_drive(empty_loop(&var m))} {m.len()}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var n: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3, 4]\\n+    print(\\\"break then mutate {__saw_drive(break_then_mutate(&var n))} {n.len()}\\\")\\n+\\n+    let o: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    print(\\\"inner loop jump closes nothing {__saw_drive(inner_loop_jump_closes_nothing(&o))}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"outer continue closes the inner window {__saw_drive(outer_continue_closes_the_inner_window(&o))}\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/conformance/K137_window_destruction_order.saw b/examples/conformance/K137_window_destruction_order.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..d280e686\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/conformance/K137_window_destruction_order.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,126 @@\\n+// K137 (design 275 U3, part (i)): TWO EVENTS AT THE CLOSE, IN THIS ORDER —\\n+// the iterator is DESTROYED, and THEN the root charge ends.\\n+//\\n+// The order matters because a hand-written `deinit` on a borrowing struct may\\n+// still read THROUGH the reference, so the referent has to outlive the drop.\\n+// The fixture is a user borrowing iterator holding a COUNTED resource beside a\\n+// COUNTED Vector owner, because an iterator of reference-plus-Int cannot test\\n+// destruction at all: the iterator's own `deinit` prints, the owner's prints,\\n+// and the interleaving IS the assertion.\\n+//\\n+// Owned fields and a hand-written `deinit` on a borrowing struct are SUPPORTED\\n+// (the drop machinery is the existing one — design 131's deinit prefix plus the\\n+// synthesized field drops — and refusing them would make the std iterators the\\n+// only writable shape). THE REFERENCE FIELD IS EXEMPT from drop, design 88: it\\n+// owns nothing.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// (The `walk` line prints AFTER the iterator's deinit because the interpolation\\n+// runs the call first — which is itself the assertion: the drop happens inside\\n+// the `for` statement, not at the end of the enclosing scope.)\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// -- sync\\n+// iterator deinit, owner still has 3\\n+// walk 6\\n+// owner deinit\\n+// after 0\\n+// -- driven\\n+// iterator deinit, owner still has 3\\n+// walk 6\\n+// owner deinit\\n+// after 0\\n+// -- break on the first element\\n+// iterator deinit, owner still has 3\\n+// walk 1\\n+// owner deinit\\n+\\n+import std.task.{yield_now}\\n+\\n+// A COUNTED resource the ITERATOR owns — this is what makes the drop visible.\\n+struct Tag {\\n+    name: String\\n+}\\n+extension Tag: NoCopy {}\\n+\\n+struct Bag {\\n+    private items: Vector<Int>\\n+}\\n+extension Bag: NoCopy {\\n+    // The COUNTED owner. Its deinit must run AFTER the iterator's, because the\\n+    // iterator's own deinit reads through the reference into it.\\n+    func deinit(&var self) { print(\\\"owner deinit\\\") }\\n+}\\n+\\n+borrows struct BagIterator {\\n+    private bag: &Bag\\n+    private index: Int,\\n+    private tag: Tag            // an OWNED field, dropped by the synthesized tail\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension BagIterator: NoCopy {\\n+    // A hand-written `deinit` that READS THROUGH THE REFERENCE. It runs BEFORE\\n+    // the root charge ends, which is what makes the read legal.\\n+    func deinit(&var self) {\\n+        print(\\\"{self.tag.name}, owner still has {self.bag.items.len()}\\\")\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension Bag {\\n+    public func iter(&self) borrows -> BagIterator {\\n+        BagIterator(bag: lends self, index: 0, tag: Tag(name: \\\"iterator deinit\\\"))\\n+    }\\n+    public func len(&self) -> Int { self.items.len() }\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension BagIterator: Iterator {\\n+    type Item = Int\\n+    public func next(&var self) -> Int? {\\n+        if self.index < self.bag.items.len() {\\n+            let i = self.index\\n+            self.index = self.index + 1\\n+            self.bag.items.get(i)\\n+        } else {\\n+            None\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func walk_sync(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in b.iter() { total = total + x }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func walk_driven(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in b.iter() { yield_now()  total = total + x }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func walk_break(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in b.iter() { total = total + x  break }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    print(\\\"-- sync\\\")\\n+    var v1: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    var b1 = Bag(items: move v1)\\n+    print(\\\"walk {walk_sync(&b1)}\\\")\\n+    // The iterator's deinit already printed, inside the `for`'s close; `b1`'s\\n+    // prints here, where the `move` retires it.\\n+    let _ = move b1\\n+    print(\\\"after 0\\\")\\n+\\n+    print(\\\"-- driven\\\")\\n+    var v2: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    var b2 = Bag(items: move v2)\\n+    print(\\\"walk {__saw_drive(walk_driven(&b2))}\\\")\\n+    let _ = move b2\\n+    print(\\\"after 0\\\")\\n+\\n+    print(\\\"-- break on the first element\\\")\\n+    var v3: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    var b3 = Bag(items: move v3)\\n+    print(\\\"walk {walk_break(&b3)}\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/conformance/K138_window_acquire_sequence.saw b/examples/conformance/K138_window_acquire_sequence.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..8af0a31b\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/conformance/K138_window_acquire_sequence.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,208 @@\\n+// K138 (design 275 U3, B3): THE ACQUIRE SEQUENCE IS ONE SEQUENCE.\\n+//\\n+// It is LANGUAGE_SPEC's \\\"Argument Evaluation Order\\\" — receiver before\\n+// arguments, arguments left to right — plus design 141's rule that a reference\\n+// argument's window is the whole CALL EXPRESSION, applied to the head\\n+// unchanged. Nothing about U3 changes evaluation order:\\n+//\\n+//   A1  the receiver PLACE is resolved, and is a persistent slot (which the\\n+//       direct-head rule is what guarantees), so it is never re-resolved to a\\n+//       different address;\\n+//   A2  the head's SHARED borrow of that root opens and spans the ENTIRE head\\n+//       call expression, ARGUMENTS INCLUDED — an argument that writes, moves\\n+//       or reallocates the root is REFUSED (the row lives in\\n+//       `examples/errors/window_acquire_mutating_argument.saw`), a SUSPENDING\\n+//       argument is ACCEPTED;\\n+//   A3  arguments evaluate left to right; a FAILING argument exits before the\\n+//       producer runs, so there is no statement charge, no iterator, and\\n+//       nothing to clean;\\n+//   A4  the producer runs and returns the borrowing struct;\\n+//   A5  the head window hands off to the STATEMENT charge with no gap.\\n+//\\n+// B4's own row is here too: an OWNED iterator produced by a SUSPENDING HEAD is\\n+// evaluated ONCE and preserved, which is what separates it from the borrowed\\n+// head's temporary-receiver refusal.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// (The bare `a` / `b` lines ahead of each `order` line ARE the sequence: the\\n+// receiver's own call prints `a`, the argument prints `b`, and the summary line\\n+// follows because an interpolation runs its call first.)\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// a\\n+// b\\n+// order sync a b 9\\n+// a\\n+// b\\n+// order driven a b 9\\n+// suspending argument sync 9\\n+//\\n+// suspending argument driven 9\\n+// failing argument: no iterator built, root writable 4\\n+// owned iterator from a suspending head 12\\n+\\n+import std.task.{yield_now}\\n+\\n+struct Bad { why: String }\\n+extension Bad: Error {\\n+    func format(&self, into: &var StringBuilder) { try! into.append(self.why) }\\n+}\\n+\\n+struct Bag {\\n+    private items: Vector<Int>,\\n+    private built: Int\\n+}\\n+extension Bag: NoCopy {}\\n+\\n+borrows struct BagIterator {\\n+    private bag: &Bag\\n+    private index: Int,\\n+    private skip: Int\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension Bag {\\n+    // A producer WITH AN ARGUMENT, which is what makes the ACQUIRE sequence\\n+    // observable at all: the receiver is resolved first, then the argument.\\n+    public func iter_from(&self, skip: Int) borrows -> BagIterator {\\n+        BagIterator(bag: lends self, index: 0, skip: skip)\\n+    }\\n+    public func built(&self) -> Int { self.built }\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension BagIterator: Iterator {\\n+    type Item = Int\\n+    public func next(&var self) -> Int? {\\n+        if self.index < self.skip { self.index = self.skip }\\n+        if self.index < self.bag.items.len() {\\n+            let i = self.index\\n+            self.index = self.index + 1\\n+            self.bag.items.get(i)\\n+        } else {\\n+            None\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func note(tag: String) -> Int {\\n+    print(\\\"{tag}\\\")\\n+    0\\n+}\\n+\\n+func note_slow(tag: String) -> Int {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    print(\\\"{tag}\\\")\\n+    0\\n+}\\n+\\n+// A3: a FAILING argument exits before the producer runs.\\n+func failing(x: Int) -> Result<Int, Bad> {\\n+    if x > 0 { return Bad(why: \\\"argument failed\\\") }\\n+    0\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- A1/A3: the RECEIVER is resolved first, then arguments left to right ----\\n+//\\n+// `receiver()` prints `a` and `note(\\\"b\\\")` prints `b`, so the printed order IS\\n+// the sequence. It is the ordinary argument order and is measured, not assumed.\\n+struct Holder {\\n+    bag: Bag\\n+}\\n+extension Holder: NoCopy {}\\n+\\n+extension Holder {\\n+    func chosen(&self) -> Int { print(\\\"a\\\")  0 }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func order_sync(h: &Holder) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    let _ = h.chosen()\\n+    for x in h.bag.iter_from(note(\\\"b\\\")) { total = total + x }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func order_driven(h: &Holder) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    let _ = h.chosen()\\n+    for x in h.bag.iter_from(note(\\\"b\\\")) { total = total + yield_and(x) }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func yield_and(x: Int) -> Int {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    x\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- A2: a SUSPENDING argument is ACCEPTED, value equal to the sync twin ----\\n+\\n+func suspending_argument_sync(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in b.iter_from(0) { total = total + x }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func suspending_argument_driven(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in b.iter_from(note_slow(\\\"\\\") + 0) { total = total + x }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- A3: a FAILING argument leaves NO charge and NO iterator ---------------\\n+\\n+func failing_argument(b: &var Bag) -> Result<Int, Bad> {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in b.iter_from(try failing(1)) { total = total + x }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- B4: an OWNED iterator from a SUSPENDING head, evaluated ONCE ----------\\n+\\n+struct Counter {\\n+    private next_value: Int,\\n+    private last: Int\\n+}\\n+extension Counter: Iterator {\\n+    type Item = Int\\n+    public func next(&var self) -> Int? {\\n+        if self.next_value > self.last {\\n+            None\\n+        } else {\\n+            let v = self.next_value\\n+            self.next_value = self.next_value + 1\\n+            v\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func slow_counter(last: Int) -> Counter {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    Counter(next_value: 1, last: last)\\n+}\\n+\\n+func owned_from_suspending_head() -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in slow_counter(3) { total = total + x * 2 }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    var v1: Vector<Int> = [2, 3, 4]\\n+    let h = Holder(bag: Bag(items: move v1, built: 0))\\n+    print(\\\"order sync a b {order_sync(&h)}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"order driven a b {__saw_drive(order_driven(&h))}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var v2: Vector<Int> = [2, 3, 4]\\n+    var b = Bag(items: move v2, built: 0)\\n+    print(\\\"suspending argument sync {suspending_argument_sync(&b)}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"suspending argument driven {__saw_drive(suspending_argument_driven(&b))}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var v3: Vector<Int> = [2, 3, 4]\\n+    var fb = Bag(items: move v3, built: 0)\\n+    let r = match failing_argument(&var fb) {\\n+        case Ok(n) -> n,\\n+        case Err(_) -> 0\\n+    }\\n+    // The root is writable in the caller: no charge was ever taken, because the\\n+    // argument failed before the producer ran.\\n+    let _ = r\\n+    print(\\\"failing argument: no iterator built, root writable {fb.built() + 4}\\\")\\n+\\n+    print(\\\"owned iterator from a suspending head {__saw_drive(owned_from_suspending_head())}\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/conformance/K140_window_under_cancellation.saw b/examples/conformance/K140_window_under_cancellation.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..de14b241\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/conformance/K140_window_under_cancellation.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,176 @@\\n+// K140 (design 275 U3): CANCELLATION IS A SAFETY GUARANTEE, NOT A TERMINATION\\n+// CLAIM.\\n+//\\n+// A cancel request does NOT close a window. The borrow stays live while\\n+// cancellation is delivered, while it is caught and handled inside the body,\\n+// and until the statement has actually exited. WHAT IS GUARANTEED is that a\\n+// live window is never destroyed by dropping a still-running frame: teardown\\n+// DRIVES the frame (a cancel wakes a parked task — design 102) and JOINS it\\n+// (design 124 item 5, K87) before its box is released, so the frame reaches\\n+// the statement's exit by ordinary control flow and iterator and owner cleanup\\n+// run exactly once on the route actually taken.\\n+//\\n+// THE CELLS, each counting deinits:\\n+//   * a cancel delivered while the task is PARKED IN A NESTED CALLEE inside\\n+//     the body — one iterator deinit, one owner deinit;\\n+//   * a caught cancellation that CONTINUES the loop — the window stays live,\\n+//     the iterator is not destroyed, and the loop runs to exhaustion;\\n+//   * a cancel delivered BEFORE the head is initialized. A cancel is a\\n+//     REQUEST: it does not stop the head from running, so the cell that\\n+//     measures zero constructions is one where the cancellation is PROPAGATED\\n+//     out of the head's own evaluation (B3's A3) — ZERO iterator\\n+//     constructions, ZERO iterator drops, and the OWNER's deinit count is\\n+//     whatever its initialization state was;\\n+//   * cancel at the park, then exhaustion.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// -- parked in a nested callee\\n+// iterator deinit\\n+// owner deinit\\n+// -- caught and continued: 3 items seen\\n+// iterator deinit\\n+// owner deinit\\n+// -- cancellation out of the head: 0 iterators built\\n+// owner deinit\\n+// -- cancel at the park then exhaustion\\n+// iterator deinit\\n+// owner deinit\\n+\\n+import std.task.{yield_now}\\n+\\n+struct Bad { why: String }\\n+extension Bad: Error {\\n+    func format(&self, into: &var StringBuilder) { try! into.append(self.why) }\\n+}\\n+\\n+struct Tag { name: window live in the caller's frame.\\n+func park_deep(x: Int) -> Int {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    x\\n+}\\n+\\n+func parked_in_a_nested_callee(b: Bag) -> Int {\\n+    var total = stays live and the\\n+// iterator is not destroyed until the statement exits on its own terms.\\n+func caught_and_continued(b: Bag) -> Int {\\n+    var seen = 0\\n+    for x in b.iter() {\\n+ print(\\\"-- caught and continued: {seen} items seen\\\")\\n+    seen\\n+}\\n+\\n+// B3's A3 under cancellation: the failure comes out of the HEAD's own\\n+// evaluation, so the producer never runs and no iterator is ever constructed.\\n+func failing_head(x: Int) -> Result<Int, Bad> {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    return Bad(why: \\\"cancelled in the head\\\")\\n+}\\n+\\n+func cancellation_out_of_the_head(b: Bag) -> Result<Int, Bad> {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in b.iter_from(try failing_head(0)) { total = total + x }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func The refusal was unreachable from a collection `for`\\n+// until design 275 U3 charged its back-edge (SL-317 gave that loop its state\\n+// split): an uncharged loop does not span, so the `if` carrying the `continue`\\n+// was never split, and this exact body passed as the `if_continue` cell of\\n+// `examples/coro_head_move_relinquishes_before_early_exit.saw` — where it still\\n+// sits, now measuring the split path with it. The same body over a RANGE `for`\\n+// met the refusal on every compiler before U3, which is what says the limit was\\n+// the split lowering's and not the loop's.\\n+//\\n+// THE ORACLE IS THE DROP COUNT: `r` is destroyed once per iteration, in the\\n+// callee, and frame teardown destroys nothing.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// drop old\\n+// drop old\\n+// 5\\n+\\n+import std.task.{yield_now}\\n+\\n+struct Res { name: String }\\n+extension Res: NoCopy {\\n+    func deinit(&var self) { print(\\\"drop {}\\\", self.name) }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func note(r: &Res) -> Int { r.name.len() }\\n+func consume_bool(r: Res) -> Bool { true }\\n+\\n+func if_continue(items: Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    for i in items.iter() {\\n+        var r = Res(name: \\\"old\\\")\\n+        if note(&r) + i < 0 { return -1 }\\n+        if consume_bool(move r) { continue }\\n+    }\\n+    5\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    print(__saw_drive(if_continue([1, 2])))\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/coro_move_in_split_match_scrutinee.saw b/examples/coro_move_in_split_match_scrutinee.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..c4b3cee8\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/coro_move_in_split_match_scrutinee.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,53 @@\\n+// SL-338, the third split container: a `move` of the SCRUTINEE of a\\n+// suspension-SPANNING `match` relinquishes the frame's claim exactly ONCE.\\n+//\\n+// `_split_match` evaluated its scrutinee inside the dispatch it emits and\\n+// refused the drop-flag clears a `move` owes, for the same reason `_split_if`\\n+// and `_split_while` did: the block the dispatch sits in is terminated by it,\\n+// so a trailing clear has no home and a leading one would run before the move.\\n+// All three hoist now — bind the head to a temp, give up the claim between the\\n+// temp and the container — which is `_hoist_head_and_relinquish`, the routine\\n+// the in-place lowering has used since SL-234. The scrutinee keeps the PLAIN\\n+// name rather than a `move` (`consuming=False`), because a `match` consumes an\\n+// owned scrutinee by construction and spelling a `move` there would cost the\\n+// arm bindings their own drop flags.\\n+//\\n+// The sibling sweep behind SL-338's fix is what found this one: the corpus\\n+// covered `match` only through `match_return`, whose arm `return`s and so needs\\n+// no split at all. This body `continue`s instead, which is what makes the\\n+// `match` a CFG-split container inside a loop that spans.\\n+//\\n+// THE ORACLE IS THE DROP COUNT: one `Res` per iteration, destroyed once, in the\\n+// callee.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// drop old\\n+// drop old\\n+// 6\\n+\\n+import std.task.{yield_now}\\n+\\n+struct Res { name: String }\\n+extension Res: NoCopy {\\n+    func deinit(&var self) { print(\\\"drop {}\\\", self.name) }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func note(r: &Res) -> Int { r.name.len() }\\n+func consume_int(r: Res) -> Int { 1 }\\n+\\n+func match_continue(items: Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    for i in items.iter() {\\n+        var r = Res(name: \\\"old\\\")\\n+        if note(&r) + i < 0 { return -1 }\\n+        match consume_int(move r) {\\n+            case 1 -> { continue },\\n+            case _ -> { }\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+    6\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    print(__saw_drive(match_continue([1, 2])))\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/coro_move_in_split_try_body.saw b/examples/coro_move_in_split_try_body.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..8ec7cac6\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/coro_move_in_split_try_body.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,149 @@\\n+// SL-339: a `move` of a frame local in the BODY of a suspension-SPANNING\\n+// `try { } catch { }` relinquishes the frame's claim on BOTH edges out of the\\n+// call — the ERROR edge as well as the straight-line one.\\n+//\\n+// THE RULE. `move r` hands ownership to the callee AT THE CALL, so the value is\\n+// gone whichever way control leaves afterwards. A leaf statement's lowering\\n+// emits the drop-flag clear as a TRAILING statement, which is correct and\\n+// sufficient for a straight-line edge (it is where SL-234 put it); a statement\\n+// inside a split try body then gets wrapped by `_emit_try_landing` in a\\n+// synthesized `try { <it> } catch { … }` whose error edge leaves BEFORE the\\n+// clear is reached, and `_emit_try_propagate` wraps the same way for a `try`\\n+// that leaves the coroutine entirely. Both edges now re-emit the clears that\\n+// `_trailing_forgets` recorded while the statement lowered, so exactly one runs\\n+// per path. Before that the error edge left the claim standing and frame\\n+// teardown destroyed what the callee had already destroyed — silent, exit 0.\\n+//\\n+// WHY THIS FILE EXISTS. Measured on MAIN with the BLOCK spelling, so the defect\\n+// is older than design 275 U3; what U3 changed is the REACH, by routing the\\n+// INLINE `try EXPR catch { … }` through the same split (SL-215) and by charging\\n+// a collection `for`'s back-edge (SL-317) so such a loop spans. These two bodies\\n+// are the inline spelling, and they also sit as the `try_break` / `try_continue`\\n+// cells of `examples/coro_head_move_relinquishes_before_early_exit.saw`.\\n+//\\n+// THE ORACLE IS THE DROP COUNT: one `Res` per iteration, destroyed exactly once,\\n+// in the callee.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// drop old\\n+// 2\\n+// drop old\\n+// drop old\\n+// 3\\n+// drop blk\\n+// 100\\n+// drop let\\n+// 200\\n+// drop prop\\n+// prop err fail\\n+// drop ret\\n+// ret err fail\\n+\\n+import std.task.{yield_now}\\n+\\n+struct Res { name: String }\\n+extension Res: NoCopy {\\n+    func deinit(&var self) { print(\\\"drop {}\\\", self.name) }\\n+}\\n+\\n+struct Bad { why: String }\\n+extension Bad: Error {\\n+    func format(&self, into: &var StringBuilder) { try! into.append(self.why) }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func note(r: &Res) -> Int { r.name.len() }\\n+func consume(r: Res) -> Result<Void, Bad> { Bad(why: \\\"fail\\\") }\\n+\\n+func try_break(items: Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    for i in items.iter() {\\n+        var r = Res(name: \\\"old\\\")\\n+        if note(&r) + i < 0 { return -1 }\\n+        try consume(move r) catch { break }\\n+    }\\n+    2\\n+}\\n+\\n+func try_continue(items: Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    for i in items.iter() {\\n+        var r = Res(name: \\\"old\\\")\\n+        if note(&r) + i < 0 { return -1 }\\n+        try consume(move r) catch { continue }\\n+    }\\n+    3\\n+}\\n+\\n+// --- the BLOCK spelling, which is where SL-339 was measured on main ----------\\n+\\n+func consume_int(r: Res) -> Result<Int, Bad> { Bad(why: \\\"fail\\\") }\\n+\\n+func block_body(items: Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var n = 0\\n+    try {\\n+        yield_now()\\n+        for i in items.iter() {\\n+            var r = Res(name: \\\"blk\\\")\\n+            if note(&r) + i < 0 { return -1 }\\n+            try consume(move r)\\n+            n = n + 1\\n+        }\\n+    } catch { n = 100 }\\n+    n\\n+}\\n+\\n+// The same edge reached from a `let` rather than a bare expression statement:\\n+// `_trailing_forgets` has one entry per leaf statement kind, and this is the\\n+// second of them.\\n+func block_let(items: Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    var n = 0\\n+    try {\\n+        yield_now()\\n+        for i in items.iter() {\\n+            var r = Res(name: \\\"let\\\")\\n+            if note(&r) + i < 0 { return -1 }\\n+            let v = try consume_int(move r)\\n+            n = n + v\\n+        }\\n+    } catch { n = 200 }\\n+    n\\n+}\\n+\\n+// --- the OTHER edge: no enclosing catch, so the `try` leaves the COROUTINE ---\\n+// (`_emit_try_propagate`, whose landing is the frame's own done sequence).\\n+\\n+func propagate_body(items: Vector<Int>) -> Result<Int, Bad> {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    for i in items.iter() {\\n+        var r = Res(name: \\\"prop\\\")\\n+        if note(&r) + i < 0 { return -1 }\\n+        try consume(move r)\\n+    }\\n+    5\\n+}\\n+\\n+// …and through `return try f(move r)`, whose trailing clears are `_done_seq`'s.\\n+func propagate_return(items: Vector<Int>) -> Result<Int, Bad> {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    for i in items.iter() {\\n+        var r = Res(name: \\\"ret\\\")\\n+        if note(&r) + i < 0 { return -1 }\\n+        return try consume_int(move r)\\n+    }\\n+    6\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    print(__saw_drive(try_break([1, 2])))\\n+    print(__saw_drive(try_continue([1, 2])))\\n+    print(__saw_drive(block_body([1, 2])))\\n+    print(__saw_drive(block_let([1, 2])))\\n+    match __saw_drive(propagate_body([1, 2])) {\\n+        case Ok(v) -> print(\\\"prop ok {v}\\\"),\\n+        case Err(e) -> print(\\\"prop err {e}\\\")\\n+    }\\n+    match __saw_drive(propagate_return([1, 2])) {\\n+        case Ok(v) -> print(\\\"ret ok {v}\\\"),\\n+        case Err(e) -> print(\\\"ret err {e}\\\")\\n+    }\\n+}\\ndiff --git loop's condition path — the\\n+// rule SL-234 gave the non-split `while` and SL-226 review r1 corrected, now\\n+// reaching the SPLIT lowering too.\\n+//\\n+// WHERE THE CLEAR GOES, and why `while` is the head that had to say it. Every\\n+// other container evaluates its head once, so its hoist is a prelude ahead of\\n+// the container. A `while` CONDITION re-evaluates, and repeated evaluation of a\\n+// MOVING condition is valid: the body may reinitialize the local before the\\n+// backedge, so the next pass consumes the REPLACEMENT. In the in-place lowering\\n+// `_head_into_while_body` puts the hoist inside the loop ahead of the body\\n+// dispatch; in the SPLIT lowering the HEADER is already its own state, re-entered\\n+// by every backedge and every `continue`, so `_split_while` emitting the hoist\\n+// there says the same thing with nothing extra. It used to refuse instead, the\\n+// condition being evaluated inside `_branch(...)` with no block left for a\\n+// trailing clear.\\n+//\\n+// WHY THIS FILE EXISTS. These two bodies are the DRIVEN halves of\\n+// `examples/coro_head_move_with_early_exit_drops_once.saw`, where they sit\\n+// beside their sync twins — the oracle — and where they were lowered in place\\n+// until design 275 U3 charged a collection `for`'s back-edge and everything\\n+// nested inside it (SL-317). The inner `while` SPANS now, so the same source\\n+// exercises the split path, which is what this file names.\\n+//\\n+// THE ORACLE IS THE SYNC TWIN: the `move` in the head relinquishes exactly once,\\n+// so a contained block that exits early destroys the value once, and one that\\n+// REINITIALIZES keeps its replacement's claim.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// while-exit driven\\n+// drop old\\n+// -> 3\\n+// while-reinit driven\\n+// drop old\\n+// drop new\\n+// -> 6\\n+\\n+import std.task.{yield_now}\\n+\\n+struct Res { name: String }\\n+extension Res: NoCopy {\\n+    func deinit(&var self) { print(\\\"drop {}\\\", self.name) }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func note(r: &Res) -> Int { r.name.len() }\\n+func consume_bool(r: Res) -> Bool { true }\\n+\\n+func while_exit_driven(items: Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    for i in items.iter() {\\n+        var r = Res(name: \\\"old\\\")\\n+        if note(&r) + i < 0 { return -1 }\\n+        while consume_bool(move r) { return 3 }\\n+    }\\n+    0\\n+}\\n+\\n+func while_reinit_driven(items: Vector<Int>) -> Int {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    for i in items.iter() {\\n+        var r = Res(name: An iterator carrying `&var Collection` could\\n+// REALLOCATE the very collection an outer shared iterator is reading through\\n+// its own field, and recording the root alone would not catch it — so a `&var`\\n+// field is refused at the field, with the rule named. U3 admits ONE origin — the RECEIVER — so every other\\n+// spelling is refused by name rather than approximated by a root walk:\\n+//\\n+//   * `lends self.items` — a PROJECTION of the receiver. `lend the ROOT, not\\n+//     the buffer` is the whole shape of the four-line iterator: a struct that\\n+//     snapshots what the receiver points at is back to a pointer the owner can\\n+//     invalidate.\\n+//   * `lends other` — another reference PARAMETER. The window charges the\\n+//     RECEIVER place's root, and nothing at the call site names this one.\\n+//   * a by-value receiver is not a row here: the PARSER already refuses `self`\\n+//     without a sigil (\\\"'self' must be a reference\\\"), so the origin rule's own\\n+//     refusal for it is unreachable from source and is the belt to that braces.\\n+//   * a `&var self` receiver — U3 admits SHARED reference fields only, so an\\n+//     exclusive lend would be the origin of a field this type may not hold.\\n+//   * a field NOT initialized with `lends self` on a returning path.\\n+//   * a `borrows struct` with NO reference field — design 130's rule-7\\n+//     teaching error: the keyword says values of this type carry a borrow, and\\n+//     every use site reads that claim.\\n+// EXPECT: error\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: U3 admits the receiver as the only borrow origin\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: lend the ROOT, not the buffer\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: requires a SHARED receiver (`&self`)\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: holds no lent place\\n+\\n+struct Bag {\\n+    private items: Vector<Int>\\n+}\\n+extension Bag: NoCopy {}\\n+\\n+borrows struct Projected {\\n+    private items: &Vector<Int>\\n+    private index: Int\\n+}\\n+\\n+borrows struct Foreign {\\n+    private bag: &Bag\\n+    private index: Int\\n+}\\n+\\n+borrows struct Exclusive {\\n+    private bag: &Bag\\n+    private index: Int\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ROW: a `borrows struct` with no reference field at all.\\n+borrows struct Placeless {\\n+    private index: Int\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension Bag {\\n+    // ROW: a PROJECTION of the receiver.\\n+    public func projected(&self) borrows -> Projected {\\n+        Projected(items: lends self.items, index: 0)\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    // ROW: another reference PARAMETER.\\n+    public func foreign(&self, other: &Bag) borrows -> Foreign {\\n+        Foreign(bag: lends other, index: 0)\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    // ROW: an EXCLUSIVE receiver.\\n+    public func exclusive(&var self) borrows -> Exclusive {\\n+        Exclusive(bag: lends self, index: 0)\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    print(\\\"unreached\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/errors/borrowing_struct_type_positions.saw b/examples/errors/borrowing_struct_type_positions.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..386c56ee\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/errors/borrowing_struct_type_positions.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,95 @@\\n+// K143 (design 275 U3): NOTHING ERASES IT — the IMPOSSIBLE SPELLINGS.\\n+//\\n+// A helper forwarding a borrowing struct and a generic identity wrapper are\\n+// not value-position rows: a borrowing struct cannot BE a plain function's\\n+// parameter or return type, nor a type argument, nor an existential, so no\\n+// helper can name one and the refusal lands at the HELPER's declaration, where\\n+// the author can act on it. That is what makes \\\"a generic identity wrapper\\n+// launders the value\\\" unwritable rather than caught.\\n+//\\n+// The `Item` row is the NO-SECOND-ITEM-LIFETIME rule at its declaration\\n+// position: a borrowing struct's `Iterator.Item` may be neither a reference\\n+// nor a borrowing struct, so the loop variable OWNS what it is handed.\\n+// EXPECT: error\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: may not name a borrowing struct\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: parameter `it` of `hold`\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: the return type of `hand_back`\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: field `it` of `Holder`\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: static `KEPT`\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: associated type `Item` of `Chain`\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: A type argument, an existential, a field, a parameter and a plain return each outlive the window\\n+\\n+struct Bag {\\n+    private items: Vector<Int>\\n+}\\n+extension Bag: NoCopy {}\\n+\\n+borrows struct BagIterator {\\n+    private bag: &Bag\\n+    private index: Int\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension Bag {\\n+    public func iter(&self) borrows -> BagIterator {\\n+        BagIterator(bag: lends self, index: 0)\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension BagIterator: Iterator {\\n+    type Item = Int\\n+    public func next(&var self) -> Int? {\\n+        if self.index < self.bag.items.len() {\\n+            let i = self.index\\n+            self.index = self.index + 1\\n+            self.bag.items.get(i)\\n+        } else {\\n+            None\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ---- the rows -------------------------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+// ROW: a PARAMETER — the position a reference is legal in and a borrowing\\n+// struct is not, because a parameter outlives nothing but the callee may store\\n+// it and the window's extent ends at a statement the callee cannot see.\\n+func hold(it: BagIterator) -> Int { 0 }\\n+\\n+// ROW: a plain RETURN. The producer's own `borrows -> BagIterator` above is\\n+// the one declaration that may name it.\\n+func hand_back(b: &Bag) -> BagIterator { b.iter() }\\n+\\n+// ROW: a FIELD.\\n+struct Holder {\\n+    private it: BagIterator\\n+}\\n+\\n+// ROW: a TYPE ARGUMENT — \\\"may not instantiate ANY type parameter\\\", reached by\\n+// the same walk as the bare spelling, which is what closes `Optional<It>`,\\n+// `Result<It, E>`, `Box<It>` and a generic `T` with it.\\n+func stash(v: Vector<BagIterator>) -> Int { 0 }\\n+\\n+// ROW: a STATIC.\\n+static KEPT: BagIterator\\n+\\n+// ROW: an `Item` LIFETIME — an associated-type assignment naming one.\\n+borrows struct ChainIterator {\\n+    private bag: &Bag\\n+    private index: Int\\n+}\\n+extension Bag {\\n+    public func chain(&self) borrows -> ChainIterator {\\n+        ChainIterator(bag: lends self, index: 0)\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+struct Chain {\\n+    private n: Int\\n+}\\n+extension Chain: Iterator {\\n+    type Item = BagIterator\\n+    public func next(&var self) -> BagIterator? { None }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    print(\\\"unreached\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/errors/borrowing_struct_value_positions.saw b/examples/errors/borrowing_struct_value_positions.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..520606e2\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/errors/borrowing_struct_value_positions.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,131 @@\\n+// K142 (design 275 U3): THE FENCE, value positions, with a USER-DEFINED\\n+// borrowing struct over a user collection — std is the only positive consumer\\n+// migrated, so the negative fixtures are owed in the shape an author would\\n+// write them.\\n+//\\n+// A `borrows` call returning a borrowing struct is legal in exactly ONE\\n+// position: the DIRECT head of a `for` whose receiver is a place rooted in a\\n+// named binding. Everything else goes through ONE rejector, whose sentence is\\n+// the RULE — a borrowing struct holds a lent place, so its values live only\\n+// inside their window, and the extent is the `for` statement. ELEVEN rows\\n+// below reach that one message; the matrix is the rows, not a table of\\n+// phrasings.\\n+//\\n+// TWO positions get their own diagnosis because the ACT is different: a\\n+// TEMPORARY receiver (\\\"bind the collection first\\\" — a temporary has no\\n+// persistent storage for the window to point into) and a head that is an\\n+// EXPRESSION yielding the call rather than the call itself.\\n+//\\n+// The positions that are IMPOSSIBLE SPELLINGS rather than value-position rows\\n+// — a parameter, a field, a plain return, a type argument, an existential, an\\n+// `Item` — are refused at the declaration that names the type, and live in\\n+// `examples/errors/borrowing_struct_type_positions.saw`.\\n+// EXPECT: error\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: may not be produced from a TEMPORARY receiver\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: may not be produced by an `if` / `match` / block / `try` expression in the head\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: may not be used outside a window head\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: a borrowing struct holds a LENT PLACE\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: write the call as the head of the `for` that consumes it\\n+\\n+struct Bag {\\n+    private items: Vector<Int>\\n+}\\n+extension Bag: NoCopy {}\\n+\\n+borrows struct BagIterator {\\n+    private bag: &Bag\\n+    private index: Int\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension Bag {\\n+    public func iter(&self) borrows -> BagIterator {\\n+        BagIterator(bag: lends self, index: 0)\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension BagIterator: Iterator {\\n+    type Item = Int\\n+    public func next(&var self) -> Int? {\\n+        if self.index < self.bag.items.len() {\\n+            let i = self.index\\n+            self.index = self.index + 1\\n+            self.bag.items.get(i)\\n+        } else {\\n+            None\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func make() -> Bag {\\n+    var v: Vector<Int> = [1, 2]\\n+    Bag(items: move v)\\n+}\\n+\\n+func count(n: Int) -> Int { n }\\n+\\n+// ---- the rows -------------------------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+func row_let(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    let it = b.iter()                       // ROW 1: a `let` initializer\\n+    0\\n+}\\n+\\n+func row_var(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    var it = b.iter()                       // ROW 2: a `var` initializer\\n+    0\\n+}\\n+\\n+func row_argument(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    count(b.iter())                         // ROW 3: an argument\\n+}\\n+\\n+func row_discard(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    b.iter()                                // ROW 4: evaluated and discarded\\n+    0\\n+}\\n+\\n+func row_tuple(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    let pair = (b.iter(), 1)                // ROW 5: a tuple element\\n+    0\\n+}\\n+\\n+func row_array(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    let xs = [b.iter()]                     // ROW 6: a collection-literal element\\n+    0\\n+}\\n+\\n+func row_assign_rhs(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    var n = 0\\n+    n = b.iter()                            // ROW 7: an assignment RHS\\n+    n\\n+}\\n+\\n+func row_closure_return(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    let f = { b.iter() }                    // ROW 8: a closure's returned value\\n+    0\\n+}\\n+\\n+func row_optional(b: &Bag) -> Int {\\n+    let n = b.iter() ?? 0                   // ROW 9: a `??` operand\\n+    0\\n+}\\n+\\n+func row_temporary() -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in make().iter() {                // ROW 10: a TEMPORARY receiver\\n+        total = total + x\\n+    }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func row_head_expression(b: &Bag, other: &Bag, flag: Bool) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in if flag { b.iter() } else { other.iter() } {   // ROW 11: a head EXPRESSION\\n+        total = total + x\\n+    }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    print(\\\"unreached\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/errors/for_iter_body_mutates_the_collection.saw b/examples/errors/for_iter_body_mutates_the_collection.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..f32db27c\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/errors/for_iter_body_mutates_the_collection.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,32 @@\\n+// K132 (design 275 U3, closing SL-321): NO USE-AFTER-FREE IN SAFE CODE.\\n+//\\n+// This program compiled clean, exited 0 and printed `seen = 1025` for a sum of\\n+// 10. `Vector.iter` handed back an OWNED value carrying a raw buffer pointer\\n+// and a length SNAPSHOT; the `&self` borrow ended at the return, so the type\\n+// system held no link between the iterator and the vector and the Law of\\n+// Exclusivity never saw the body's `&var v` as conflicting. After the first\\n+// body's pushes reallocated, the remaining `next()` reads came out of FREED\\n+// MEMORY — in safe sync code, with no `unsafe` anywhere in the source.\\n+//\\n+// The iterator is a BORROWING STRUCT now: it holds `&Vector<T, A>` and the\\n+// `for` statement is its window, so the mutation is the clean exclusivity\\n+// error `v.push` already got inside a `with_ref` window.\\n+// EXPECT: error\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: exclusive access violation\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: `v` cannot be accessed by reference here\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: borrows `v` for the whole of the `for` statement\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    var v: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3, 4]\\n+    var seen = 0\\n+    for x in v.iter() {\\n+        seen = seen + x\\n+        var k = 0\\n+        while k < 64 {\\n+            let _ = v.push(100 + k)\\n+            k = k + 1\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+    print(\\\"seen = {}\\\", seen)\\n+    print(\\\"count = {}\\\", v.len())\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/errors/inline_catch_multi_error.saw b/examples/errors/inline_catch_multi_error.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..36843c4b\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/errors/inline_catch_multi_error.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,51 @@\\n+// K148 (design 275 U3 / SL-215): DF-196b's MULTI-ERROR REFUSAL IS INHERITED\\n+// WHOLE.\\n+//\\n+// A suspending `try { } catch { }` block may raise only ONE error type: the\\n+// catch would bind a union the split lowering cannot build. SL-215 routes the\\n+// INLINE form through that same split, so the same fence applies — and it is\\n+// satisfied by construction for a one-expression inline catch, which raises\\n+// exactly one type. What is refused is the BLOCK the inline form desugars\\n+// into when an author writes two error types in it, which is the shape that\\n+// was refused before U3 and is refused identically after.\\n+// EXPECT: error\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: may raise only ONE error type\\n+\\n+import std.task.{yield_now}\\n+\\n+struct Alpha { why: String }\\n+extension Alpha: Error {\\n+    func format(&self, into: &var StringBuilder) { try! into.append(self.why) }\\n+}\\n+\\n+struct Beta { why: String }\\n+extension Beta: Error {\\n+    func format(&self, into: &var StringBuilder) { try! into.append(self.why) }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func a(x: Int) -> Result<Int, Alpha> {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    if x < 0 { return Alpha(why: \\\"a\\\") }\\n+    x\\n+}\\n+\\n+func b(x: Int) -> Result<Int, Beta> {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    if x < 0 { return Beta(why: \\\"b\\\") }\\n+    x\\n+}\\n+\\n+func two_error_types(x: Int) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    try {\\n+        total = total + (try a(x))\\n+        total = total + (try b(x))\\n+    } catch {\\n+        total = 0\\n+    }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    print(\\\"{__saw_drive(two_error_types(1))}\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/errors/mutex_guarded_for_suspends.saw b/examples/errors/mutex_guarded_for_suspends.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..3f7ccc18\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/errors/mutex_guarded_for_suspends.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,29 @@\\n+// K141's REFUSAL half (design 275 U3, part (iii)): a suspension inside a\\n+// `Mutex.lock` body is refused by the `sync`-body rule.\\n+//\\n+// `Mutex.lock<R>(&self, body: (&var T) sync -> R)` declares a `sync` body, so\\n+// the `for` inside one is a SYNC window: it works with the window's guarantees\\n+// intact, and a suspension there is the existing design-141/230 fence rather\\n+// than a new rule. Nothing about U3 extends a guard's lifetime, and nothing\\n+// has to.\\n+// EXPECT: error\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: sync\\n+\\n+import std.mutex.{Mutex}\\n+import std.task.{yield_now}\\n+\\n+func step(x: Int) -> Int {\\n+    yield_now()\\n+    x\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    var v: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    let guarded = Mutex(value: move v)\\n+    let a = guarded.lock({ items in\\n+        var total = 0\\n+        for x in items.iter() { total = total + step(x) }\\n+        total\\n+    })\\n+    print(\\\"{a}\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/errors/window_acquire_mutating_argument.saw b/examples/errors/window_acquire_mutating_argument.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..88c1c3f0\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/errors/window_acquire_mutating_argument.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,59 @@\\n+// K139 (design 275 U3, B3 step A2): THE HEAD'S BORROW SPANS THE WHOLE CALL\\n+// EXPRESSION, ARGUMENTS INCLUDED.\\n+//\\n+// Exactly as `f(&v, g(&var v))` is already a conflict today (design 199: an\\n+// argument's borrow extends over the whole call expression), an explicit or\\n+// default ARGUMENT of the head that writes, moves or reallocates the root is\\n+// refused — before the producer ever runs. To hold a lent place for the extent of ONE window, declare \\\"\\n+                f\\\"the type `borrows struct {struct_name}` (design 275 U3): a \\\"\\n+                f\\\"borrowing struct's values live only inside their window, \\\"\\n+                f\\\"which is what makes the shared field sound\\\"\\n+                if found is field_type and not field_type.reference_mutable\\n+                else \\\"\\\"))\\n \\n     def reject_reference_payload(self, payload_name: str, variant_name: str,\\n                                  enum_name: str, payload_type: SawType,\\ndiff --git a/sawc/place_transform.py b/sawc/place_transform.py\\nindex 4c89f43f..66306760 100644\\n--- a/sawc/place_transform.py\\n+++ b/sawc/place_transform.py\\n@@ -181,6 +181,13 @@ class _PlaceTransform:\\n     def run(self, program: Program) -> None:\\n         for func in getattr(program, 'functions', []) or []:\\n             if getattr(func, 'is_borrows', False):\\n+                if _is_borrowing_producer(func):\\n+                    # A free `borrows` function that lends nothing is the U3\\n+                    # PRODUCER shape with no receiver to be the origin of.
