That\\n+                    # is exactly what DF-146b already fixed for a design-141\\n+                    # accessor, so it is the same bit, not a second ABI: the\\n+                    # checker still sees a plain `&self` body, and everything\\n+                    # but the `lends self` in it stays ordinary `&self` code.\\n+                    if not getattr(method, 'is_static', False):\\n+                        method.place_self_by_pointer = True\\n+                    continue\\n                 sig = self._signature(method)\\n                 if sig is None:\\n                     continue          # reported; left un-lowered on purpose\\n@@ -1038,6 +1057,39 @@ def _mentions_lend_var(node) -> bool:\\n     return node is not None and _contains(node, LendVarLiteral)\\n \\n \\n+def _is_borrowing_producer(decl) -> bool:\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"Is this `borrows` declaration the U3 VALUE-returning producer rather\\n+    than a design-141 lend?\\n+\\n+    A `borrows` function either LENDS exactly once or RETURNS a borrowing\\n+    struct, never both — so the presence of a `lend` in the body is the whole\\n+    answer, and it is an answer this pass can give. Which is what it needs: the\\n+    place transform runs BEFORE type checking, so it cannot ask whether the\\n+    return type names a `borrows struct` (the struct may be imported). A\\n+    declaration that lends nothing has no window-closure form to lower into;\\n+    everything else about it — that the return type really is a borrowing\\n+    struct, the receiver, the origin — is `typechecker/borrowing.py`'s, which\\n+    runs when the answer is knowable.\\n+\\n+    TWO conditions, and the RETURN TYPE is the first because SL-333 R1 made it\\n+    load-bearing: `borrows -> &T` NAMES A LENT REFERENCE, so such a declaration\\n+    is a design-141 accessor whatever its body does and a missing `lend` stays\\n+    design 141's coverage error (\\\"a `borrows` body must `lend` a place\\\"). Only\\n+    a NON-reference return can be the U3 producer — and then only if the body\\n+    lends nothing, so a `borrows -> T` body that DOES lend still gets R1's\\n+    \\\"write `-> &T`\\\" fixit from `_signature` rather than being read as a\\n+    producer that forgot its struct.\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    if not getattr(decl, 'is_borrows', False):\\n+        return False\\n+    declared = getattr(decl, 'return_type', None)\\n+    if declared is None or declared.kind == TypeKind.REFERENCE:\\n+        return False\\n+    if declared.kind == TypeKind.VOID:\\n+        return False\\n+    return not _contains(getattr(decl, 'body', None), LendStatement)\\n+\\n+\\n def _fold_lend_var(block: Block, flavor: bool) -> None:\\n     \\\"\\\"\\\"Fold `#lend_var` to `flavor` through `block`, PRUNING what it decides.\\n \\ndiff --git a/sawc/sawc.py b/sawc/sawc.py\\nindex 52190619..af16e3bc 100755\\n--- a/sawc/sawc.py\\n+++ b/sawc/sawc.py\\n@@ -1382,6 +1382,7 @@ def admit_declarations(adm) -> bool:\\n     tc.exempt_ext_scope = True\\n     tc.exempt_shadowed_qualifier = True\\n     tc.exempt_prelude_gate = True\\n+    tc.exempt_statement_window = True\\n \\n     for attempt in range(2):\\n         entry_ns = run_typecheck(tc, lambda: tc.check_module(\\ndiff --git a/sawc/std/vector.saw b/sawc/std/vector.saw\\nindex db163bb0..b0c2104d 100644\\n--- a/sawc/std/vector.saw\\n+++ b/sawc/std/vector.saw\\n@@ -653,17 +653,25 @@ extension Vector<T: Copy, A: Allocator = GlobalAllocator> {\\n     // is a copy at the source. Unbounded, `next()` returned a bitwise duplicate\\n     // of the slot, so an owning element (`Vector<String>`, `Vector<Vector<..>>`,\\n     // `Vector<File>`) was owned by BOTH the loop variable and the vector and\\n-    // deinit'd twice (design 122 unit A). This follows the `StringBytes`/\\n-    // `StringScalars` model — an iterator yields values the consumer owns\\n-    // outright, with their own retain — rather than a scoped-borrow shape:\\n-    // a borrow iterator would have to hold the container borrowed for the\\n-    // whole loop, which is exactly what `with_ref`/`with_var_ref` already\\n-    // offer per element, and where `each`/`map` reach a NoCopy element type.\\n+    // deinit'd twice (design 122 unit A). The ELEMENT policy is unchanged; what\\n+    // changed in design 275 U3 is the ITERATOR's relationship to the vector.\\n+    // It used to be an owned value carrying a raw buffer pointer and a length\\n+    // SNAPSHOT — an untracked borrow, which SL-321 proved unsound: the loop\\n+    // body could `v.push` until the buffer reallocated, and the remaining\\n+    // `next()` reads came out of freed memory in safe sync code. Now the\\n+    // iterator is a BORROWING STRUCT holding `&Vector<T>` itself, the `for`\\n+    // statement is its window, and a body mutation is the clean exclusivity\\n+    // error `v.push` already gets inside a `with_ref` window.\\n     /// Returns an iterator over the vector's elements (`for x in v.iter()`).\\n     /// Each element is yielded as an owned copy, which is what requires\\n-    /// `T: Copy`.\\n-    public func iter(&self) unsafe -> VectorIterator<T> {\\n-        VectorIterator<T>(buffer: self.buffer, length: self.length, index: 0)\\n+    /// `T: Copy`. The iterator BORROWS the vector for the whole loop, so the\\n+    /// body may read it but not add to it, remove from it or replace it.\\n+    // The ALLOCATOR rides through: the iterator holds `&Vector<T, A>`, so a\\n+    // vector on a custom allocator iterates without its allocator being\\n+    // erased. (The old shape held a bare `UnsafePointer<T>`, which is why it\\n+    // needed no `A` — and why nothing connected the iterator to the vector.)\\n+    public func iter(&self) borrows -> VectorIterator<T, A> {\\n+        VectorIterator<T, A>(vector: lends self, index: 0)\\n     }\\n \\n     // A concrete iterator yielding `(index, element)` pairs for `for` loops\\n@@ -671,9 +679,9 @@ extension Vector<T: Copy, A: Allocator = GlobalAllocator> {\\n     // each element is produced by value.\\n     /// Returns an iterator yielding `(index, element)` pairs. The iterator follows\\n     /// the bounds of `iter()`. Because elements are yielded by value, `T` must\\n-    /// be `Copy`.\\n-    public func enumerated(&self) unsafe -> EnumeratedIterator<T> {\\n-        EnumeratedIterator<T>(buffer: self.buffer, length: self.length, index: 0)\\n+    /// be `Copy`. Like `iter()`, it BORROWS the vector for the whole loop.\\n+    public func enumerated(&self) borrows -> EnumeratedIterator<T, A> {\\n+        EnumeratedIterator<T, A>(vector: lends self, index: 0)\\n     }\\n }\\n \\n@@ -763,14 +771,19 @@ extension Vector<T, A: Allocator = GlobalAllocator> {\\n // VectorIterator<T> - Iterator over vector elements\\n // =============================================================================\\n \\n-// Iterator holds a pointer and length, not the Vector itself\\n-// This allows Vector to be NoCopy while still supporting iteration\\n-// `public` because `Vector.iter()` HANDS ONE OUT: a public API needs public\\n-// types (Aug 21), and a caller that holds the value of a `for x in v.iter()`\\n-// must be able to name its type. The fields stay private.\\n-public struct VectorIterator<T> {\\n-    private buffer: UnsafePointer<T>?\\n-    private length: Int\\n+// A BORROWING STRUCT (design 275 U3): it holds the VECTOR, lent, and reads\\n+// `length` and `buffer` through the reference on every `next()`. Nothing is\\n+// snapshotted, so there is no stale pointer to go stale — which is what makes\\n+// SL-321's repro a compile error at the `push` instead of a read out of freed\\n+// memory. LEND THE ROOT, NOT THE BUFFER is the whole of the shape: an iterator\\n+// over `self.buffer` would be back to a pointer the vector can invalidate.\\n+//\\n+// `public` for the design-80 signature-visibility rule — `Vector.iter()` is\\n+// public and a public signature may not name a private type — NOT so a caller\\n+// can hold one: holding a borrowing struct is refused, and its only legal\\n+// position is the head of the `for` that consumes it. The fields stay private.\\n+public borrows struct VectorIterator<T, A: Allocator = GlobalAllocator> {\\n+    private vector: &Vector<T, A>\\n     private index: Int\\n }\\n \\n@@ -784,14 +797,14 @@ public struct VectorIterator<T> {\\n // duplicable-family bound admits.\\n // A move-only element type never reaches here (use `with_ref`/`with_var_ref`,\\n // or the closure methods, which carry no bound at all since design 216).\\n-extension VectorIterator<T: ExplicitCopy>: Iterator {\\n+extension VectorIterator<T: ExplicitCopy, A: Allocator = GlobalAllocator>: Iterator {\\n     type Item = T\\n \\n     public func next(&var self) unsafe -> T? {\\n-        if self.index < self.length {\\n+        if self.index < self.vector.length {\\n             let idx = self.index\\n             self.index = self.index + 1\\n-            if let buf = self.buffer {\\n+            if let buf = self.vector.buffer {\\n                 buf[idx].copy()\\n             } else {\\n                 None\\n@@ -806,25 +819,24 @@ extension VectorIterator<T: ExplicitCopy>: Iterator {\\n // EnumeratedIterator<T> - yields (index, element) pairs (design 53)\\n // =============================================================================\\n \\n-// `public` for the same reason as `VectorIterator` above:\\n-// `Vector.enumerated()` returns one.\\n-public struct EnumeratedIterator<T> {\\n-    private buffer: UnsafePointer<T>?\\n-    private length: Int\\n+// The same four-line shape as `VectorIterator` above, and `public` for the\\n+// same design-80 reason: `Vector.enumerated()` returns one.\\n+public borrows struct EnumeratedIterator<T, A: Allocator = GlobalAllocator> {\\n+    private vector: &Vector<T, A>\\n     private index: Int\\n }\\n \\n // `T: ExplicitCopy` + `.copy()` for the same reason as `VectorIterator` above:\\n // the yielded pair OWNS its element, so it must not be a bitwise duplicate of\\n // the slot. (`enumerated()` keeps the stricter `T: Copy` at the producer.)\\n-extension EnumeratedIterator<T: ExplicitCopy>: Iterator {\\n+extension EnumeratedIterator<T: ExplicitCopy, A: Allocator = GlobalAllocator>: Iterator {\\n     type Item = (Int, T)\\n \\n     public func next(&var self) unsafe -> (Int, T)? {\\n-        if self.index < self.length {\\n+        if self.index < self.vector.length {\\n             let idx = self.index\\n             self.index = self.index + 1\\n-            if let buf = self.buffer {\\n+            if let buf = self.vector.buffer {\\n                 (idx, buf[idx].copy())\\n             } else {\\n                 None\\ndiff --git a/sawc/typechecker/borrowing.py b/sawc/typechecker/borrowing.py\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..128947d5\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/sawc/typechecker/borrowing.py\\n@@ -0,0 +1,845 @@\\n+\\\"\\\"\\\"BORROWING STRUCTS: a type that holds a lent place (design 275 U3).\\n+\\n+    borrows struct VectorIterator<T> {      // (1) the type declares its nature\\n+        private vector: &Vector<T>          // (2) the field is a plain reference\\n+        private index: Int\\n+    }\\n+    extension Vector<T: Copy> {\\n+        public func iter(&self) borrows -> VectorIterator<T> {   // (3) the signature echoes it\\n+            VectorIterator<T>(vector: lends self, index: 0)      // (4) the body proves it\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+\\n+Four spellings, each a Saw word or a design-141 word one position over, and\\n+each one half of a rule this module enforces:\\n+\\n+(1) `borrows struct` is design 130's `unsafe struct` shape — the parser's, and\\n+    `parser/types.py:reject_reference_field` is where the field half lives.\\n+(2) The field keeps the ordinary reference spelling. SHARED ONLY: a `&var`\\n+    field, and an exclusive `lends` that would feed one, are refused, because a\\n+    user iterator carrying `&var Collection` could reallocate the very\\n+    collection an outer shared iterator is reading through its own field —\\n+    recording the root alone would not catch it. (Distinct from `next(&var\\n+    self)`: the cursor is the iterator's OWN state.)\\n+(3) `borrows` in the effect slot on a VALUE-returning function. A `borrows`\\n+    function either `lend`s exactly once (design 141) or RETURNS a borrowing\\n+    struct, never both — `place_transform` splits on exactly that, by whether\\n+    the body contains a `lend`.\\n+(4) `lends self` at the init site is the body's proof, and THE ORIGIN IS A\\n+    CHECKED COMPILER FACT: U3 admits the RECEIVER and nothing else, the\\n+    receiver must be a REFERENCE receiver (`&self`), and the summary is\\n+    recorded on the declaration as `Method.borrow_origin` — a DECLARED\\n+    annotation field, so it rides `substitute_ast_types` through\\n+    monomorphization and travels with an imported declaration exactly as\\n+    `is_reference` does.\\n+\\n+THE FENCE, and the ONE REJECTOR (obligation 1). A `borrows` call returning a\\n+borrowing struct is legal in exactly ONE position: the DIRECT head of a `for`\\n+whose RECEIVER is a PLACE rooted in a named binding. Every other position goes\\n+through `reject_borrowing_value`, whose `POSITIONS` table is the matrix the\\n+tests walk row by row. Type positions — a parameter, a field, a return, a type\\n+argument, an existential — go through `reject_borrowing_type`, and the two are\\n+one funnel in the sense that matters: one message vocabulary, one rule\\n+sentence, one place to add a row.\\n+\\n+NOTHING ERASES IT. A borrowing struct may not instantiate any type parameter,\\n+be erased into an existential, or be named by a helper's parameter or return\\n+type. Those are not a value-position refusal each; they are IMPOSSIBLE\\n+SPELLINGS, refused at the DECLARATION that names the type — which is what makes\\n+\\\"a generic identity wrapper launders the value\\\" unwritable rather than caught.\\n+\\n+WHY THE FENCE IS SOUND AND A HANDLE WOULD NOT BE. The extent of a borrowing\\n+struct's borrow is the window, and the window is a STATEMENT. Refused (user,\\n+Sep 20) was the alternative — a handle that carries the borrow past the call\\n+that made it, design 201's `TaskCaptureBorrow` extent generalized to any value.\\n+That is a lifetime in disguise: design 201 gets away with it because a task\\n+handle is fenced on every side, and a general iterator handle would owe that\\n+fence at every position a value can go.\\n+\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+\\n+from ast_nodes import LendsExpr, MethodCall, SawType, SelfExpr, TypeKind\\n+from errors import ErrorKind\\n+from windows import RECEIVER_ROOT, SHARED, WindowTable\\n+\\n+#: The name of the hidden binding a window's RESOURCE lives in — exception e2,\\n+#: never nameable in source (the lexer reserves the `__saw_`/`__` prefix for the\\n+#: compiler). GENERIC by B1: the `for` client puts an iterator in it, a future\\n+#: accessor client would put a lend result, and nothing downstream reads the\\n+#: name to learn which.\\n+WINDOW_RESOURCE = \\\"__iter\\\"\\n+\\n+\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+# THE FENCE's position matrix — one row per refusal, one message each\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+#\\n+# Each row is `(key, what the author wrote)`. The sentence the rejector renders\\n+# is the same for all of them — that is the point of one rejector — and the row\\n+# supplies the phrase that names the position, so a reader is told WHICH of\\n+# their positions the rule caught rather than being handed the rule alone.\\n+#\\n+# The four NARROW EXCEPTIONS are stated here so they license no user storage:\\n+#   e1  construction and `return` inside the producing `borrows` function — its\\n+#       ONLY legal return;\\n+#   e2  the compiler's hidden window binding — a frame field in a driven body,\\n+#       an alloca in a sync one, never nameable;\\n+#   e3  the receiver position `&self` / `&var self` of the struct's OWN\\n+#       extension methods (`next`);\\n+#   e4  INSIDE those methods, the reference field read as a non-escaping\\n+#       RE-BORROW (design 106's forwarding rules).\\n+\\n+#: THE POSITIONS THE REJECTOR NAMES. Three, and that is deliberate: the fence\\n+#: asks its question at the ONE place every expression's type is stamped, where\\n+#: the parent slot is not in hand, so the sentence that matters is the RULE and\\n+#: not the slot. The two named positions are the ones a CLIENT knows and that\\n+#: diagnose a different ACT — a temporary receiver wants \\\"bind the collection\\n+#: first\\\", and a head that is an expression wants \\\"write the call itself\\\".\\n+#:\\n+#: THE MATRIX IS THE TESTS, not this table. Every other position an author can\\n+#: write — a `let`/`var` initializer, an argument, a `return`, a struct, enum,\\n+#: tuple or collection-literal element, a closure capture or return, a spawn\\n+#: argument or capture, a `move` operand, an assignment RHS, a `?.`/`??`\\n+#: operand, a discarded statement — is a ROW of\\n+#: `examples/errors/borrowing_struct_value_positions.saw`, and each of them\\n+#: reaches this one message, which is what \\\"one rejector\\\" is for. The\\n+#: IMPOSSIBLE SPELLINGS (a parameter, a field, a plain return, a type argument,\\n+#: an existential, an `Item`) are refused at the DECLARATION instead — see\\n+#: `reject_borrowing_type`.\\n+POSITIONS = {\\n+    'temporary':  \\\"produced from a TEMPORARY receiver\\\",\\n+    'head-expr':  \\\"produced by an `if` / `match` / block / `try` expression in \\\"\\n+                  \\\"the head\\\",\\n+    'other':      \\\"used outside a window head\\\",\\n+}\\n+\\n+#: The one sentence every refusal states, so the rule reads the same wherever\\n+#: it is met.\\n+RULE = (\\\"a borrowing struct holds a LENT PLACE, so its values live only inside \\\"\\n+        \\\"their window — the extent is the `for` statement, which is what makes \\\"\\n+        \\\"the borrow trackable without a lifetime\\\")\\n+\\n+#: The way out, likewise once.\\n+FIX = (\\\"write the call as the head of the `for` that consumes it \\\"\\n+       \\\"(`for x in v.iter() { … }`); to hold a value past the loop, copy what \\\"\\n+       \\\"you need out of it inside the body\\\")\\n+\\n+#: A WINDOW's introducer, per CLIENT. The diagnostics name \\\"the window\\\"\\n+#: generically and take this phrase from the client, so the generic\\n+#: scoped-borrow statement the follow-up specifies adds a row here rather than\\n+#: forking every message.\\n+_CLIENT_WORDING = {\\n+    'for': \\\"the `for` statement that iterates it\\\",\\n+    'generic': \\\"the statement that opened it\\\",\\n+}\\n+\\n+_CLIENT_FIX = {\\n+    'for': (\\\"a `for` over a borrowed iterator holds the collection for the \\\"\\n+            \\\"whole loop, exactly as a `with_ref` window does — collect what \\\"\\n+            \\\"you need first, or index the collection with a `while` loop if \\\"\\n+            \\\"you must mutate it while walking it\\\"),\\n+    'generic': (\\\"the window lasts for the whole statement that opened it; \\\"\\n+                \\\"move the access before it or after it\\\"),\\n+}\\n+\\n+\\n+class _WindowHeadMark:\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"`with checker._window_head(node):` — the node is a legal window head.\\n+\\n+    Exactly one node, and only while it is being checked. The set is keyed by\\n+    `id`, so nothing about the node's SHAPE grants the permission: an\\n+    expression that merely looks like a head (a `v.iter()` written as an\\n+    argument, say) is a different object and is refused.\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+\\n+    __slots__ = ('checker', 'node', 'saved')\\n+\\n+    def __init__(self, checker, node):\\n+        self.checker = checker\\n+        self.node = node\\n+\\n+    def __enter__(self):\\n+        self.saved = getattr(self.checker, '_window_head_ids', frozenset())\\n+        self.checker._window_head_ids = self.saved | {id(self.node)}\\n+        return self.node\\n+\\n+    def __exit__(self, *exc):\\n+        self.checker._window_head_ids = self.saved\\n+        return False\\n+\\n+\\n+class BorrowingMixin:\\n+    # ==================================================================== #\\n+    # Recognizing the type\\n+    # ==================================================================== #\\n+\\n+    def is_borrowing_struct(self, t) -> bool:\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"Does `t` NAME a borrowing struct?\\n+\\n+        Keyed on the TYPE IDENTITY (design 144: defining module + name), never\\n+        on a std visibility — a user `borrows struct` in another module is\\n+        admitted and refused by exactly these rules.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        if t is None or t.kind != TypeKind.STRUCT or not t.struct_name:\\n+            return False\\n+        info = self.get_struct_info(t.struct_name)\\n+        return bool(info is not None and getattr(info, 'is_borrowing', False))\\n+\\n+    def _first_borrowing_struct_in(self, t, depth: int = 0):\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"The first borrowing struct reachable from `t`, or None.\\n+\\n+        Pre-order, and it recurses through every composition a type has —\\n+        `Optional<It>`, `Result<It, E>`, `Vector<It>`, `Box<It>`, a tuple, an\\n+        array — because \\\"may not instantiate ANY type parameter\\\" is exactly the\\n+        statement that no composition of it is nameable. Stops at a nested\\n+        FUNCTION type's parameters for the same reason the no-escape walk does:\\n+        it would be a closure's parameter, which is a position no value reaches\\n+        without passing one of the rows above first.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        if t is None or depth > 8:\\n+            return None\\n+        if self.is_borrowing_struct(t):\\n+            return t\\n+        if t.kind == TypeKind.FUNCTION:\\n+            return None\\n+        parts = []\\n+        if t.kind in (TypeKind.OPTIONAL, TypeKind.REFERENCE,\\n+                      TypeKind.EXISTENTIAL):\\n+            parts.append(t.inner_type)\\n+        if t.kind == TypeKind.ARRAY:\\n+            parts.append(t.array_element_type)\\n+        parts.extend(t.element_types or [])\\n+        parts.extend(t.type_args or [])\\n+        for part in parts:\\n+            hit = self._first_borrowing_struct_in(part, depth + 1)\\n+            if hit is not None:\\n+                return hit\\n+        return None\\n+\\n+    # ==================================================================== #\\n+    # THE ONE REJECTOR — value positions\\n+    # ==================================================================== #\\n+\\n+    def check_borrowing_fence(self, expr, result) -> None:\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"THE FENCE, at the ONE chokepoint every expression's type passes\\n+        through (`_check_expression`'s stamp of `resolved_type`).\\n+\\n+        A borrowing struct's value is legal in exactly one position — the\\n+        DIRECT head of a `for` whose receiver is a place rooted in a named\\n+        binding — plus the four narrow exceptions named in the module\\n+        docstring. Asking the question HERE rather than at each construct is\\n+        what makes the position list a matrix of TESTS rather than a matrix of\\n+        checks: there is no `let`-arm, no argument-arm and no capture-arm to\\n+        forget, because every one of them stamps a type through this line.\\n+\\n+        The exceptions, in the order they are cheapest to answer:\\n+          e1  inside the PRODUCING function — its construction and its return;\\n+          e3  inside the borrowing struct's OWN extension methods, where `self`\\n+              has that type;\\n+          e2  the compiler's hidden window binding — not seen here at all,\\n+              because the fence runs on the AUTHORED tree only (the E8\\n+              exemption in `typechecker/core.py`);\\n+          e4  the reference FIELD, which never has this type (it has the\\n+              referent's, auto-dereferenced at the member access).\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        if getattr(self, 'exempt_statement_window', False):\\n+            return\\n+        if not self.is_borrowing_struct(result):\\n+            return\\n+        if id(expr) in getattr(self, '_window_head_ids', ()):\\n+            return\\n+        method = getattr(self, 'current_method', None)\\n+        if method is not None:\\n+            # e1: the producer's own construction and tail.\\n+            ret = getattr(method, 'return_type', None)\\n+            if (self.is_borrowing_struct(ret)\\n+                    and ret.struct_name == result.struct_name):\\n+                return\\n+            # e3: the type's own extension methods.\\n+            recv = self._self_type_for_current_method()\\n+            if (recv is not None and recv.kind == TypeKind.STRUCT\\n+                    and recv.struct_name == result.struct_name):\\n+                return\\n+        self.reject_borrowing_value(result, 'other', expr)\\n+\\n+    def reject_borrowing_value(self, t, position, node, extra: str = \\\"\\\") -> None:\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"A borrowing-struct VALUE appeared somewhere that is not a window head.\\n+\\n+        ONE message, one rule sentence, one fixit, anchored at the position the\\n+        author wrote. `position` is a key of `POSITIONS`; adding a position\\n+        means adding a row there, never a second message here.\\n+\\n+        The two positions the CALLER can name are the head's own\\n+        (`temporary`, `head-expr`), because the `for` adapter is the only place\\n+        that knows a head was written and what was wrong with it. Everywhere\\n+        else the position is `other`: the fence asks its question at the\\n+        expression-type chokepoint, where the parent is not in hand and the\\n+        sentence that matters is the RULE, not the slot.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        where = POSITIONS.get(position, POSITIONS['other'])\\n+        name = t.struct_name if t is not None else \\\"a borrowing struct\\\"\\n+        self._error(\\n+            ErrorKind.TYPE_MISMATCH,\\n+            f\\\"`{name}` is a `borrows struct` and may not be {where}: {RULE}\\\"\\n+            f\\\"{extra}\\\",\\n+            getattr(node, 'line', 0), getattr(node, 'column', 0),\\n+            hint=FIX)\\n+\\n+    def reject_borrowing_type(self, t, what, line, column) -> None:\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"A DECLARATION named a borrowing struct in a position that stores or\\n+        hands one back.\\n+\\n+        The IMPOSSIBLE SPELLINGS half of the fence. A helper forwarding the\\n+        result and a generic identity wrapper are not value-position rows at\\n+        all — a borrowing struct cannot be a plain function's parameter or\\n+        return type, nor a type argument — so the refusal lands at the HELPER's\\n+        declaration, which is where the author can act on it.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        found = self._first_borrowing_struct_in(t)\\n+        if found is None:\\n+            return\\n+        names_it = (\\\"is a `borrows struct`\\\" if found is t\\n+                    else f\\\"names a `borrows struct` (`{found.struct_name}`)\\\")\\n+        self._error(\\n+            ErrorKind.TYPE_MISMATCH,\\n+            f\\\"{what} may not name a borrowing struct: `{t}` {names_it}, and \\\"\\n+            f\\\"{RULE}. A type argument, an existential, a field, a parameter \\\"\\n+            f\\\"and a plain return each outlive the window, so none of them can \\\"\\n+            f\\\"name one — which is what makes a generic identity wrapper \\\"\\n+            f\\\"unwritable rather than caught\\\",\\n+            line, column,\\n+            hint=\\\"the one declaration that may name it is the `borrows` \\\"\\n+                 \\\"producer that RETURNS it (`func iter(&self) borrows -> \\\"\\n+                 \\\"VectorIterator<T>`) and the type's own extension methods\\\")\\n+\\n+    def validate_no_borrowing_struct_escape(self, program) -> None:\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"THE TYPE-POSITION half of the fence, over one program.\\n+\\n+        Runs beside `_validate_no_ref_laundering_in_program` and over the SAME\\n+        matrix (`NO_ESCAPE_POSITIONS`) — a struct field, an enum payload, every\\n+        flavour of return, a static, an associated-type assignment, a\\n+        generic-parameter default — PLUS the one position that matrix\\n+        deliberately omits: a PARAMETER. A reference belongs in a parameter; a\\n+        borrowing struct does not, because a parameter outlives nothing but it\\n+        can be stored by the callee and the window's extent ends at a statement\\n+        the callee cannot see.\\n+\\n+        Two exemptions, and both are the producer's:\\n+          * the PRODUCER's own return type — `func iter(&self) borrows -> It`\\n+            is the one declaration that may name it;\\n+          * the borrowing struct's OWN extension methods' receiver, which is\\n+            not in this matrix at all (a receiver is not a declared parameter\\n+            position here).\\n+\\n+        The ASSOCIATED-TYPE row is what carries the ITEM-LIFETIME rule: a\\n+        borrowing struct's `Iterator.Item` may be neither a reference (the\\n+        no-escape walk's answer) nor a borrowing struct (this one's), so the\\n+        loop variable OWNS what it is handed.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        if getattr(self, 'exempt_statement_window', False):\\n+            # E8: the post-transform program is the compiler's. The window's\\n+            # resource IS a frame field there (exception e2 — a `Slot<It>` a\\n+            # driven body holds inline, never nameable in source), which is the\\n+            # ONE storage position the rule exists to describe rather than to\\n+            # forbid. The authored program met this walk one pass earlier.\\n+            return\\n+        for t, what, line, column in self._borrowing_type_positions(program):\\n+            self.reject_borrowing_type(t, what, line, column)\\n+\\n+    def _borrowing_type_positions(self, program):\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"`(type, what, line, column)` for every declared position a borrowing\\n+        struct may NOT name — the no-escape matrix plus PARAMETERS, minus the\\n+        producer's own return.\\n+\\n+        Written out rather than derived from `_no_escape_positions` because the\\n+        two rules differ in exactly two rows and a filter over a shared\\n+        generator would hide both: a PARAMETER is legal for a reference and not\\n+        for a borrowing struct, and a `borrows` RETURN is legal for a borrowing\\n+        struct and (in the `-> &T` spelling) for a reference.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        def params_of(decl, label):\\n+            for p in (getattr(decl, 'parameters', None) or []):\\n+                if getattr(p, 'name', None) == 'self':\\n+                    continue\\n+                yield (getattr(p, 'type', None),\\n+                       f\\\"parameter `{p.name}` of {label}\\\",\\n+                       getattr(decl, 'line', 0), getattr(decl, 'column', 0))\\n+\\n+        def returns_of(decl, label):\\n+            if getattr(decl, 'is_borrows', False):\\n+                return          # the producer's one legal return\\n+            yield (getattr(decl, 'return_type', None),\\n+                   f\\\"the return type of {label}\\\",\\n+                   getattr(decl, 'line', 0), getattr(decl, 'column', 0))\\n+\\n+        for struct in getattr(program, 'structs', []) or []:\\n+            for f in struct.fields:\\n+                yield (f.type, f\\\"field `{f.name}` of `{struct.name}`\\\",\\n+                       getattr(f, 'line', struct.line),\\n+                       getattr(f, 'column', struct.column))\\n+        for enum in getattr(program, 'enums', []) or []:\\n+            for variant in enum.variants:\\n+                for payload in (variant.associated_types or []):\\n+                    name, pt = ((payload[0], payload[1])\\n+                                if isinstance(payload, tuple)\\n+                                else (variant.name, payload))\\n+                    yield (pt, f\\\"payload `{name}` of case `{variant.name}` in \\\"\\n+                               f\\\"enum `{enum.name}`\\\", enum.line, enum.column)\\n+        for func in getattr(program, 'functions', []) or []:\\n+            yield from returns_of(func, f\\\"`{func.name}`\\\")\\n+            yield from params_of(func, f\\\"`{func.name}`\\\")\\n+        for ext in getattr(program, 'extensions', []) or []:\\n+            for m in (getattr(ext, 'methods', None) or []):\\n+                yield from returns_of(m, f\\\"`{m.name}`\\\")\\n+                yield from params_of(m, f\\\"`{m.name}`\\\")\\n+            for assign in (getattr(ext, 'type_assignments', None) or []):\\n+                yield (getattr(assign, 'assigned_type', None),\\n+                       f\\\"associated type `{assign.name}` of \\\"\\n+                       f\\\"`{getattr(ext, 'struct_name', '?')}`\\\",\\n+                       getattr(assign, 'line', ext.line),\\n+                       getattr(assign, 'column', ext.column))\\n+        for trait in getattr(program, 'traits', []) or []:\\n+            for tm in (getattr(trait, 'methods', None) or []):\\n+                yield from returns_of(tm, f\\\"`{trait.name}.{tm.name}`\\\")\\n+                yield from params_of(tm, f\\\"`{trait.name}.{tm.name}`\\\")\\n+        for block in getattr(program, 'extern_blocks', []) or []:\\n+            for fn in (getattr(block, 'functions', None) or []):\\n+                yield from returns_of(fn, f\\\"extern `{fn.name}`\\\")\\n+                yield from params_of(fn, f\\\"extern `{fn.name}`\\\")\\n+        for static in getattr(program, 'statics', []) or []:\\n+            yield (getattr(static, 'type', None), f\\\"static `{static.name}`\\\",\\n+                   getattr(static, 'line', 0), getattr(static, 'column', 0))\\n+        for decl in self._declarations_with_type_params(program):\\n+            for tp in (getattr(decl, 'type_params', None) or []):\\n+                if getattr(tp, 'is_const', False):\\n+                    continue\\n+                yield (getattr(tp, 'default', None),\\n+                       f\\\"the default of type parameter `{tp.name}`\\\",\\n+                       getattr(tp, 'line', 0) or getattr(decl, 'line', 0),\\n+                       getattr(tp, 'column', 0) or getattr(decl, 'column', 0))\\n+\\n+    # ==================================================================== #\\n+    # (4) `lends self` — the origin proof\\n+    # ==================================================================== #\\n+\\n+    def _check_lends_expr(self, expr: LendsExpr):\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"`lends self` in the initializer of a borrowing struct's reference\\n+        field. Returns `&Self`, or None after reporting.\\n+\\n+        THE ORIGIN IS A CHECKED COMPILER FACT (codex c41 point 1). A `borrows`\\n+        signature says a borrow exists; the call site must know WHICH root to\\n+        charge. U3 admits ONE origin — the RECEIVER — so this refuses every\\n+        other spelling by name rather than approximating a root walk.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        method = getattr(self, 'current_method', None)\\n+        producer = (method is not None\\n+                    and getattr(method, 'is_borrows', False)\\n+                    and self.is_borrowing_struct(\\n+                        getattr(method, 'return_type', None)))\\n+        if not producer:\\n+            self._error(\\n+                ErrorKind.TYPE_MISMATCH,\\n+                \\\"`lends self` is the ORIGIN PROOF of a borrowing struct's \\\"\\n+                \\\"construction and belongs only in a `borrows` function that \\\"\\n+                \\\"RETURNS one\\\",\\n+                expr.line, expr.column,\\n+                hint=\\\"declare the producer `func name(&self) borrows -> \\\"\\n+                     \\\"TheBorrowingStruct` and initialize its reference field \\\"\\n+                     \\\"with `lends self`\\\")\\n+            return None\\n+        if not isinstance(expr.place, SelfExpr):\\n+            self._error(\\n+                ErrorKind.TYPE_MISMATCH,\\n+                \\\"U3 admits the receiver as the only borrow origin, so `lends \\\"\\n+                \\\"self` is the only spelling: a projection of the receiver \\\"\\n+                \\\"(`lends self.buffer`), another reference parameter, or a \\\"\\n+                \\\"local names a root the CALL SITE cannot attribute, and the \\\"\\n+                \\\"window charges the receiver place's root\\\",\\n+                expr.line, expr.column,\\n+                hint=\\\"lend the ROOT, not the buffer — read through the \\\"\\n+                     \\\"reference field on every access instead of snapshotting \\\"\\n+                     \\\"what it points at\\\")\\n+            return None\\n+        if not getattr(method, 'self_is_reference', False):\\n+            self._error(\\n+                ErrorKind.TYPE_MISMATCH,\\n+                \\\"`lends self` requires a REFERENCE receiver: a by-value \\\"\\n+                \\\"receiver's `self` is the callee's own local, not the caller's \\\"\\n+                \\\"persistent storage, so there is no live borrowed origin to \\\"\\n+                \\\"hand to the window\\\",\\n+                expr.line, expr.column,\\n+                hint=\\\"declare the producer `func name(&self) borrows -> …`\\\")\\n+            return None\\n+        if getattr(method, 'self_mutable', False):\\n+            self._error(\\n+                ErrorKind.TYPE_MISMATCH,\\n+                \\\"`lends self` requires a SHARED receiver (`&self`): U3 is a \\\"\\n+                \\\"shared-window feature, and an exclusive lend would feed a \\\"\\n+                \\\"`&var` field a borrowing struct may not hold\\\",\\n+                expr.line, expr.column,\\n+                hint=\\\"declare the producer `func name(&self) borrows -> …`; \\\"\\n+                     \\\"mutating the produced value's OWN state stays ordinary \\\"\\n+                     \\\"`&var self` on ITS methods\\\")\\n+            return None\\n+        self_type = self._self_type_for_current_method()\\n+        expr.referent_type = self_type\\n+        return SawType(TypeKind.REFERENCE, inner_type=self_type,\\n+                       reference_mutable=False)\\n+\\n+    def _self_type_for_current_method(self):\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"`Self` as the body sees it — the receiver binding's own type.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        info = self.current_scope.lookup('self')\\n+        t = getattr(info, 'type', None) if info is not None else None\\n+        if t is not None and t.kind == TypeKind.REFERENCE:\\n+            return t.inner_type\\n+        return t\\n+\\n+    def check_borrowing_producer(self, method) -> None:\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"The declaration-side rules for a `borrows -> S` producer, and the\\n+        recording of its ORIGIN.\\n+\\n+        Run after the body is checked, so `lends self` has reported its own\\n+        refusals first and this adds only what the body as a whole owes: a\\n+        reference receiver, and EVERY reference field of the produced struct\\n+        initialized with `lends self` on EVERY returning path.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        if getattr(self, 'exempt_statement_window', False):\\n+            # E8 again: `Slot<It>.take` in a post-transform frame is a\\n+            # monomorphization of the compiler's OWN storage wrapper over the\\n+            # window's resource, and its `-> T` is not a producer's return.\\n+            return\\n+        if getattr(method, 'place_type', None) is not None:\\n+            # A LOWERED design-141 accessor, not a producer. The lowering\\n+            # rewrote its signature into the window-closure form, whose result\\n+            # is the caller's `__R` — and `__R` monomorphizes to whatever the\\n+            # use site's expression produced, which in a post-transform driven\\n+            # body is sometimes a borrowing struct itself (`for x in\\n+            # self.items.value().iter()`, where the frame's `Slot` lends the\\n+            # vector and the iterator is the window's value). `place_type` is\\n+            # the lowering's own record that this declaration LENDS, and it is\\n+            # what separates the two shapes after the fact exactly as the\\n+            # presence of a `lend` separated them before it.\\n+            return\\n+        ret = getattr(method, 'return_type', None)\\n+        if not self.is_borrowing_struct(ret):\\n+            return\\n+        if not getattr(method, 'is_borrows', False):\\n+            self._error(\\n+                ErrorKind.TYPE_MISMATCH,\\n+                f\\\"`{method.name}` returns the borrowing struct \\\"\\n+                f\\\"`{ret.struct_name}`, so its signature must say so: write \\\"\\n+                f\\\"`borrows -> {ret}` in the effect slot\\\",\\n+                method.line, method.column,\\n+                hint=\\\"a reader sees at the DECLARATION that the result \\\"\\n+                     \\\"borrows the receiver, which is the whole of why the \\\"\\n+                     \\\"word is there\\\")\\n+            return\\n+        if not getattr(method, 'self_is_reference', False):\\n+            self._error(\\n+                ErrorKind.TYPE_MISMATCH,\\n+                f\\\"a `borrows -> {ret}` producer needs a REFERENCE receiver \\\"\\n+                f\\\"(`&self`): a by-value receiver's `self` is the callee's own \\\"\\n+                f\\\"local, so there is no live borrowed origin for the window to \\\"\\n+                f\\\"charge\\\",\\n+                method.line, method.column,\\n+                hint=\\\"declare it `func {}(&self) borrows -> {}`\\\".format(\\n+                    method.name, ret))\\n+            return\\n+        if getattr(method, 'self_mutable', False):\\n+            self._error(\\n+                ErrorKind.TYPE_MISMATCH,\\n+                f\\\"a `borrows -> {ret}` producer needs a SHARED receiver \\\"\\n+                f\\\"(`&self`): U3 admits shared reference fields only, and an \\\"\\n+                f\\\"exclusive receiver would be the origin of one this type may \\\"\\n+                f\\\"not hold\\\",\\n+                method.line, method.column,\\n+                hint=\\\"declare it `func {}(&self) borrows -> {}`\\\".format(\\n+                    method.name, ret))\\n+            return\\n+        missing = self._producer_paths_missing_origin(method, ret)\\n+        if missing is not None:\\n+            self._error(\\n+                ErrorKind.TYPE_MISMATCH,\\n+                f\\\"U3 admits the receiver as the only borrow origin: every \\\"\\n+                f\\\"returning path of `{method.name}` must construct \\\"\\n+                f\\\"`{ret.struct_name}` with `lends self` in every reference \\\"\\n+                f\\\"field{missing}\\\",\\n+                method.line, method.column,\\n+                hint=\\\"a path that constructs the struct any other way, lends a \\\"\\n+                     \\\"non-receiver place, a projection of `self`, another \\\"\\n+                     \\\"reference parameter or a local has no root the call site \\\"\\n+                     \\\"can charge\\\")\\n+            return\\n+        # THE ORIGIN SUMMARY, recorded on the declaration (design 126's AST\\n+        # contract). The call site reads it and substitutes it onto the\\n+        # RECEIVER PLACE's root through design 141's root attribution.\\n+        method.borrow_origin = RECEIVER_ROOT\\n+\\n+    def _producer_paths_missing_origin(self, method, ret):\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"None when every returning path is a well-formed origin proof, else a\\n+        sentence naming what went wrong.\\n+\\n+        A producer's body is deliberately TINY — the four-line shape — so this\\n+        asks the structural question directly rather than running a dataflow:\\n+        every construction of the produced struct in the body initializes each\\n+        of its reference fields with `lends self`, and the body's result is one\\n+        of those constructions.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        info = self.get_struct_info(ret.struct_name)\\n+        if info is None:\\n+            return None\\n+        ref_fields = [n for n in info.field_order\\n+                      if (info.fields.get(n) is not None\\n+                          and info.fields[n].kind == TypeKind.REFERENCE)]\\n+        if not ref_fields:\\n+            # A `borrows struct` with no reference field is design 130's rule-7\\n+            # teaching error, reported at the TYPE. Nothing to prove here.\\n+            return None\\n+        inits = list(self._struct_inits_of(method.body, ret.struct_name))\\n+        if not inits:\\n+            return (f\\\" — no construction 193 u5\\n     )\\n \\n+    def _borrowing_field_referent(self, struct, field, ftype, line, column):\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"A `borrows struct` field's type as the no-escape walk should see it.\\n+\\n+        Returns what the walk runs on: the REFERENT for the one exempt shape (a\\n+        top-level SHARED reference, aliases resolved), and the field's own type\\n+        otherwise — so every position but the exempt one keeps the ordinary\\n+        refusal, with the ordinary message. Reports the MODE error itself,\\n+        because `&var` is not a position question: it is the shared-window rule\\n+        (U3's part 0), and a message about parameters-only would teach the\\n+        wrong thing.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        resolved = ftype\\n+        for _ in range(_MAX_ALIAS_DEPTH):\\n+            if resolved is None or resolved.kind != TypeKind.STRUCT:\\n+                break\\n+            aliased = self._alias_target(resolved)\\n+            if aliased is None:\\n+                break\\n+            resolved = aliased\\n+        if resolved is None or resolved.kind != TypeKind.REFERENCE:\\n+            return ftype\\n+        if resolved.reference_mutable:\\n+            value = (resolved.inner_type if resolved.inner_type is not None\\n+                     else \\\"T\\\")\\n+            self._error(\\n+                ErrorKind.TYPE_MISMATCH,\\n+                f\\\"field `{field.name}` of `borrows struct {struct.name}` may \\\"\\n+                f\\\"not be an EXCLUSIVE reference: `{ftype}` is `&var`, and a \\\"\\n+                f\\\"borrowing struct holds SHARED references only. A window is \\\"\\n+                f\\\"shared, and an exclusive field would let this type \\\"\\n+                f\\\"reallocate the very storage another reader is walking \\\"\\n+                f\\\"through its own field — which recording the root alone \\\"\\n+                f\\\"would not catch\\\",\\n+                line, column,\\n+                hint=f\\\"lend it shared (`{field.name}: &{value}`); mutating \\\"\\n+                     f\\\"this type's OWN state stays ordinary `&var self` on \\\"\\n+                     f\\\"its methods\\\")\\n+            return None\\n+        return resolved.inner_type\\n+\\n     def _no_escape_positions(self, program):\\n         \\\"\\\"\\\"Yield `(type, what, line, column)` for every position in\\n         `NO_ESCAPE_POSITIONS`, in that order.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n         for struct in getattr(program, 'structs', []):\\n+            borrowing = getattr(struct, 'is_borrowing', False)\\n             for field in struct.fields:\\n-                yield (field.type, f\\\"field `{field.name}` of `{struct.name}`\\\",\\n+                ftype = field.type\\n+                if borrowing:\\n+                    # design 275 U3, and SL-333 R5's FIELD clause: a reference\\n+                    # type is legal as a struct field iff the struct is\\n+                    # `borrows struct`. The parser applied the exception to the\\n+                    # WRITTEN form; this is the same exception once aliases are\\n+                    # resolved, so `type Ref = &Vector<Int>` is neither a way\\n+                    # past the rule nor a way past the exception. Only the TOP\\n+                    # LEVEL is exempt, and only SHARED — the walk then runs on\\n+                    # what the reference points at, so `&(Int, &T)` is still\\n+                    # refused on the inner one.\\n+                    ftype = self._borrowing_field_referent(\\n+                        struct, field, ftype,\\n+                        getattr(field, 'line', struct.line),\\n+                        getattr(field, 'column', struct.column))\\n+                yield (ftype, f\\\"field `{field.name}` of `{struct.name}`\\\",\\n                        getattr(field, 'line', struct.line),\\n                        getattr(field, 'column', struct.column))\\n         for enum in getattr(program, 'enums', []):\\n@@ -5931,6 +5987,17 @@ class TypeUtilsMixin:\\n                         b, 'capture' if id(e) in capture_entries else 'access',\\n                         ln, col, root=path[0])\\n \\n+        # design 275 U3, access site 3: the same cross-check against the open\\n+        # STATEMENT WINDOWS. This is where `v.push(x)` inside a `for x in\\n+        # v.iter()` body arrives (a `&var self` receiver is a `mut` entry), and\\n+        # with it every other spelling that reaches a call — a `&var` argument,\\n+        # an `o.take()` receiver, a closure's borrow captures, and a design-201\\n+        # spawn argument STARTED inside the body.\\n+        for kind, path, e, ln, col in entries:\\n+            self.check_window_access(\\n+                path, kind != 'imm',\\n+                'capture' if id(e) in capture_entries else 'access', ln, col)\\n+\\n         n = len(entries)\\n         for i in range(n):\\n             ki, pi, ei, li, ci = entries[i]\\ndiff --git a/sawc/windows.py b/sawc/windows.py\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..77cadc4c\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/sawc/windows.py\\n@@ -0,0 +1,306 @@\\n+\\\"\\\"\\\"THE STATEMENT-SCOPED WINDOW (design 275 U3).\\n+\\n+A WINDOW is a borrow of a ROOT that lasts for the extent of ONE STATEMENT. It\\n+is not a for-loop feature: `for` is its FIRST CLIENT, and the reuse obligation\\n+(user, Sep 20) is that the generic scoped-borrow statement the follow-up brief\\n+specifies adds its syntax, its typechecking client and its acquisition/result\\n+binding — and NO second implementation of root accounting, pinning, suspension\\n+persistence or exit cleanup.\\n+\\n+WHY A RECORD AND NOT A RULE ON `ForLoop`. Three passes need the same four\\n+facts, and each of them would otherwise re-derive them from the loop's shape:\\n+\\n+  * the TYPECHECKER charges the root, refuses the writers, and accounts for\\n+    the close on every route;\\n+  * the COROUTINE TRANSFORM asks \\\"is a window live across this suspension\\\" and,\\n+    when it is, mints the frame field, encodes the referent as a design-88\\n+    pointer, and closes the window on every exit route out of the state\\n+    machine;\\n+  * CODEGEN lowers the sync form — the resource's slot, and the DROP-BEFORE-\\n+    CHARGE-END order.\\n+\\n+None of those three reads `ForLoop`. They read the RECORD, which is what makes\\n+the second client a new `open_window` call rather than a second copy of all\\n+three. (`tools/test_window_seam.py`, the `windowseam` battery lane, fails when\\n+a `ForLoop` mention appears in this module or in the transform's window\\n+handling outside the named adapter.)\\n+\\n+THE FOUR BOUNDARIES (codex c44), which this module's API shape encodes:\\n+\\n+  B1  ITERATION belongs to the client; WINDOW LIFETIME belongs here. The record\\n+      holds a GENERIC resource slot (`resource_name`, `resource_type`), never a\\n+      field named `__iter` nor an `Iterator`-shaped payload — the `for` client\\n+      puts an iterator in it, a future accessor client would put a lend result.\\n+      Not every `ForLoop` opens one: a RANGE loop and an OWNED-iterator loop\\n+      open none and take the ordinary split.\\n+  B2  A window closes by CONTROL-FLOW TARGET, not by the spelling of the exit.\\n+      `windows_left_by` takes a RESOLVED target and answers which windows an\\n+      edge leaves, inside-out. An implementation that special-cases `Continue`\\n+      as \\\"keep open\\\" leaks the first client's shape; this one asks whether the\\n+      target is inside or outside each extent.\\n+  B3  The record has a LIFECYCLE — ACQUIRE, ACTIVE, RELEASE — and RELEASE runs\\n+      a CLIENT-PROVIDED cleanup operation. For `for` that is \\\"destroy the\\n+      borrowing iterator\\\"; an accessor window's would be an epilogue, and an\\n+      optional head may take an `else` branch without ever activating a window,\\n+      so nothing here assumes release is a drop or that every evaluated head\\n+      opens a window.\\n+  B4  The direct-head / temporary-receiver refusals apply to BORROWING results\\n+      only. A window exists because a value CARRIES a borrow; a head that\\n+      produces an owned value opens none.\\n+\\n+THE MODES. `shared` is the only one U3 exercises; `exclusive` is reserved\\n+rather than invented, because the derivation (writer-versus-reader, nested\\n+windows) is the follow-up's to specify. The field is here so the follow-up\\n+adds rows rather than a second record.\\n+\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+\\n+from typing import Any, List, Optional, Tuple\\n+\\n+#: The window modes. `shared` composes with other readers; `exclusive` excludes\\n+#: everything. U3 opens shared windows only — see the module docstring.\\n+SHARED = 'shared'\\n+EXCLUSIVE = 'exclusive'\\n+MODES = (SHARED, EXCLUSIVE)\\n+\\n+#: The ORIGINS a window's borrow may have. U3 admits one, and the string is the\\n+#: summary recorded on the producing declaration (`Method.borrow_origin`), so\\n+#: the call site substitutes it onto the receiver place's root rather than\\n+#: walking the body.\\n+RECEIVER_ROOT = 'receiver root'\\n+ORIGINS = (RECEIVER_ROOT,)\\n+\\n+\\n+class StatementWindow:\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"ONE window: a root borrowed for the extent of one statement.\\n+\\n+    DELIBERATELY NOT A DATACLASS, and this is load-bearing rather than style.\\n+    The record is STAMPED ON THE STATEMENT (`ForLoop.window`), and\\n+    `typechecker/effects.py:substitute_ast_types` — monomorphization's type\\n+    rewrite — walks `dataclasses.fields()` rather than `structural_fields()`\\n+    and recurses into every dataclass VALUE it finds. A dataclass record whose\\n+    `extent` points back at the statement holding it is therefore a cycle, and\\n+    the walk goes down it until the interpreter's recursion limit. A plain\\n+    class is opaque to that walk, which is exactly the right answer: a window\\n+    record carries no `SawType` for monomorphization to substitute.\\n+\\n+    `extent` is the CHECKER's field. After the checker, `mono_copy` may clone\\n+    the statement and the clone's record still names the ORIGINAL node — which\\n+    costs nothing, because from the transform onward what is read is the root,\\n+    the mode and the resource, never the identity of the extent.\\n+\\n+    `root_path` is design 141's root attribution applied to the head's RECEIVER\\n+    place — `v.iter()` charges `('v', ())`, `st.patches.iter()` charges\\n+    `('st', ('patches',))`, an enclosing-owner path per design 8/10. It is what\\n+    every conflict question compares, and it is a PATH rather than a name\\n+    because a field path names a smaller thing than its owner and the Law\\n+    already knows how to overlap the two.\\n+\\n+    `extent` is the STATEMENT node the window lives for. Lexical, so every\\n+    route out leaves it — which is the whole of \\\"no route may leave the charge\\n+    live past the statement\\\".\\n+\\n+    `resource_*` is B1's generic slot: the value that CARRIES the borrow, which\\n+    the client initialized into persistent storage at ACQUIRE and which\\n+    RELEASE destroys before the charge ends.\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    __slots__ = ('root_path', 'root_id', 'mode', 'origin', 'extent', 'client',\\n+                 'line', 'column', 'resource_name', 'resource_type', 'state',\\n+                 'reported')\\n+\\n+    def __init__(self, root_path, root_id, mode, origin, extent, client,\\n+                 line, column):\\n+        self.root_path: Tuple[str, Tuple[str, ...]] = root_path\\n+        self.root_id: Optional[int] = root_id\\n+        self.mode: str = mode\\n+        self.origin: str = origin\\n+        self.extent: Any = extent       # the statement node that IS the extent\\n+        self.client: str = client       # 'for' — supplies diagnostic wording\\n+        self.line: int = line\\n+        self.column: int = column\\n+        # B1's generic resource slot — the borrowing value, its type, and the\\n+        # name of the hidden binding it lives in (never nameable in source).\\n+        self.resource_name: Optional[str] = None\\n+        self.resource_type: Optional[Any] = None\\n+        # B3: ACQUIRE -> ACTIVE -> RELEASE. A window is only `active` once the\\n+        # resource is initialized into its slot; RELEASE never runs on an\\n+        # uninitialized resource, which is what an optional head's `else` arm\\n+        # needs and what a failing argument (A3) relies on.\\n+        self.state: str = 'acquiring'\\n+        # Lines already reported against, so one statement that reaches the\\n+        # root several times yields one diagnostic (the `_task_borrows`\\n+        # precedent).\\n+        self.reported: set = set()\\n+\\n+    @property\\n+    def root_name(self) -> str:\\n+        return self.root_path[0]\\n+\\n+    def renders_root(self) -> str:\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"The root as an author would read it back.\\n+\\n+        A path's projections are the Law of Exclusivity's own\\n+        `('field', name)` / `('tuple', i)` / `('index', k)` triples\\n+        (`_build_access_path`), so rendering is spelling them back rather than\\n+        joining strings.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        head, hops = self.root_path\\n+        out = head\\n+        for hop in hops or ():\\n+            if not isinstance(hop, tuple) or len(hop) != 2:\\n+                out += \\\".?\\\"\\n+            elif hop[0] == 'field':\\n+                out += f\\\".{hop[1]}\\\"\\n+            elif hop[0] == 'tuple':\\n+                out += f\\\".{hop[1]}\\\"\\n+            else:\\n+                out += \\\"[…]\\\"\\n+        return out\\n+\\n+\\n+def paths_overlap(a: Tuple[str, Tuple[str, ...]],\\n+                  b: Tuple[str, Tuple[str, ...]]) -> bool:\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"Do two access paths name storage that can be the same?\\n+\\n+    The same test the Law of Exclusivity runs on a call's access set, spelled\\n+    here because a window compares paths OUTSIDE any call: same root, and one\\n+    hop sequence a prefix of the other. `v` overlaps `v.items`; `p.a` and `p.b`\\n+    do not.\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    if a[0] != b[0]:\\n+        return False\\n+    ha, hb = a[1], b[1]\\n+    n = min(len(ha), len(hb))\\n+    return ha[:n] == hb[:n]\\n+\\n+\\n+class WindowTable:\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"THE ONE CHOKEPOINT every statement window is opened and closed through.\\n+\\n+    CLIENTS (obligation 1 — a funnel names its entries). Each client calls\\n+    `open_window` with its own root, mode, origin and extent, supplies its own\\n+    RELEASE operation, and reads back nothing of its own shape:\\n+\\n+      * `for` — `typechecker/borrowing.py:open_for_window` /\\n+        `close_for_window`, called from `typechecker/statements.py`'s two\\n+        `for` checkers. It supplies the head (a `borrows` producer call on a\\n+        place) and the body, and its release operation destroys the borrowing\\n+        iterator. THE ONLY CLIENT IN U3.\\n+\\n+      * (reserved) the generic scoped-borrow STATEMENT the follow-up brief\\n+        specifies — a keyword-introduced block binding a lent place, with an\\n+        `else` arm for a `borrows -> &T?` head. It would call `open_window`\\n+        with mode from its own spelling, an `else` arm that never reaches\\n+        `activate`, and a release operation that runs the accessor epilogue.\\n+\\n+    WHAT THE CHOKEPOINT DOES, so no client does it twice:\\n+      * the ROOT CHARGE (`open_window` -> `conflict_for`, read at the four\\n+        access sites the Law already has);\\n+      * the EXCLUSIVITY conflicts, including a `&var` extent live at the head\\n+        or STARTED inside the body;\\n+      * the MOVE / REASSIGN / TAKE / SWAP refusals on the root and on every\\n+        enclosing owner (K20's precedent, one message);\\n+      * the CLOSE-ON-EVERY-ROUTE accounting, by RESOLVED control-flow target\\n+        (`windows_left_by`).\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+\\n+    def __init__(self):\\n+        #: The open windows, OUTERMOST FIRST. LIFO: the innermost window is the\\n+        #: last entry, which is what makes `windows_left_by` answer inside-out\\n+        #: by walking the list backwards.\\n+        self.open: List[StatementWindow] = []\\n+        #: Every window this table ever opened, in open order — the transform\\n+        #: and codegen read records off their extent nodes, but the ledger is\\n+        #: what a dump and a gate lane enumerate.\\n+        self.all: List[StatementWindow] = []\\n+\\n+    # -- ACQUIRE / ACTIVE / RELEASE (B3) ---------------------------------\\n+\\n+    def open_window(self, root_path, root_id, mode, origin, extent, client,\\n+                    line, column) -> StatementWindow:\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"Open ONE window and make it the innermost. B3's ACQUIRE step A5.\\n+\\n+        The caller has already run A1-A4 — resolved the receiver PLACE, opened\\n+        the head's own shared borrow over the WHOLE call expression (arguments\\n+        included), evaluated the arguments left to right, and run the producer.\\n+        This is the HAND-OFF with no gap: the same root, the same mode, one\\n+        continuous extent.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        assert mode in MODES, mode\\n+        assert origin in ORIGINS, origin\\n+        w = StatementWindow(root_path=tuple(root_path), root_id=root_id,\\n+                            mode=mode, origin=origin, extent=extent,\\n+                            client=client, line=line, column=column)\\n+        self.open.append(w)\\n+        self.all.append(w)\\n+        return w\\n+\\n+    def activate(self, w: StatementWindow, resource_name, resource_type):\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"The resource is initialized into its persistent slot: ACTIVE.\\n+\\n+        Separated from `open_window` because RELEASE must never run on an\\n+        uninitialized resource — an optional head that takes its `else` arm,\\n+        and a head whose argument failed (A3), both leave a window that was\\n+        never activated and owes no cleanup.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        w.resource_name = resource_name\\n+        w.resource_type = resource_type\\n+        w.state = 'active'\\n+        return w\\n+\\n+    def close_window(self, w: StatementWindow):\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"RELEASE. Exactly once, and only on a window that is open.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        if w in self.open:\\n+            self.open.remove(w)\\n+        w.state = 'released'\\n+        return w\\n+\\n+    # -- B2: closing by CONTROL-FLOW TARGET ------------------------------\\n+\\n+    def windows_left_by(self, target_extents) -> List[StatementWindow]:\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"The open windows an edge LEAVES, innermost first.\\n+\\n+        `target_extents` is the set of extent nodes the edge's RESOLVED target\\n+        is still inside — everything the edge does NOT leave. A window whose\\n+        extent is in that set stays open; every other open window closes, and\\n+        they close inside-out.\\n+\\n+        This is B2 stated as one question rather than as a case per statement\\n+        kind. `continue` targeting THIS loop passes the loop's own extent, so\\n+        the window stays; `break` targeting it does not, so it closes;\\n+        `break`/`continue` targeting an INNER loop passes every outer extent,\\n+        so nothing of the outer closes; a window nested inside an outer loop\\n+        closes when control continues that outer loop, because the outer loop's\\n+        extent is in the set and the inner one is not; and `return`, an error\\n+        propagation and a cancellation pass the EMPTY set, so every crossed\\n+        scope closes.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        keep = {id(e) for e in (target_extents or ())}\\n+        return [w for w in reversed(self.open) if id(w.extent) not in keep]\\n+\\n+    # -- the root charge, read by the Law's existing access sites ---------\\n+\\n+    def conflict_for(self, root_path, writes: bool,\\n+                     started_inside: bool = False) -> Optional[StatementWindow]:\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"The innermost open window an access to `root_path` collides with.\\n+\\n+        THE TABLE, which is the Law of Exclusivity read over a statement-length\\n+        window rather than a call-length one:\\n+\\n+          * a SHARED window composes with other readers and collides with a\\n+            WRITE, a MOVE, a REASSIGN, a TAKE or a SWAP of the root or of any\\n+            enclosing owner on its path;\\n+          * an EXCLUSIVE window (reserved) collides with every touch.\\n+\\n+        `started_inside` names an access that OPENS a new by-reference extent\\n+        inside the body (a `&var` argument, a design-189 task capture): those\\n+        conflict on the same table, and the flag exists only so the diagnostic\\n+        can say which of the two the author wrote.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        for w in reversed(self.open):\\n+            if w.state == 'released':\\n+                continue\\n+            if not paths_overlap(w.root_path, tuple(root_path)):\\n+                continue\\n+            if w.mode == EXCLUSIVE or writes:\\n+                return w\\n+        return None\\ndiff --git
